## 10. Roadmap

### Phase 0: Ground truth — DONE (2026-10-14)
- EU4 install: `D:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Europa Universalis IV` (1.37.5).
- Reference nation: **DAN** (base game, westerngfx). Full chain recorded in `base_models.json` by `library/scanner.py`, with pytest coverage (synthetic fixtures, plus a regeneration check against the real install when `EU4_PATH` is set).
- The user **owns the Scandinavian unit pack (dlc116)**. In-game, `dlc116_DAN_sprite_pack` (order 100, `primary_culture = danish`) and `version = 4` entities replace DAN's base-game tiers 1, 3 and 4. Tier 2 isn't in that pack, so it stays base game. Don't use DAN or Danish-culture countries as a generated test nation, except on purpose as a known pack-priority/version case.
- Corrections to the original spec, now written into the sections above:
  1. pdxmeshes live in `interface/assets/*.gfx` → `objectTypes` (§2).
  2. Animation indirection: state → id → type → `.anim` (§2, §5).
  3. States are `idle walk attack retreat`; no `death` (§5).
  4. Muskets on `Left_hand_node`, melee on `Right_hand_node` (§5).
  5. Riders use a pdxmesh for the tier's infantry `.mesh` and need the `horse_*` ids; crew needs `artillery_attack` (§5; western only, see §5 for other cultures).
  6. Vanilla entities have no `version`; DLC uses `version = 4` / `-1` (§3).
  7. `graphicalculturetype.txt` is a bare name list (§3).
  8. gfx-culture defaults are wired by a `graphical_cultures` sprite pack (§3, §7.4).
  9. Pack-order entries are optional (default 0); the generator still writes one for tag packs (§3).
  10. Normal maps are DXT1; diffuse DXT1; spec DXT5 (§4).
  11. Some nation meshes are PdxMeshStandard; recolour bases default to the generic meshes (§4).
  12. Sprite tiers confirmed at MIL 0/12/21/26 (§2).
- Moved out of Phase 0: bone **parenting** (hierarchy) is deferred to Phase 3. The Blender import of one infantry, horse+rider and artillery mesh moves with it; bone names, locators, shaders, texture names and DDS headers are already done.

### Phase 1: Scaffolder + validator (no meshes) — DONE (2026-10-14)
Done (2026-10-14): parser/writer, naming, templates, generator, validator, export dry-run/manifest, CLI, all in-game tests below, and the nation wizard (`python -m eu4unitgen.gui`: Settings, Unit set, Validate, Generate, Test mod; it only calls `export/pipeline.py`). MAC/hellenicgfx validates clean against 1.37.5 + DLC116.
- **Mod checks are off by default** (`Project.check_mod_collisions`, CLI `--check-mod`) until the Hellenistic Revolution folder exists. Off means the mod folder is not read for collisions or shared-file copies. Export ownership checks always run.
- **Test mod.** `cli test-mod` writes `<EU4 mod dir>/eu4unitgen_test.mod` + `eu4unitgen_test/` (descriptor keys copied from the user's own `.mod` files: `version tags name supported_version path`). It contains the package plus the tag's vanilla country file with INTEGRATION.md applied. It reads no other mod.
- **Test tag.** `cli pick-test-tag` checks every tag that owns land on 1444.11.11 against every vanilla and DLC sprite-pack trigger (tag, was_tag, primary_culture, culture, culture_group, colonial flags, has_reform at the start date, AND/OR/NOT; anything else counts as "might match") and against vanilla/DLC `TAG_*` unit entities. On 1.37.5 exactly one tag passes: **HDA (Haida)**, 1 province, north_american tech, northamericagfx. DLC unit packs cover every other 1444 culture. Test set: `macedonian_test` on HDA, graphical culture overridden to hellenicgfx.
- **Windows Controlled Folder Access** blocks python.exe from writing under Documents on the user's machine. The writer probes before writing; `--stage-dir` builds the mod elsewhere for copying in Explorer. The user copies by hand; don't ask to whitelist Python.
- **Drop-in package.** `cli generate` writes `output/<set>/`: only files the tool creates (entities, packs, order, units, localisation) plus `INTEGRATION.md` at its root. Patched copies of game files (`mil.txt`, `graphicalculturetype.txt`) are not in the package; they exist only in memory so the validator checks the merged result. The manifest is `output/.eu4unitgen/<set>.manifest.json`, beside the package. `generate` never writes to a mod folder: it refuses an output path inside the EU4 mod directory, under a folder with `descriptor.mod`, or under a folder with a sibling `<name>.mod`. `--mod` is only read, and only with `--check-mod`.
- **Test-only sprite levels.** `test-mod --test-sprite-techs 2 3 4` moves `sprite_level` 2–4 to those MIL techs in the test mod's `mil.txt` only, unlocks each tier's units there and all tier-1 units at MIL 1. HDA's tech group (north_american) starts at MIL 1, so tier 1 shows at start and tiers 2–4 are one MIL tech apart. The validator requires sprite levels 1–4 exactly once, rising with the tech.
- Clausewitz parser/writer with round-trip tests on vanilla files; replace the stopgap reader in `scanner.py`.
- `naming.py`, Jinja2 templates, nation wizard (GUI).
- Generate: 12 TAG entities (full definitions, no clones); gfx-culture default set; **TAG sprite pack and gfx-culture sprite pack (`graphical_cultures` block)**; pack-order entry for the TAG pack; `graphicalculturetype.txt` (vanilla list + new names, at export); units; mil.txt enables; localisation; country snippet.
- Validator: dependency graph, missing nodes, version collisions (vanilla + DLC + mod), orphan entities, missing loc keys, state animation ids, pack-order shadowing.
- Export with dry-run diff + manifest.
- **In-game test 1 (2026-10-14, HDA / `macedonian_test` / hellenicgfx, 1.37.5 + DLC116, test sprite techs 2 3 4):**
  - **Infantry tiers 1–4: pass.** The TAG pack's `HDA_INFANTRY_n` shows at each tier.
  - **The map model follows the country's `sprite_level` (from MIL tech), not the unit selected for the regiment.** So the sprite-pack tier index comes from tech, and a tier's unit files don't pick its model.
  - **Cavalry and artillery: fail.** error.log had `Couldn't find parent clone entity westerngfx_CAVALRY_1_rider / westerngfx_CAVALRY_n / westerngfx_ARTILLERY_1` for every clone in both `hr_macedonian_test_units.asset` and `hr_hellenicgfx_units.asset`. The parents exist with exactly those names in `western_units.asset`, which loads after `hr_*` (§3). Both fell back to HDA's native northamericagfx models. Fix: full entity definitions plus the `clone_order` rule.
- **In-game test 2 (2026-10-14, same setup, full entity definitions): pass.** Cavalry and artillery show the western models at multiple tiers. error.log has no clone, entity or `hr_` errors. What's left in it isn't ours: Workshop `.mod` descriptor parse warnings (`ugc_*.mod`), vanilla GUI warnings (`unitpanel_card.gui`, `countrygovernmentview.gui`, `macrobuild_mapicons.gui`) and a vanilla `economic_ideas` scope error. Cavalry and artillery at all 4 tiers were confirmed in test 3.
  - The EU4 launcher rewrites `<mod dir>/<name>.mod` and drops its final newline. The exporter treats that one change as unedited for launcher descriptors only.
- **In-game test 3 (2026-10-14): `test-mod --suite`.** Two variant mods, `eu4unitgen_test_v1` and `_v2`, enable one per launch. Both hold the same files except two order entries. The CLI prints the pass/fail checklist.
  - **Pack priority on HDA:** pack A `hr_macedonian_test_units` (western copies) against rival pack B `hr_priority_test_units` (`tag = HDA`, lists vanilla `asiangfx_*`). v1: A 110, B 111. v2: A 111, B 110. HDA tiers 1–4 are checked in whichever launch shows A.
  - **Fallback tags** come from pick-test-tag's coverage check, run on facts after the patch. Each gets HDA's culture (`haida`) through a patched copy of its `history/countries` file (the `primary_culture` lines in effect at 1444.11.11; nothing else changes), so no culture-based vanilla or DLC pack can match it, whichever DLC is owned. On 1.37.5 the picker chooses (largest by provinces within the preferred look):
    - (a) **BTS** (africangfx): no pack, no `BTS_*`. Expect africangfx.
    - (b) **BAL** (indiangfx): `BAL_*` entities (western copies, `hr_bal_fallback_units.asset`), no pack. western = the engine still looks up `TAG_*` names; indian = it doesn't.
    - (c) **QTO** (southamericagfx), `graphical_culture = hellenicgfx`: only the hellenicgfx gfx pack. In the suite the hellenicgfx defaults copy **muslimgfx** (`--gfx-set-base`), so a pass can't be confused with a westerngfx fallback.
  - The `test_fixtures` validator rule re-reads every suite file: patched cultures, no pack can match a fallback tag, only case b has `TAG_*` entities, case c's country file, the rival trigger and the one-step order gap.
  - **Results (2026-10-14): all pass.** v1: HDA asiangfx (rival, higher order); BTS africangfx; BAL indiangfx; QTO muslimgfx (the hellenicgfx defaults). v2: HDA western; tiers 1–4 pass for infantry, cavalry and artillery. error.log after both launches: no clone, entity or `hr_` lines, and nothing about the fallback tags. The only lines were Workshop `ugc_*.mod` descriptor parse warnings, the news banner (`steam_depot_id`, JSON) and vanilla GUI warnings. Findings are in §3 (pack order, fallback order, graphical culture list).
- **Done when:**
  - ~~a nation generated by the app shows correct models in all 4 tiers in-game with a clean error.log;~~ done (tests 1–3);
  - ~~**in-game test of fallback order and pack priority** is done and §3's UNVERIFIED items are resolved:~~ done (test 3). §3 items still open (version default/ties, clone attach merging, case-sensitive load order, gfx list replace vs merge) don't affect generated output and stay marked;
    - Fallback: (a) a tag with no sprite pack and no TAG entities; (b) a tag with `TAG_*` entities but no pack; (c) a tag whose gfx culture has only a gfx pack. Record which models show.
    - Priority: two packs for the same tag with different entities at orders N and N+1, then swapped. Record which wins. DLC116 + DAN is a second, free check (order 100, `version = 4`).

### Phase 2: Texture pipeline — DONE (2026-10-14)
Scope: DDS read/write, spec-mask painter (R/G/B = country colour regions, A = gloss presets), diffuse pattern overlays, reference-palette recolour. Retextured units use a new pdxmesh on the **vanilla `.mesh` with a `meshsettings` texture override** (confirmed on units, in-game test 4). The Blender copy-and-re-point job is no longer needed for retextures.

Decisions (user, 2026-10-14):
- Derived textures from **base-game** files are allowed in generated mod output; never in the repo or app, never DLC-derived (CLAUDE.md rule 2).
- `meshsettings` first; Blender mesh copy only as fallback.
- v1 masks are external PNGs (painted in GIMP/Krita over an exported guide), plus a **reference-palette mode**: load a reference image, extract its dominant colours, and recolour the existing spec-masked regions of the diffuse with them (no hand-painting).

Facts found while planning:
- Pillow 12.3 reads vanilla DXT1/DXT5 maps but writes one mip level only; vanilla maps have full chains (10 at 512²). The writer builds the chain itself.
- texconv is not installed on the user's machine: Pillow is the default encoder, texconv used only if on PATH.

Order (each step with tests and its validator rule; **stop and replan if step 3 shows `meshsettings` fails on units**):
1. **Done.** `textures/dds.py`: read (Pillow), write the base's format/size with a full mip chain (each level encoded by Pillow, header written by us with vanilla's flags `0xa1007`, caps `0x401008`), no DX10 header, not sRGB. Mip levels are downsampled per channel so spec areas with alpha 0 keep their colour. Re-encoding a vanilla 512² diffuse/spec gives the same byte size and header (except the reserved NVTT tag), mean error ~0.3/255. Rule `dds_format`: every generated DDS records its base (`GeneratedFile.derived_from`), the base is a base-game file (not DLC), and the header matches it.
2. **Done.** `textures/recolour.hue_shift`. `core/retexture.py`: `retextured_mesh` (new pdxmesh on the vanilla `.mesh`, all animation ids, scale and extra keys restated, one `meshsettings` block for the mesh's single shape) and `apply_retextures` (points a tier's infantry + rider, horse, and an own `TAG_ARTILLERY_n_cannon` entity at them; the entity template now restates state events such as the cannon's muzzle flash). Rule `mesh_settings`: base-game mesh, shape name at that index, known shader (warning if not the mesh's own), shader file exists, all three textures resolve, every generated DDS is named by a pdxmesh. The graph adds meshsettings texture edges (bare name = mesh folder, a name with `/` = game path; both UNVERIFIED).
3. **In-game test 4 (2026-10-14, HDA / `macedonian_test`, `eu4unitgen_test_textures`, test sprite techs 2 3 4):**
   - **Variant A (tier 1; bare names, DDS in the mod at `gfx/models/Units/`): PASS.** Infantry, horse and cannon showed the green hue shift: meshsettings overrides unit textures. HDA's country colours still showed (spec untouched).
   - Tier-1 rider: visually inconclusive; confirmed from the files that `HDA_CAVALRY_1_rider` uses `hr_macedonian_test_infantry_1_mesh` (and `HDA_CAVALRY_1` attaches it on `Saddle_Node`).
   - **Variant B (tier 2; full paths into `gfx/models/units/hr_macedonian_test/`): FAIL, all black.** error.log: the engine prefixes the mesh's folder to the names (§4). Variant B is dropped: names are bare, DDS beside the mesh.
   - Tiers 3–4 (control): normal.
   - **The whole-texture hue shift recoloured skin too.** Recolouring must be region-limited (below).
4. **Done.** **Region-limited recolouring** (`textures/regions.py`, `textures/recolour.py`). Regions are soft masks at the base texture's size:
   - `country1/2/3`: the spec map's R/G/B (PdxMeshColor bases only).
   - `metal`: spec alpha (gloss) ≥ 14 and low saturation, outside country regions (matches helmets, armour, buckles on the western bases).
   - `skin`: auto-detected (HSV: skin hue, saturation 35–190/255, value ≥ 80, outside country/metal; median, grow, shrink). A heuristic: it catches faces, necks and arms, with a few light-leather false hits.
   - `cloth`: everything else (leather, cloth, the parts no country colour reaches).
   - user PNG masks (`mask:<file>`), painted externally over an exported guide.
   - **Protect mask**, applied to every diffuse operation: `skin` on by default, `metal` optional; a base-provided protect PNG (per base, in the project) replaces the auto mask.
   - Operations, applied in order: `recolour` (region → colour, keeps each pixel's lightness detail), `hue_shift`, `pattern` (tiled PNG; normal/multiply/overlay; opacity), `spec_paint` (PNG mask → set/clear a country channel), `gloss` (mask or region → preset: matte 3%, cloth 8%, metal 13% of 255 alpha).
   - **Reference palette**: extract the reference image's dominant colours (median cut), assign them to the target regions by size (largest region ↔ most dominant colour) and recolour those regions. Default targets are the spec-masked regions; because the shader multiplies the diffuse there by the country colour, palette mode by default **clears that spec channel in the region** (fixed colours). Targeting `cloth` instead keeps country colours.
   - Guide export: base diffuse PNG + a colour-coded region map + the spec map, to paint masks over (`<output>/texture_guides/<set>/`; derived from base-game files, so user output only).
   - Tuning on the real western bases (2026-10-14): the skin detector catches faces, necks and arms with a few light-leather hits; on horses the same hues down to value 20 are the whole hide, so a cloth recolour can't leave the coat patchy. Palette colours: fine median cut + k-means, then the most common colours at least 60 apart in RGB.
5. **Done.** **Texture recipes** (`core/recipe.py`, JSON): per slot `<tier>/<role>` (`infantry` incl. rider, `horse`, `cannon`): base (default: the base culture's generic pdxmesh for that tier; base game only), protect settings, operations. Paths are relative to the recipe file.
6. **Done.** **Generator + validator**: `generate(..., recipe=)` writes the retextured pdxmeshes (`interface/assets/<prefix><set>.gfx`), derived DDS (diffuse, and spec when changed) and points the TAG set's entities at them (gfx-culture defaults stay vanilla-based). Rules: `dds_format`, `mesh_settings`, `texture_paths`.
7. **Done.** **GUI Textures screen** (between Unit set and Validate; `gui/textures_page.py`, form = `core.recipe.SimpleSlot`, previews and guides through `Session.texture_preview` / `export_texture_guide`): slot picker, protect toggles and mask, palette reference, recolour, pattern, spec masks, gloss; guide export; 2D preview that applies the shader's country-colour formula with the set's colour; load/save recipe.
**In-game test 5 (2026-10-14, HDA / `macedonian_test`, recipe `build/recipe_test/recipe.json`, test sprite techs 2 3 4):**
   - T1 (palette crimson/gold on primary/secondary, fixed; cloth recolour; horse palette; cannon hue): **PASS.** Palette colours shown, skin and helmets protected, horse coat natural.
   - T2 (palette crimson, `fixed: false`, primary only): **unreadable.** From the files the diffuse did change (primary region grey 98 → crimson 139/35/42; everything else unchanged), but the game multiplies it by HDA's red unit colour, so it still looked red. **Retest (blue/green palette on primary + secondary, `fixed: false`): PASS** — purple coat with green trim on the infantry, the artillery crew and the cavalry rider, as the preview predicted; horse coat natural. Keep mode works: the game multiplies the recoloured diffuse by the unit colours.
   - T3 (metal gloss matte + metal recolour gold): as designed. The gold is the `metal` region (16% of the texture: helmet/hat and armour pieces; mean 47/46/50 → 167/140/61); the uniform stayed HDA's colour. Gloss from the files: metal alpha mean 22.9 → 8.3 (matte = 3% = 8); elsewhere 6.4 → 6.0 (re-encode noise).
   - T4 control: plain western. **Test 5 passes on all tiers.** error.log: nothing from the mod (Workshop descriptors, news banner, vanilla GUI/war/script lines only).
   - **Finding: units are tinted with `common/country_colors` `color1/2/3`, not the country file's map `color`.** HDA has no entry there and its units showed red; the file's example template is 205 32 26 (UNVERIFIED engine default). The Textures preview now uses the tag's `country_colors` (default template colours when missing, with a note); the Unit set colour is labelled as the map colour.
**Unit colours per tag** (after test 5): `Nation.unit_colours` (color1-3; GUI Unit set "Unit colours", CLI `--unit-colours`). INTEGRATION.md section 4 hands over the `common/country_colors/00_country_colors.txt` entry to merge (replace the tag's entry); the test mod applies it to a copy of vanilla's file (`patch_country_colours`, keeps the rest byte-for-byte and the file's line endings). Rule `unit_colours`: a note when the tag has no entry (vanilla, mod or generated) and the set gives none, so the engine default applies (on 1.37.5 both HDA and MAC have no entry); an error when INTEGRATION.md or a generated copy doesn't carry the set's colours. The Textures preview uses the set's unit colours first, then the tag's entry, then the template default.
**Multi-tag unit sets** (2026-10-14): one set shared by several tags. `Nation(tag=...)` takes one tag or a list (`Nation.tags`; `Nation.tag` is the first); several tags need a set name. The set gets **one sprite pack** whose trigger is `country_trigger = { OR = { tag = MAC tag = EPI } }` (the form vanilla's base packs use, e.g. `+01_eastern_base_sprites.txt`); one tag stays `tag = MAC`. The set's entities are named after the first tag (`MAC_INFANTRY_1`): in-game test 3 showed `TAG_*` names alone don't pick models, the pack does. INTEGRATION.md section 1 lists every tag's country file (same lines for each); section 4 gives a `country_colors` entry per tag that has unit colours (`Nation.unit_colours` = one triple for every tag, or `{tag: triple}`). Tech group: the tags' shared 1444 group, else it must be set (the pipeline refuses mixed groups). Rules: `sprite_packs` (the OR trigger), `pack_order` (other packs that may match any of the tags), `integration` (every tag's country file), `unit_colours` (per tag). The test mod patches every tag's country file; the test suite and the texture test take one tag. GUI: Tag(s) field, a tag picker for unit colours; CLI: `--tag MAC EPI --set argead`, `--tag-unit-colours TAG R G B R G B R G B`. Before this, several tags made one set per tag; that mode is gone.
**CLI on the pipeline**: `cli generate` and `cli test-mod` build `Settings` + `SetSpec` and call `export/pipeline.py` (one code path with the GUI); `build_test_mod(write=stage|apply|dry)`.
**Blender runner (step 8, done)**: `blender/runner.py` checks the pinned version (`blender --version`), runs `jobs/<job>.py` with `--background --python-exit-code 1` (no `--factory-startup`: io_pdx_mesh is a user extension), passes arguments and results as JSON, applies a timeout and keeps the log (`<output>/blender_logs/`). `jobs/check_addon.py`: on 4.2 the addon is the extension module `bl_ext.user_default.io_pdx_mesh`, version (0, 91, 0), enabled (verified 2026-10-14, 1.3 s). Settings screen: "Check Blender".
8. `blender/runner.py` (version check, timeout, log) **Done** (see above). + `check_addon.py`. The mesh copy job isn't needed for retextures (Phase 3 kitbash will use the runner).

**In-game test 6 (2026-10-14, HDA / `macedonian_test`, recipe `build/test6/recipe.json`, unit colours blue/yellow/black): PASS on all tiers, matching the preview.**
- Unit colours: blue/yellow everywhere: the test mod's `country_colors` entry (appended to a copy of vanilla's file) recolours HDA's units.
- T1 pattern overlay: stripes on the cloth. T2 `spec_paint`: cloth turned blue (primary channel). T3 protect PNG + `mask:` region: the left half green, face included (the PNG replaced the auto skin protection). T4 gloss: shiny cloth (80%), matte armour (0%).
- error.log: clean (reported by the user).

**Known limits after Phase 2** (deliberate; revisit when needed):
- Retexturing handles **one-shape meshes** only; others are refused with an error. Every western/generic base is one shape.
- Recipes retexture the **TAG set**; the gfx-culture default set (`<gfx>_INFANTRY_n`) stays on vanilla meshes.
- **texconv** isn't wired in. Pillow's output matches vanilla's size and header (mip chain written by us); the stack lists texconv as optional.
- The **engine default unit colours** for a tag with no `country_colors` entry are UNVERIFIED (test 5 showed red; the file's template is 205 32 26). Only `00_country_colors.txt` is handed over/patched; whether the engine also loads other files in `common/country_colors` is UNVERIFIED, so entries are merged into that one file.
- The **skin detector** is a colour heuristic (some light-leather hits); a protect PNG per base overrides it. The **preview** is flat (no lighting), so gloss can't be judged there.
- **Shared sets**: entities are named after the first tag, so a later single-tag set for that same tag would collide (`version_collisions` reports it); the map colour, if set, goes to every tag; the test suite and the texture test take one tag.
- The desktop shortcut is **Windows only** (`scripts/make_shortcut.ps1`); on Linux run `python -m eu4unitgen.gui` (no `.desktop` file yet). Under `pythonw.exe` errors go to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\eu4unitgen\gui.log` and a message box.

### Phase 3: Asset generation (Blender mesh jobs) — IN PROGRESS
Decisions (user, 2026-10-14):
- ~~Derived meshes allowed as a fallback if attaching rigid parts to the `head` bone fails.~~ **Dropped (2026-10-15):** in-game test 7 showed the head attach works, so no derived (skinned-in) meshes are planned. Every part is a rigid static mesh made from scratch and attached to a bone.
- AI image-to-3D is a later, pluggable backend. Local model: **TripoSG only** (MIT, >= 8 GB VRAM; the user's RTX 3060 has 12 GB), in its own isolated environment (never the app's .venv). **No Hunyuan3D option**: its license excludes the EU, UK and South Korea, including use of outputs there. Hosted fallback: Tripo API, opt-in per request; details at that step. TRELLIS.2 (24 GB, Linux-only) is out.
- Hoplon face in country colour (PdxMeshColor + spec mask) by default, optional painted blazon.

Facts found while planning (1.37.5): the western, eastern, indian and african generic infantry share one 28-bone skeleton (incl. `head`, `Right_hand_node`, `Left_hand_node`, `attach_back`). Base game spear+shield infantry: `indiangfx_INFANTRY_1` has `attach = { Right_hand_node = "muslim_spear" Left_hand_node = "indian_generic_01_type1_shield" }` and its pdxmesh maps idle/walk/attack to `spear_shield_*` (base-game animations; `polearm_onehanded_*` exist too). Vanilla weapons and shields are static meshes (no bones, no locators, PdxMeshStandard, scale 0.5). Vanilla attaches use plain bone names too (`root`, `L_hand`, `R_hand`, `attach_back`), but never `head`; **attaching to `head` works (in-game test 7)**.

Order (each step with tests and its validator rule):
1. **Done.** Export round-trip (`blender/jobs/roundtrip.py`, shared helpers `jobs/_pdx.py`): io_pdx_mesh 0.91's `import_meshfile`/`export_meshfile` (module `bl_ext.user_default.io_pdx_mesh.pdx_blender.blender_import_export`) read vanilla `muslim_spear.mesh` (72 verts, PdxMeshStandard) and the western body (28 bones) straight from the install; a procedural box exports with vanilla's static-weapon layout (object > shape > mesh `p n ta u0 tri` + `aabb` + `material`, empty `locator`). Differences: the addon always writes a `boundingsphere` on each mesh, which none of the 458 binary vanilla meshes has (18 more are text `@@t@`), so `scanner.strip_mesh_props` removes it, using `write_pdx_mesh` (byte-identical on all 458). Exports need tris/quads only (the addon computes tangents): parts are triangulated. Material export rule: custom property `shader`, texture file names from image nodes on the BSDF's Base Color / Roughness / Normal.
2. **Done.** Skeleton facts: `summarize_mesh` returns each bone's parent and bind pose (inverse of `tx`; `scanner.bind_pose`; vanilla `attach_free` has an all-zero `tx` = no pose) and the mesh's bounding box; `base_models.json` skeletons carry `parents` and `bind` (from their first mesh), pdxmeshes carry `aabb`. Cross-checked against Blender: head joint (0, 14.4988, 0.0842) Y-up = Blender (0, 0.0842, 14.4988) Z-up. Western body: T-pose, height 16.65, hand nodes at y ~10 (x -6.1 right, +5.9 left), `Right_hand_node` frame = mesh axes with Y and Z flipped. Hand weapons run along +Z with the grip at the origin and the point/blade at +Z (halberd z -10.8..13.9, blade z 8.8..12.2; spear head z ~9.6), no bones, scale 0.5.
**Spike / in-game test 7 (2026-10-15, HDA / `macedonian_test`, `test-mod --parts`, mod `eu4unitgen_test_parts`, test sprite techs 2 3 4): PASS.** `core/parts.py` + `blender/jobs/build_parts.py` built a crude dory (wood shaft z -11.5..11, bronze leaf head to z 15, sauroter to -12.8; 42 tris) and a crude front-to-back crest (head-bone frame, y 0.98..3.1, z ±1.32; 84 tris), textures from scratch (`textures/procedural.py`: colour swatches, flat normal, gloss in spec alpha). Tier-1 infantry (so also the tier-1 gun crew): `attach = { Right_hand_node = "hr_macedonian_test_dory_1" head = "hr_macedonian_test_crest_1" }`. Rules `attach_bones` (attach nodes are bones/locators of the entity's mesh, case-insensitive) and `part_mesh` (parses, no boundingsphere, no skeleton, known shader, 1-3000 tris, non-degenerate box); `dds_format` accepts procedural textures in vanilla's form; `texture_paths` counts textures named by generated `.mesh` files.
   - **H1 head attach works:** the crest sits on the helmet. **H2** it follows the head marching and fighting. **H3** size and height good (base 1.9 above the head joint; the western tier-1 helmet top is 2.15).
   - **D1** the dory is in the right hand, point up. **D2** grip and orientation are right in the attack (spear levelled at the enemy): the vanilla hand-weapon convention (+Z = point, grip at the origin) holds for our meshes. **D3** length good.
   - The tier-1 gun crew shows the crest (the crew is the infantry entity). Tiers 2-4 plain western (control).
   - error.log: clean (only Workshop `.mod` descriptor noise).
   - Consequences: `head` is a verified attach node; the derived-mesh fallback is dropped (see Decisions). Static meshes exported by io_pdx_mesh (boundingsphere stripped) load and render.

Order from here (renumbered after the spike):
3. **Done (2026-10-15).** Procedural part library (`core/parts.py`, `blender/jobs/build_parts.py`, `textures/procedural.py`). Five kinds, each with one bone, the roles it may dress, numeric params and colour zones:
   - `dory` (infantry, rider, crew) and `sarissa` (infantry, crew): spear builder on `Right_hand_node`; dory = the spike's (z -12.8..15), sarissa z -14.5..30. `xiphos` (all roles): leaf blade from a grip at the origin. Hand weapons take `point`: up = +Z (halberd, spears), down = -Z (cavalry sabre convention); auto = down for riders, up otherwise.
   - `hoplon` (infantry, crew) on `Left_hand_node`: a closed domed shell in the hand's YZ plane (radius 3.8, dome 0.9 towards +X, rim 0.35), face zone planar-mapped onto a 256² "disc" texture (corners hold the rim and inside swatches). Face in a unit colour by default (PdxMeshColor, spec R; precedent: vanilla `south_american_generic_01_type1_shield` is a static PdxMeshColor shield); a fixed colour makes it PdxMeshStandard. Optional blazon PNG: painted over the face in fixed colours (the country channel is cleared under it), `blazon_rotate`/`blazon_mirror` if the game shows it turned.
   - `crest` (all roles) on `head`: the spike's fin (fore-aft or transverse, thickness 0.28 total, as tested).
   - Any zone can take a unit colour (`country1-3`); the part's shader follows (PdxMeshColor if any zone does). Diffuse under a country zone is grey 170.
   - Thumbnails and fit previews: `blender/jobs/render_parts.py` imports the tier's body (its vanilla .mesh written to a temp folder) and the parts, puts each part at its bone's bind pose and renders front, left side and a 3/4 thumbnail per part (Workbench, textures, body and country zones tinted with the unit colours). `Session.parts_preview`, CLI `parts-preview`, GUI "Preview slot". Saved to `<output>/part_previews/<set>/<tier>_<role>.png` (shows a base-game body: user output only).
   - Facts found: the unit faces PDX -Z (nose and toes point there in the side render); io_pdx_mesh flips V on import and export, so image coordinates map to Blender UVs as v -> 1 - v and what Blender shows is what the game gets; seen from outside the shield (+X) the viewer's right is PDX +Z. **Vanilla `western_generic_03_type1` and `_04_type1` have a `Right_hand_node` inverse bind scaled by ~1e12**, so the raw bind pose shrinks anything on it to a point; previews use its rotation only (`fitting.rigid`). The halberd shows in game, so the engine animates from the .anim, not the bind pose.
4. **Done.** Fitting (`core/fitting.py`, pure Python): from the body's .mesh, the stature (head joint height, 14.499 on every generic western body) and the head's shape (vertices skinned mostly to `head`, in that bone's frame). Lengths scale by stature / 14.499; not by the bounding box, which counts headgear (western tier 4's shako: 18.06 against 16.65). Crest: base = helmet top - 0.25, radius = 1.1 x the head's larger front/back reach, clamped 0.8-2.5; on western tier 1 that is base 1.90, radius 1.40: test 7's values, now measured instead of typed. Western head tops: tier 1 2.15, tier 2 2.88, tier 3 2.40, tier 4 3.56. Order: kind default < fitted < the user's value.
5. **Done.** Generator loadouts (`core/loadout.py`, `generate(..., loadout=, runner=, part_cache=)`): JSON slots `<tier>/<infantry|rider|crew>` with parts and a stance. A hand weapon replaces the base's whole hand loadout (**western tiers 2-4 carry their musket on `Left_hand_node`** with rifleman animations; a sarissa mustn't leave it behind). Stance `auto`: hoplon -> `spear_shield` (indiangfx's set; same 28-bone skeleton), dory/sarissa -> `polearm_twohanded` (the western halberd set the test-7 dory used), xiphos -> `polearm_onehanded`, nothing in hand -> base; or named. A stance edits the retextured pdxmesh in place or adds one on the vanilla .mesh with every other animation id restated. A crew slot gives the gun crew its own entity (a copy of the base infantry, the gunman repointed); its pdxmesh is the infantry's when they move alike, else `hr_<set>_crew_n_mesh`. Built meshes are cached per session (Blender runs only for changed parts). Rules: `loadout` (part entity/pdxmesh/.mesh agree, shader included; each part on its kind's bone only; hand weapons have their grip inside the mesh; a PdxMeshColor part's spec map has a mask; crew = its artillery's gunman; stance in the pdxmesh; warnings: a shield without `spear_shield`, a weapon part with the base's item still in the left hand), plus `part_mesh`, `attach_bones`, `naming` (part and crew names). CLI `--loadout`, `--blender`.
6. **Done.** GUI Parts screen (`gui/parts_page.py`, between Textures and Validate): tier, body (infantry/rider/crew; "dress the gun crew apart"), stance; one box per bone with the kind, its params ("auto" = default, fitted), zone colours (default / fixed / unit colour) and the hoplon's blazon; fit preview; load/save.
7. **In-game test 8 — PASS** (2026-10-15; HDA / `macedonian_test`, `build/test8/loadout.json` + `blazon_R.png`, unit colours blue 40 60 160 / yellow 220 190 40 / black 20 20 20, test sprite techs 2 3 4; staged in `build/test8/stage`, checklist `build/test8/checklist.txt`; validator 0 errors, 0 warnings). Every tier dressed differently:
   - T1 hoplite: dory + hoplon (blue face) + red crest, `spear_shield`; crew apart with a yellow crest and the halberd; rider xiphos (point down) + the shared crest.
   - T2 phalangite: sarissa + black transverse crest, `polearm_twohanded` (replaces the musket and rifleman set); crew = the infantry; rider dory (point down).
   - T3: dory + off-white hoplon with the "R" blazon, `spear_shield`; rider xiphos point up.
   - T4: xiphos + blue crest, `polearm_onehanded`; crew apart with a black hoplon only (`hr_macedonian_test_crew_4_mesh`, `spear_shield`); rider plain (vanilla sabre).
   - **UNVERIFIED, decided by test 8:** riders' weapon orientation (down vs up, T1/T2 vs T3); whether a blazon reads the right way round in game (Blender shows it correct); PdxMeshColor on our static shield; `spear_shield` and `polearm_onehanded` on western bodies; a separate crew entity with its own stance; the crest on tier 2-4 hats.
   - **Result (2026-10-15): PASS.** T1: dory + blue hoplon (takes the unit colour: PdxMeshColor works on our static mesh) + red crest perfect; crew apart with the yellow crest and the halberd as designed; rider xiphos "down" = blade levelled forward, natural. T2: sarissa reads correctly (very long, vertical); rider dory levelled forward, natural; the crest on the tier-2 hat body is hard to see. T3: the "R" blazon reads correctly in game (not mirrored or rotated); off-white hoplon correct; rider xiphos "up" = blade upright and back, looks great. T4: xiphos + blue crest on the shako works; crew with the black hoplon only as designed; rider vanilla sabre (control). error.log: clean (only Workshop `.mod` descriptor noise, the news banner and vanilla GUI warnings).
   - Resolved: both rider directions work (down = levelled forward, up = upright/back), so direction is a choice (below); the blazon mapping is right; `spear_shield`, `polearm_twohanded`, `polearm_onehanded` and a separate crew entity with its own stance all work on western bodies.
   - Requests after test 8 (user, 2026-10-15), **done**:
     1. **Body per tier** (`"bodies": {"<tier>": "<pdxmesh>"}` in the loadout; GUI "Body for this tier"). A tier's infantry, rider and crew wear a chosen base-game infantry body instead of the culture's own (17th-18th c. hat bodies don't suit Hellenistic units), e.g. `western_generic_01_type1` (armoured) for all four tiers. Applied before retexturing (a recipe retextures the chosen body) and fitting. `check_body`: base-game pdxmesh on a base-game .mesh with the infantry and rider animation ids (never DLC). `Session.body_candidates` lists the 101 base-game bodies on 1.37.5 with `head` and both hand nodes (one per .mesh, the generic name preferred), incl. `western_generic_01_type2`-`type5`. A chosen body without `artillery_attack` (every non-western one) gets it on a set pdxmesh, mapped to its own attack type as western bodies map it, so the gun crew animates. Rule `loadout`: the infantry wears the chosen body's .mesh; a body without PdxMeshColor is noted (its uniform takes no unit colours).
     2. **Weapon direction per loadout**: `"weapon_point": {"infantry"|"rider"|"crew": "up"|"down"}` is what a weapon's `auto` point means (default up for infantry and crew, down for riders); a part's own `point` still overrides it. GUI: "Weapon point when auto".
     3. **Xyston** (cavalry lance, any role): spear builder, z -18..22 with a 2.0 butt spike (40 units, ~4.2 m at 1.75 m per 16.65), head 3.0, shaft radius 0.16. Auto stance with it on infantry: `polearm_twohanded`.
     4. **Shield without a weapon**: rule `loadout` warns when an entity carries a hoplon and nothing in its right hand (western tiers 2-4 hold their musket in the left hand, which the shield replaces); the Parts screen shows the same hint for a slot with a shield and no weapon part (`loadout.slot_warnings`).
8. AI backend (TripoSG local, Tripo API opt-in). **Next.**

**Removed:** the spike's `test-mod --parts` / GUI "Parts spike" (`build_parts_test`); the normal test mod with `--loadout` replaces it.
- Headless import/export, kitbash of pre-weighted accessories, thumbnail rendering for GUI preview.
- Import one vanilla infantry, horse + rider and artillery mesh via io_pdx_mesh; record **bone parenting** into `base_models.json` (scanner or a Blender job).

### Vertical slice: Macedon in Victoria's Secret (2026-10-15)
The user's mod `mod/Victorias_Secret` (launcher name "Victoria's Secret"; a git working copy that tracks only 7 of its ~7,400 files; the launcher loads the Workshop copy `ugc_3449352309.mod` -> `D:/.../workshop/content/236850/3449352309`). Read only (`--check-mod`, `--base-mod`).
- **MAC** is a mod tag (vanilla has none): `common/countries/Macedonia.txt` (easterngfx, colour 235 200 0, eastern/Ottoman historical units), history `history/countries/MAC = Macedonia.txt` (the engine takes the tag from the name's first 3 letters): eastern tech group, primary culture greek, accepts bulgarian, orthodox, capital 148 Thessaloniki. **No land at 1444**: cores on Kastoria (1853), Siroz (4702) and Ohrid (4780). Has `MAC_ideas`. No `country_colors` entry. The mod has no unit graphics (no entities, packs, models); its `common/units` equals vanilla; it has its own `mil.txt` (33 techs, sprite levels at MIL 0/12/21/26 like vanilla), `graphicalculturetype.txt` (= vanilla), `country_colors`, `technology.txt`, `cultures`, `defines.lua`.
- **Design choice of the mod, not a bug:** the Byzantine culture group is folded into **latin** (greek, pontic_greek, cappadocian_greek, spartan, athenian, ...), part of its Roman Empire design. **Never trigger a Macedon-specific set on latin or greek cultures**: greek is the primary culture of BYZ, GRE, CEP, CRT, MOE and MAC. Macedonian sets trigger on `tag = MAC` (no `macedonian` culture exists).
- **Fix A (done):** with a mod read (`--check-mod`, or the base mod of a test mod), country tags, country files and country history come through the mod (`LayeredFiles`), including tags with no land at the start (`tag_facts(..., tags=)`): MAC's units get `unit_type = eastern` and INTEGRATION.md names `common/countries/Macedonia.txt`. Rule `integration` accepts a mod's country file.
- **Fix B (done):** `test-mod --base-mod <folder>` (GUI: Test mod -> Base mod) builds the test mod as a **copy of that mod with the set integrated**: INTEGRATION.md applied to copies of the mod's own files (country file, `mil.txt`, `graphicalculturetype.txt`, `country_colors`), the package added, every other file copied unchanged (role `base`, no diff), `.git` and its `descriptor.mod` left out. Folder `eu4unitgen_test_<base folder>` (`Naming.base_test_mod`), launcher name "<base mod name> - unitgen test". Enable it **instead of** the base mod. The base mod is only read.
- **Fix C (done, TEST ONLY):** `--give-cores`: the tag owns, controls and cores its cored provinces and its history capital at the start (`patch_province_owner`; rule `test_owners`), so a releasable tag is playable at once.
- **In-game test 9 — STAGED (2026-10-15):** `build/macedon/stage` = `eu4unitgen_test_victorias_secret.mod` + folder ("Victoria's Secret - unitgen test"), a git repo on `unitgen-test` (`main` baseline; commit "Macedonian unit set"; commit "TEST ONLY"). Set `macedonian` on `tag = MAC`, hellenicgfx, unit colours purple 120 30 140 / gold 210 170 40 / off-white 230 225 210; loadout `build/macedon/loadout.json`: bodies T1 `western_generic_01_type1`, T2 `_type3`, T3 `_type2`, T4 `_type4` (`_type5` has French fleurs-de-lis: avoid); T1 infantry dory + hoplon (primary-colour face, Vergina sun blazon drawn from scratch) + red crest, `spear_shield`; T2-4 sarissa, `polearm_twohanded`, crests white / black transverse / secondary colour; Companions: xyston + the tier's crest; gun crews apart: xiphos + crest (`polearm_onehanded`). TEST ONLY: MAC owns Kastoria, Siroz, Ohrid and Thessaloniki; sprite tiers at MIL 4/5/6 (eastern starts at MIL 3 in the mod). Validator 0 errors, 0 warnings. Checklist `build/macedon/checklist.txt`.
- **In-game test 9 — result (2026-10-16): parts PASS, colours FAIL, caused by the mod.** T1-T4 parts all as designed (dory + hoplon with the Vergina sun, sarissas, xyston Companions, xiphos crews, crests per tier, steel-grey type4). Colours wrong on every tier (olive cloth, blue trousers, yellow hoplon = MAC's map colour). Cause: Color Your Subject's startup `restore_country_color` on every country (§4 "Mod scripts that recolour countries"); A/B with `cys.1` disabled in the staged copy (commit `20c845f`): purple tunic, purple hoplon with the gold Vergina sun. Rule `colour_scripts` now warns about it (it ran before as 0 warnings because no rule looked at mod scripts). error.log: none of ours. The mod's own (for its author): ~29.8k "Unknown culture group byzantine/gaelic" from vanilla scripts after the culture regrouping, ~1.7k mission-file parse errors, province history warnings, parse errors in `history/provinces/149 - Edirne.txt` and `151 - Constantinople.txt`.
- **Git:** because the user's repo tracks almost nothing, a branch in it can't isolate edits (untracked files are shared by every branch, and committing them on a branch would delete them from disk on switching back to main). The test is staged as its own git repo instead: `main` = a baseline copy of the working tree, `unitgen-test` = the set integrated, then a TEST ONLY commit.

### Track R: Reference-driven full characters (PLANNING, 2026-10-16)
Goal: "reference in, full unit out": from one or more reference images, a game-ready unit with new body geometry, hair, clothing, armour, accessories, weapons, textures and skinning. **Status: planning only.** Nothing is built except the approved deformation spike (R0). Builds on Phase 3 (parts, bodies, stances) and step 8 (AI backend).

**Measured constraints (1.37.5, base game):**
- Skeleton `western_generic_01_type1` (shared by `western_generic_01-04_type1` and DAN 1-4): 28 bones. `root > lower_spine > mid_spine > top_spine > head`; arms off `top_spine` (`*_shoulder > *_arm > *_elbow > *_hand > *_hand_node`, `*_wrist` off the elbow); `hip` off `lower_spine` (`*_thigh > *_knee > *_foot > *_toe`); `attach_back` (mid_spine), `attach_free` (root). **No neck, tail, hair or cloth bones.** The skin holds up to 4 influences per vertex.
- Triangles (whole .mesh): bodies `western_generic_01_type1` 2,883 (1,809 verts), `western_generic_02_type1` 4,360, `eastern_generic_01_type1` 1,724; `cavalry_horse` 1,874; weapons `cavalry_saber` 146, `western_halbard` 220, `western_musket` 714; shields (indian, south american, AZT) 80. **Budget for a full character: body + skinned additions ≤ 4,500 tris (the largest vanilla body), each rigid part ≤ 300 tris** (rule `part_mesh` allows up to 3,000; a tighter warning per kind is a TODO).
- The type1 body plays `polearm_twohanded_{idle,moving,attack,death,retreat}.anim` (infantry), `cavalry_rider_*` (rider) and `polearm_twohanded_attack` (artillery_attack); scale 0.5. Stature ~14.5 units; the head top is 2.15 above the `head` joint.
- The type1 **helmet is part of the body mesh**: a new helmet (e.g. with ear openings) needs a bareheaded body, or a body mesh edited to remove the helmet (a derived mesh).
- EU4 has no cloth or hair physics. Deforming geometry moves only by skinning to existing bones.
- Attached entities (`attach = { bone = entity }`) are rigid and follow one bone (in-game tests 7-8). Skinned additions must live **inside the body's .mesh**: a body mesh with new skinned geometry, i.e. a **derived mesh** of a base-game body (rule 2: generated output only, never DLC). **Decided 2026-10-16: allowed** on the same terms as derived textures (base game only, never in the repo or app, never from DLC; CLAUDE.md rule 2). UNVERIFIED: whether an attached entity's skinned mesh can follow the parent's skeleton.

**R-1. Reference ingestion and analysis** (no images supplied yet):
- Input: 1-n images (front/side/back ideal) + a rights declaration (original / licensed / authorised; CLAUDE.md hard rule 8).
- Analysis output: `reference_analysis.json` + a mapping table the user confirms or edits in the GUI. Fields:
  - proportions (head-to-height ratio, shoulder/hip width, leg length vs the type1 skeleton);
  - silhouette features;
  - per component: description, colours (median cut, Phase 2), material class (skin / cloth / metal / leather / hair / wood), visibility (visible / partial / not shown), confidence.
- Method: proposed by Claude from the images (vision), always confirmed by the user. Not automatic CV. Unknowns are flagged "uncertain"; never invented silently.
- **Scale rule:** anything under ~1/50 of stature (≈0.3 units: faces, rivets, frills, embroidery, buckles) is texture detail, not geometry. Units are seen at a few dozen pixels.

**Reference-to-Geometry Mapping (stress-test character: original horse-eared, horse-tailed soldier).** R = rigid part on a bone (proven attach); S = skinned into the body mesh (derived); T = texture only; B = the base body.

| Component | Rep. | Bone(s) / weights | Tris | Notes / uncertain |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Body, proportions | B | the skeleton's bind pose | ~2,900 | Proportions must match the bind pose. Animations set bone transforms, so longer limbs or a bigger head need a new mesh on the same joints; joint positions can't move. |
| Head, face | B + T | head | (body) | Face is texture-only at map scale. |
| Horse ears (2) | R | head | 2×~30 | Proven attach. Must poke through the helmet's openings. |
| Bronze crested helmet with ear openings | R (+ bareheaded body) | head | ~200 | The type1 helmet is baked into the body, so this needs a bareheaded body or a derived body without it (open). Crest = the existing `crest` part. |
| Long tied-back hair / ponytail | R (short) or S (long) | R: head. S: head → top_spine gradient | ~100-150 | R is proven but clips the back when the head turns. S is the spike question. |
| Horse tail | R (fallback) or S | R: hip. S: hip → lower_spine | ~100-200 | No tail bones. Spike R0 decides. |
| Muscle cuirass | S (or B's torso) | top/mid/lower_spine (copy weights from the body) | ~400 | Tight to the torso, so it deforms like the body. Muscle relief is normal map + texture. |
| Articulated shoulder guards (pteruges) | R per shoulder, or S | L/R_shoulder | 2×~60 | Rigid on the shoulder bone is plausible; check clipping in the attack anim. |
| Short crimson chiton (skirt) | S | hip + L/R_thigh blend | ~250 | Vanilla coats' tails are skinned this way (plausible). Front/back clipping at walk extremes; colour can be a unit colour. |
| Kopis with decorated hilt | R | Right_hand_node | ~120 | New `kopis` part kind (like xiphos). Decoration is texture. |
| Round bronze-and-wood shield | R | Left_hand_node | ~100 | The existing `hoplon` (proven test 8); bronze/wood = texture zones. |
| Greaves | S or T | knees/shins (copy weights) | ~150 | At map scale probably texture plus a slight shell; uncertain whether geometry is worth it. |
| Sandals, belt, belt accessories | T (+ belt S) | hip | ≤100 | Straps and buckles are texture. |
| Hidden/back details (not described) | — | — | — | Uncertain: flagged, not invented. |

**Per-component feasibility.** Status legend: **P** proven in this project (in game), **Pl** technically plausible, **X** experimental.

| Component | Method | Tools | Compatibility | Quality / limits | Risk | Manual work | From refs alone? | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rigid parts (ears, helmet, weapons, shield, guards) | procedural builders (`core/parts.py`), parameters read from the reference | Blender 4.2 + io_pdx_mesh | attach to bones: proven | clean low-poly, stylised; shape detail limited to builder parameters | Low | little | **No:** the builder is chosen and parameters are set from the reference, with confirmation | P (dory, sarissa, xiphos, xyston, hoplon, crest) |
| Rigid parts, unusual shapes | image-to-3D (TripoSG local, Tripo API opt-in) → decimate to budget → UV → bake | TripoSG env, Blender | static mesh: fine | single-image geometry is approximate (hidden sides guessed), dense, needs retopology/decimation and clean-up; untextured | Med-High | likely | Partly: an approximation, not an accurate reconstruction | X (step 8) |
| Body with new proportions / bareheaded | (a) choose the closest vanilla body (`body_candidates`); (b) edit a derived body (remove helmet, adjust) | Blender | same skeleton: yes; bind pose fixed | (a) zero risk, limited choice; (b) quality depends on manual editing | (a) Low, (b) High | (b) yes | No | (a) P, (b) Pl |
| Skinned additions (tail, long hair, skirt, cuirass) | geometry built (procedural/AI) + weights: copied from the nearest body surface (Data Transfer) or authored gradients; auto (heat) weights evaluated in R0 | Blender | needs a derived body .mesh with the new geometry; UNVERIFIED in game | clipping at animation extremes; no secondary motion | Med (skirt, cuirass), High (tail, long hair) | likely | No | Pl → R0 |
| New bones (tail chain) | add bones + author **every** animation the body plays (11 ids on type1, more per stance), vanilla tracks re-exported + new tracks (baked procedural lag) | Blender, io_pdx_mesh anim export | UNVERIFIED that the engine accepts extra bones with vanilla .anim; new .anim files are derived output | best motion; frozen without new anims | High | yes | No | X (last resort; est. 3-5 sessions + an in-game test) |
| Textures / materials | Phase 2 recipes (recolour, palette, pattern, spec masks) + procedural material swatches; painted blazons/decor from user PNGs | Pillow, texconv | proven chain | reference palette accurate; painted ornament needs a source image | Low | for ornament | Colours yes; ornament partly (projection from the reference is plausible but needs aligned UVs) | P (recipes), Pl (projection) |
| AI texture generation | image model inpaints UV layouts from the reference | none chosen | — | inconsistent seams; licence questions | High | yes | partly | X (not planned) |
| Full character from one image | image-to-3D whole figure | TripoSG/Tripo | fused, unrigged, arbitrary pose; doesn't fit the skeleton | not game-usable without full retopology and rigging | High | heavy | **No.** No available tool reconstructs a rigged, skeleton-matched character from one image. The pipeline assembles one from a base body + parts + skinned additions instead. | X (rejected as the main path) |

**Architecture (proposed):** reference images → `reference_analysis` (vision proposal, user-confirmed mapping) → a **character recipe** (JSON, like `loadout.json`):
- `body` (vanilla candidate or derived edit);
- `parts` (rigid, existing kinds + new kinds);
- `skinned` (meshes + weight method);
- `textures` (Phase 2 recipe);
- `colours`.

Generators (Blender jobs, subprocess only) → package → validator. New rules: skinned geometry in budget, ≤4 influences per vertex, only skeleton bones, weights sum to 1; derived body only from base-game bodies. Loadout slots stay the unit of output (tier × role).

**R0. Deformation spike (approved 2026-10-16). Answers: can a tail and a ponytail be skinned to the unmodified skeleton well enough?**
- Inputs: placeholder meshes made by the job, no copyrighted input:
  - tail: tapered tube, 8 sides × 10 rings ≈ 160 tris, root at the back of the pelvis, length ~5 units hanging down and back at 30°;
  - ponytail: tapered tube ≈ 120 tris from the back of the head (below the helmet rim), length ~4 units down the back.
- Read from the install at run time: `western_generic_01_type1.mesh` (io_pdx_mesh `import_meshfile`) and `polearm_twohanded_idle/moving/attack.anim` (`import_animfile`). Nothing is written outside `build/spike_r0/` (git-ignored). No .mesh/.anim export, no mod files.
- Weighting variants (each part, all on the unmodified 28-bone skeleton):
  - **A auto:** Blender automatic (heat) weights, limited to plausible bones (tail: hip, lower_spine, mid_spine, L/R_thigh; ponytail: head, top_spine, mid_spine, shoulders), then cut to the 4 largest and normalised;
  - **B rigid:** tail 100% hip; ponytail 100% head;
  - **C gradient:** tail hip → lower_spine along the length (root 100% hip, tip 50/50); ponytail head → top_spine (root 100% head, tip 30/70).
- Animations: each of idle, moving, attack, every frame.
- Renders (Eevee, orthographic, neutral grey materials, body semi-dark, additions coloured):
  - front, side, rear at 8 evenly spaced frames per animation, plus the frame with the worst metric;
  - one contact sheet per variant × animation;
  - a "map-scale" render (the unit ~48 px tall) per variant.
- Metrics per frame (JSON):
  - **stretch:** edge length / rest length (min, max, 95th percentile);
  - **penetration:** addition vertices inside the body mesh (ray-parity test) and the deepest one (units);
  - **flips:** triangles whose normal turns > 90° from rest, relative to the driving bone;
  - **root drift:** root-ring vertices' distance from rest in the hip/head frame;
  - **tail-through-legs:** the tail's lowest point vs the thighs' rear surface;
  - **ponytail-through-back:** penetration into the torso.
- **Pass criteria** (per part and variant, in all three animations):
  1. stretch within 0.85-1.20 for every edge, and 95th percentile within 0.92-1.08;
  2. penetration: idle and moving 0 vertices deeper than 0.10 units (~1.2 cm); attack ≤ 3% of vertices, none deeper than 0.30;
  3. no flipped triangles;
  4. root drift ≤ 0.05 units;
  5. tail never passes in front of the thigh plane; ponytail never enters the torso by more than criterion 2 allows;
  6. **user sign-off** on the contact sheets and map-scale renders (reads as a tail/ponytail, no visible popping or twisting).
- **Outcome:**
  - The technique is **acceptable without modifying the skeleton** if at least one variant per part passes 1-6. Automatic weights (A) are reported on their own: passing with B/C only means "authored weights, not auto".
  - If no variant passes: report which criterion fails where, and estimate the alternatives (rigid fallback, shorter geometry, new bones + animations).
- Limits: the spike shows Blender's playback of the vanilla .anim through io_pdx_mesh. UNVERIFIED that it matches the game exactly (stances were verified in game, the importer's playback wasn't). The in-game check (a derived body .mesh with the tail, in a test mod) is a separate step after R0.
- Code: one throwaway Blender job + runner call under `scripts/spike_r0/` (not part of the app; removed or promoted after the decision).

**R0 result (2026-10-16, Blender playback; criterion 6 = user sign-off pending).** Scripts `scripts/spike_r0/` (`run.py`, `spike_job.py`, `report.py`); output `build/spike_r0/` (`metrics.md`, `sheets/`).
- **io_pdx_mesh fact:**
  - The `polearm_twohanded` anims animate `attack_L_hand`/`attack_R_hand`, which the type1 mesh lacks, and `import_animfile` refuses ("Missing bones required for animation"). The spike adds unweighted dummy bones under `root`, which is equivalent to the game ignoring those tracks.
  - Successive anim imports pile onto one action, so reload the body per animation.
  - Clips: idle 170 frames, moving 21, attack 59, all at 15 fps.
- **Tail (156 tris):**
  - **B rigid (100% `hip`) and C gradient (`hip` → `lower_spine`, tip 50/50) pass C1-C5 in all three anims.** Stretch for C is 0.92-1.07 in the attack. No penetration, no flips, no root drift. The tip ranges from 34° to 58° below horizontal; it swings out behind in the attack lunge and looks natural by eye.
  - A auto (heat) **fails**: it put 37% of the weight on the thighs, so the tail bends with the leg. Root drift reaches 0.48 and stretch 0.67.
  - **Verdict: a tail works on the unmodified skeleton with authored weights, not automatic ones.**
- **Ponytail (124 tris, 4 units to mid-back):**
  - **No variant passes.** All are fine in the walk.
  - In idle the head turns and tilts. B rigid (100% `head`) swings into the shoulder: 57% of vertices inside, 0.38 deep.
  - In the attack the torso leans and the head tips back: B is 71% inside and 0.94 deep, and passes in front of `top_spine`. C gradient (`head` → `top_spine`, tip 30/70) is 66% inside and 0.52 deep, and stretches to 0.76.
  - A auto put 54% on the shoulders and fails everywhere.
  - Cause: geometry length and clearance against the head's rotation relative to `top_spine`, not a missing weighting trick.
  - At 48 px the clipping is barely visible.
- **Map scale (48 px):** the tail reads as a 4-6 px streak from the rear and a clear diagonal in the attack. From the front it is hidden except in the attack. Variants can't be told apart.
- **Consequences:**
  - The pipeline needs a **procedural weight author** (gradients along a part's bone chain). Automatic weights are rejected for skinned additions.
  - Ponytail alternatives, untested:
    1. a short ponytail (to the nape, ~2 units) as a **rigid part on `head`**: the proven attach path, no derived body. Low cost, one spike rerun;
    2. long hair with more clearance and a 3-bone gradient `head` → `top_spine` → `mid_spine`: Medium;
    3. new bones + re-authored anims: High, last resort.
  - UNVERIFIED that Blender playback matches the game. The tail's in-game check (derived type1 body with the tail merged) is the next gate.

**R0 sign-off (user, 2026-10-16):**
- **Tail signed off:** motion good (attached, smooth, no leg clipping). **Use the `hip` → `lower_spine` gradient (C).**
- Tail part design notes:
  - **flared/tufted base** to hide the join where the tube leaves the tunic (the plain tube's seam looked off);
  - the base sits over the tunic hem;
  - tuft detail is texture plus a few split vertices, not geometry.
- **Ponytail not signed off.** Fine in walk, fails idle and attack.

**R0b ponytail rerun (2026-10-16; `run.py --ponytail-rerun`, output `build/spike_r0/rerun_ponytail/`):**
- **D (alternative 1): short nape tuft,** 2 units at 25° from vertical, 92 tris, rigid on `head`.
  - Passes idle and walk. In idle 7.5% of verts are inside, but only 0.036 deep.
  - **Fails attack:** 50% of verts inside, 0.58 deep; passes in front of `top_spine` (the head tips back).
  - Looks right by eye in idle and walk. In the attack it sinks into the neck. Invisible at 48 px.
- **E (alternative 2): long hair,** 4 units, 0.6 clearance, weights from projecting each vertex's height onto the `head` → `top_spine` → `mid_spine` joints.
  - **Fails everything but walk drift:** stretch up to 3.37×, 12 flipped triangles, 1.03 deep.
  - It kinks at the head joint and shoots sideways in the attack, which is visible even at 48 px.
- **Lesson:** don't blend one long span across a high-rotation joint. Most of the head's rotation happens against `top_spine`.

**Required pipeline component: procedural chain-weight tool** (automatic heat weights are rejected; R0, R0b). It sets skinned weights from the part's geometry and a declared bone chain:
- **(a) along-length gradient** for parts that grow away from the chain (tail: root 100% first bone, tip blended; R0 C passes);
- **(b) chain projection** for parts lying along the chain, **never blending across a high-rotation joint** (`head`/`top_spine`; R0b E fails). A span that crosses one is split or kept rigid on one side.

Common to both modes:
- ≤4 influences per vertex, normalised;
- skeleton bones only;
- root rings locked 100% to the root bone.

A validator rule checks the result: influences, sums, bone names, and that no weight sits on a bone outside the declared chain.

**In-game test 10 (PLANNED, not built): combined derived-body test, HDA tier 1** (test mod `eu4unitgen_test`, set `macedonian_test`, hellenicgfx).
- **T1 infantry:** a **derived `western_generic_01_type1`** with the gradient tail (flared base) and the chosen ponytail merged in. The ponytail is weighted 100% `head` if rigid, because the loadout allows one attached part per bone and `head` takes the ears. Plus rigid **horse ears** on `head` (new kind `horse_ears`: both ears in one mesh), and the test-8 **dory + hoplon** with the `spear_shield` stance.
- **Rider and gun crew** keep the vanilla body: a tail on a rider is untested against the horse.
- **Pre-checks in Blender before anything goes in game:**
  - **P1:** R0 metrics for the tail and ponytail on the **`spear_shield` idle/moving/attack** anims (R0 tested `polearm_twohanded`; the hoplon stance plays `spear_shield`).
  - **P2: skinned export round-trip.** io_pdx_mesh export of the derived body is untested; only static parts have been round-tripped. Check that it:
    - re-imports;
    - keeps the skeleton identical (names, parents, bind pose);
    - leaves the vanilla vertices' positions and weights unchanged;
    - has ≤4 influences;
    - matches vanilla's skinned layout (`skin` with `bones`, `ix`, `w`), with `boundingsphere` stripped.
- **Textures:**
  - **One shape, one material.** No vanilla unit mesh has more than one shape or material (1.37.5 scan), so a two-material skinned body would be untested.
  - The additions' UVs map onto an existing flat region of the vanilla type1 diffuse that has no country-colour mask, so no new texture is needed.
  - The derived .mesh goes in the mod's `gfx/models/Units/` so its bare texture names resolve to vanilla's files (UNVERIFIED; fallback: derived `hr_` copies of the textures).
  - Ears get their own procedural textures.
- **Build:**
  - `core/naming.py` names for derived body meshes;
  - a `derived_body` builder (Blender job) with the chain-weight tool;
  - the `horse_ears` part kind;
  - a loadout `"bodies"` entry that can name a derived body;
  - a pdxmesh restating the body's animation ids and the stance.
- **New validator rule `derived_body`:**
  - the source is a base-game body (never DLC);
  - the skeleton is identical to the source;
  - weights pass the chain-weight checks;
  - ≤4,500 tris;
  - textures resolve;
  - names don't collide with vanilla.
- **In-game checklist:**
  - the tail and ponytail are attached and move with the body (idle, walk, attack);
  - the ears sit through the helmet (the type1 helmet is baked in; clipping with it is expected and accepted for this test);
  - dory and hoplon as in test 8;
  - no black or missing textures;
  - error.log clean for `hr_`/mesh/texture lines;
  - map-scale look.

**Test 10 pre-checks (2026-10-16; `scripts/spike_r0/run.py --p1 / --p1-metrics / --p2`, output `build/spike_r0/p1_spear_shield`, `p2_export`):**
- **P1 (`spear_shield` idle/moving/attack): PASS.**
  - **Flared tail (C):** stretch 0.96-1.04, no flips, no drift. The only penetration is ring 1, the flare overlapping the tunic hem by design (also in the bind pose). At least 0.70 behind the hip in the facing frame.
  - **Ponytail = F, the 1.5-unit tuft at 45°, rigid on `head`:** zero penetration in every frame. G (bun) fails the attack (3.1% > 3%, 0.28 deep); D fails again (57.5%, 0.63). Ponytail rounds are closed (user, 2026-10-16).
  - **Corrections:**
    - C5 for ponytails was coded stricter than agreed ("behind top_spine's axis" vs "no torso entry beyond C2"). No earlier verdict changes.
    - The bone-axis "behind" metric misreads a forward lunge, so `behind_facing` uses the unit's facing frame (`root`).
  - The anim importer now adds dummies for whatever bones an `.anim` names that the mesh lacks.
- **P2 (export + re-import of the weighted body, io_pdx_mesh 0.91): FAILS two agreed checks; stopped for the user's decision.**
  - Derived body: vanilla type1 + flared tail + tuft F joined into one shape and one material. The additions' UVs are in the face texture's hair patch, px 105-115 × 30-40, which has no country-colour mask.
  - **Good:**
    - re-imports, and `spear_shield_attack` plays on it;
    - same 28 bones in the same order;
    - skin layout as vanilla (`bones` 4, `ix`, `w`), ≤4 influences, weights sum to 1;
    - every vanilla vertex's position, UV and weights reproduced exactly (normals: 1 vertex off by > 0.01);
    - same 8 locators.
  - **Fails:**
    1. **Every face corner becomes its own vertex.** 8,649 verts for the untouched vanilla body (vanilla 1,809); 9,345 for the derived one. It is the exporter's behaviour, reproduced on an unmodified import → export, probably from per-corner tangents or normals; UNVERIFIED.
    2. **The skeleton is not identical.** Bind-pose round-off up to 0.0013, and **`attach_free`'s all-zero vanilla bind matrix becomes identity.**
  - `boundingsphere` is added as usual; `strip_mesh_props` handles it.
  - Options (not tried):
    - **(a) recommended:** write the derived body in Python from vanilla's parsed tree. Copy every vanilla block verbatim (skeleton, locators, material, vertices) and append only the additions' vertices, triangles and skin rows, which Blender computes. `scanner.write_pdx_mesh` is byte-identical on all 458 vanilla meshes. Blender export is not used for bodies.
    - (b) weld the exporter's output and restore vanilla's skeleton block.
    - (c) take the export as it is into the game.

**Standard path for derived bodies (decided 2026-10-16, option (a)): io_pdx_mesh's export is NOT used for skinned bodies.**
- `core/derived_body.py` parses the base-game body's `.mesh` and keeps every block as it is: skeleton (`attach_free`'s all-zero bind included), locators, material, every vertex array, skin rows and triangles.
- It appends only the additions' vertices (`p n ta u0`), skin rows and triangles, and grows the `aabb` to hold them. `scanner.write_pdx_mesh` writes the result.
- Supporting pieces:
  - `tangents()` computes `ta` in the file's convention. On base-game bodies and the horse it matches the stored tangents: median 0.07-0.13°, ~99% within 10°, handedness ~99%.
  - Additions follow the base's triangle winding (`winding_sign`; +1 on every base-game unit mesh checked).
  - `check_derived(base, derived)` verifies all of this. It is used by rule `derived_body` and by the P2 re-check.
- Bodies with more than one shape or mesh block are refused (none exist in the base game).
- **Additions** (`ADDITION_KINDS`) are built in background Blender by `blender/jobs/build_additions.py`: geometry and smooth normals only, in the base body's bind pose, converted Blender (x, y, z) → PDX (x, z, y). Weights come from `core/chain_weights.py`.
  - `tail`: length gradient `hip` → `lower_spine`, tip 50/50, flared base 0.75/0.6.
  - `tuft`: rigid on `head`, 1.5 units at 45°.
- **UVs:** the face texture's hair patch of the type1 diffuse, px 105-115 × 30-40 (`HAIR_PATCH`, image convention v down). The file's `u0` uses that convention: a vanilla head vertex's u0 maps into the helmet's texture region.
- **P2 re-check (2026-10-16): PASS.**
  - `check_derived` clean: 1,809 + 120 vertices, 2,883 + 232 triangles; every vanilla block identical.
  - Blender re-imports 1,929 vertices, 28 bones and 8 locators, and `spear_shield_attack` moves the added vertices.
  - Check render: textures loaded from the install in place, through a material built for the render (io_pdx_mesh looks for textures beside the .mesh). Tail and tuft show dark brown.
  - The added vertices sample mean diffuse 41 33 29 with country mask 0, so the unit colours don't tint them.
- **Loadout `"derived": {"<tier>": ["tail", "tuft"]}`:**
  - That tier's infantry wears `hr_<set>_body_<tier>.mesh`, placed in the base body's folder (`gfx/models/Units`) so the copied material's bare texture names resolve to the base game's textures there (UNVERIFIED in game; test 10).
  - It gets a new pdxmesh with the stance animations, scale and keys, and no `meshsettings`.
  - The rider and a crew slot's crew keep the base body. Not combinable with a texture recipe's retextured body yet.
- **Rule `derived_body`:**
  - base body from the base game, never DLC; `derived_from` recorded;
  - `check_derived` clean;
  - additions' weights only on their declared chain;
  - ≤4,500 triangles (`DERIVED_TRI_BUDGET`);
  - material textures resolve in the file's folder;
  - not a game file's name;
  - the infantry wears it.
- `part_mesh` skips derived bodies. `loadout`'s body check accepts a derived body of the chosen body.
- **New part kind `horse_ears`** (on `head`, both ears in one mesh): cones on oval bases, tilted 25° out and leaning 10° back, outer zone (95 70 50) and inner zone (150 105 95). Fitting puts the base 0.45 below the helmet top and the spread at 0.63 × the head's half-width (1.702 / 0.622 on type1).

Constraints carried over:
- The body is one shape with one material, so tail, tuft and anything skinned use the body's texture.
- One attached part per bone.
- Skinned shapes must pass the R0 criteria on the stance they'll play.
- The tuft must stay within the P1 envelope (≤1.5 units, 45°, root at the back of the head) unless re-checked.

**Base body without the medieval helmet** (read-only scan, 1.37.5):
- 101 base-game infantry bodies share type1's 28-bone skeleton.
- Head renders (`build/plan11/heads_sheet.png`) show bare heads on `african_generic_03_type1` (short hair, plain tunic and sash), `african_generic_02`, `african_generic_01_type1` (bead necklace) and `north_american_generic_03_type1` (mohawk). All are PdxMeshColor.
- Options:
  - **H1 (recommended first):** check in Blender what is under type1's helmet. Delete the triangles whose UVs fall in the helmet's texture islands and render the head.
    - If a full skull or hair cap is there, a derived type1 with the helmet removed keeps the Greek-looking tunic.
    - The writer needs a "remove triangles" mode: it drops the listed base triangles and keeps every vertex array unchanged (unreferenced vertices stay). `check_derived` then verifies everything else is identical and that only those triangles are gone.
    - If there's a hole, a scalp-cap addition (rigid `head`, hair UVs) closes it; the Greek helmet covers most of it anyway.
  - **H2:** a bareheaded base body (`african_generic_03_type1` is the closest to a plain tunic). Clothing and skin tone come from that body; a texture recipe can recolour. No mesh editing needed.
  - **H3:** both, side by side in one test (two tiers).
- **Greek helmet:** a new rigid part kind on `head`, `greek_helmet`, with styles attic, corinthian and pilos, an integrated crest zone, and cheek guards for attic/corinthian. It is fitted from the bareheaded head's measured extents.
- **Ear openings:** faked by placement (the ears come out through the helmet shell where openings would be). Real holes only if the in-game look needs them.

**Ears, tail and tuft go into the derived body**, all skinned and rigid on their bones. This frees the `head` attach for the helmet, which carries the crest.
- **Horse ears:**
  - lanceolate, about 2.2× as long as wide, pointed, base pinched;
  - front cupped (concave, opening forward and out);
  - on top of the head, closer together than the placeholder, upright and slightly forward;
  - 2 × ~60 tris;
  - outer in the hair texture, inner a darker part of the same region (no pink).
  - The placeholder looked rat-like because of the round cone base, pink inner zone, wide spread and outward tilt.
- **Tail:**
  - a short dock under the tunic hem, then a broad hair plume;
  - cross-section a flattened ellipse, wider side to side;
  - widening to ~40% of the length, then tapering;
  - a **flared, tufted base** and an uneven tip (3-5 strand points);
  - ≤350 tris. Same chain (`hip` → `lower_spine`).
- **Tuft:** a tied lock (a wrap ring near the root), flaring to a flattened brush with a split tip, within the P1 envelope. If the Greek helmet's neck guard covers the root, it moves down to the helmet's rear rim, which is a new envelope and needs a re-check (P1's nape bun failed narrowly).

**Texture for skinned additions:**
- **T1 (first):** spread their UVs over the face texture's whole hair region (streaks), not the 10 px patch.
- **T2 (if flat):** a derived copy of the body's diffuse/normal/spec (base game only, `hr_` names, same folder) with a procedural hair swatch painted into an unused grey region (flat grey 101 islands exist, e.g. around px 240-250 × 120-160). Mask 0 and a flat normal there. The derived body's material then names the derived textures.
  - Rule `derived_body` would allow exactly that material change.
  - Rule `dds_format` already checks derived textures against their base.

**Automated deformation check:** the spike's R0 metrics become a reusable Blender job (`check_deform`). It runs at generation for every derived body on the stance its infantry plays: idle, walk and attack.
- Criteria as R0, with the facing-frame C5. Base rings and flared-base rings are exempt from C2.
- It reports to the validator as errors (C1-C3) and warnings (C4-C5), plus worst-frame renders for the GUI.

**In-game test 11 (planned):** HDA tier 1 (and tier 2 if H3), the new bareheaded body, ears + tail + tuft in the derived body, `greek_helmet` (with crest), dory and hoplon, `spear_shield`.
