  - `check_deform` passes on `spear_shield`;
  - P2-style `check_derived`;
  - textured renders (front, side, rear, rear-quarter, map scale).
- **Side-view camera procedure** (the EU4 camera can't rotate; units face the way they move):
  - march the army east or west across two or more provinces and pause mid-march, zoomed fully in: units walk side-on;
  - after arriving they idle facing their last direction of movement (UNVERIFIED);
  - for fighting side-on, attack an army in the province directly east or west.
- **Checklist lines:**
  - tail visible side-on and from the rear;
  - flared base hides the hem join;
  - ears read as horse ears;
  - helmet sits on a bare head with no medieval helmet showing through;
  - tuft below the helmet rim;
  - map-scale read.

**H1 result (2026-10-16): a full head is under type1's helmet.** The helmet is 369 triangles skinned to `head` with UVs in the diffuse's px 128-240 × 0-200 (the face and hair use px 0-127). Without them, type1 is a bareheaded soldier with a closed, textured hair cap. The rest of the head is one connected piece of 506 triangles; one stray helmet triangle sits on the UV boundary (px 128, 99). Renders: `build/plan11/h1/h1_heads.png` (`scripts/plan11/head_render_job.py`). **H1 (derived type1 with the helmet removed) is the way; H2 stays the fallback.**

**Removal mode (step 2, 2026-10-16):** `core.derived_body.REMOVALS` names removable parts per base body. type1's `helmet` is `Removal("head", px 127.5-240 × 0-200)`: any triangle with a vertex mostly on `head` whose UV centroid lies there, 370 triangles including the boundary one. `append_additions(base, adds, remove)` drops them and keeps every vertex array (the helmet's vertices stay, unreferenced). `check_derived(base, derived, remove)` verifies that exactly those are gone and everything else is unchanged. Fitting (`_skin_points`) and `build_additions`' back profile use only vertices a triangle uses: the bare type1 head is top 1.795 (2.152 with the helmet), back 0.846. Loadout `"derived": {"1": {"add": [...], "remove": ["helmet"]}}` (a plain list still means add only). The derived body is built at the start of its tier, so parts on that infantry are fitted to it. Rule `derived_body` recomputes the removal from the base and counts removed triangles against the budget. Render: `build/plan11/h1/h1_removed370.png` (clean bare head).

**Step 3 (2026-10-16): shaped additions + automatic deformation check.**
- **Shapes** (`blender/jobs/build_additions.py`, ring meshes, closed and manifold):
  - `tail`: tufted flared base, slim dock, plume widest at ~40%, strand ridges, 5-point tip; 125 verts, 238 tris.
  - `tuft`: tie, flattened brush, 3-point tip, within the P1 envelope, anchored at the base's original back (type1's helmet back: where P1 validated it; the Greek helmet's back must sit there so the tuft comes out of it); 33 verts, 58 tris.
  - `ears`: two crescent-section shells cupped forward and turned 25° out, pinched base, widest a quarter up, pointed; on top of the bare head, placed from its head-skinned vertices; rigid `head`; 82 verts, 156 tris.
  - type1 − helmet + all three: 2,965 triangles. Renders: `build/plan11/s3/shapes_bind.png`, `ears_turned.png`.
- **`check_deform`** (Blender job) + `core/deform_check.py`: the R0 metrics on the infantry pdxmesh's own idle/walk/attack. About 4 s for three animations. It runs in `apply_loadout` for every derived body with additions, and rule `derived_body` reports `judge()`: C1-C3 errors, C4-C5 warnings, or a warning when the check couldn't run.
  - Per kind: `exempt` rings for C2 (tail 0-1, tuft 0, ears 0) and the `behind` joint for C5 (tail `hip`, tuft `top_spine`, ears none).
  - **Inside test fix:** the nearest base face's *smooth* normal (interpolated vertex normals). The flat face normal flagged a tuft tip in the air as 1.1 inside the head (renders `build/plan11/s3/tuft_diag.png`).
- **Results (type1 − helmet):**
  - **Tail and ears pass C1-C5 on `spear_shield` and `polearm_twohanded`.**
  - **The tuft fails C2:** spear_shield attack 29.6% / 0.97 deep, idle one vertex 0.56; polearm attack 11.1% / 0.69, idle 0.84. Real in the attack: the torso leans and the shoulders hunch, and the tip sinks into the top of the back by the collar (`tuft_attack_diag.png`).
  - Per the user's rule closing the ponytail rounds (accept the clipping as a known limit, judged in game), the loadout's `"accept_clipping": ["tuft"]` turns the tuft's C2 findings into warnings marked as accepted. They are still reported.

**Step 4 (2026-10-16): part kind `greek_helmet`** (rigid on `head`, builder `helmet`).
- **Shell:** a bronze shell with thickness. A dome (an ellipsoid fitted to the bare head) to its equator, then a near-vertical skirt to a rim whose depth below the crown varies around the head by style (`RIM_DROP`):
  - **attic:** brow 0.75, cheek guards 2.05, neck guard 1.45;
  - **corinthian:** face 2.2, sides 2.15, back 1.5, reaching 0.22 further forward over the nose, with an eye slot (zone 2) on the front row nearest eye height;
  - **pilos:** level 1.05, the crown raised into a cone.
- **Crest:** integrated (zone 1), fore-aft, transverse or none.
- **Fitting:** top = head top + 0.12, half-width + 0.13, brow at 0.8 × the head's front reach − 0.1, back = head back + 0.2. On bare type1: top 1.915, half-width 0.85, back 1.046, at type1's old helmet back, so the tuft comes out of it.
- **One attached part per bone:** the helmet takes `head`; ears, tail and tuft live in the derived body.
- **Fitting basis:** derived bodies are fitted on the base after removals and **without** additions (head-skinned ears would otherwise pass for the head). This applies both in `apply_loadout` and in the preview (`_body`).
- **Preview:** shows a tier's derived body and adds head close-ups (three-quarter, side, rear) whenever a part hangs on `head` (`render_parts` `close`/`close_views`).
- Renders: `build/plan11/helmets_close.png`, `corinthian_close.png`.

**Step 5 (2026-10-16): hair textures, option T1.** `core.derived_body.HAIR_REGIONS` gives per base body the diffuse's hair that additions' UVs span, strands running down it (each addition's length runs down the region). type1: px 70-118 × 30-92, the back-of-head hair (the largest unmasked hair-coloured rectangle, 68-120 × 28-100, kept clear of the neck skin; mean 40 32 28). A body without a region is refused. Result (`build/plan11/s5/hair_textured.png`): dark brown with faint strands, matching the soldier's hair; reads as dark hair at map scale. **T2 (a derived texture copy with a painted, lighter hair swatch) is deferred until after test 11.**

**Step 6 (2026-10-16): pre-checks for test 11, both body options.**
- **T2 body:** `african_generic_03_type1` (bareheaded, no removal). Hair region from **its scalp's own UVs** (head vertices at the crown and back; px 208-234 × 5-13 of 256, black, mask 0; `HAIR_REGIONS` entries now carry the texture size). The scalp-UV method also lands on type1's hair; candidate for computing regions automatically.
- **Staged:** `build/test11/stage`, validator **0 errors, 4 warnings**: three are the tuft's accepted C2, one is a marginal C5 (T2 tuft idle -0.04). `check_derived` clean on both bodies.
- **Deformation check:** tail and ears pass on both. T2's tuft clips far less in the attack (0.12 deep) than T1's (0.97).
- **Posed renders** (`scripts/plan11/unit_render_job.py`: derived body + staged part meshes following their bones through `spear_shield` walk/attack):
  - close-ups `build/test11/precheck/precheck_close.png`: helmet, dory and hoplon follow their bones; the tail reads clearly side-on and from the rear, most of all in the attack;
  - map scale `precheck_map.png` (48 px from above, camera angle UNVERIFIED): the helmet and hoplon read; **the dark tail and ears barely read** (dark on dark, foreshortened from above). This is the case for T2 (lighter hair swatch).

**In-game test 11 — STAGED (2026-10-16):** `build/test11/stage` (mod `eu4unitgen_test`). HDA / `macedonian_test` / hellenicgfx, loadout `build/test11/loadout.json`, unit colours blue / yellow / black, test sprite techs 2 3 4 (tier 2 at MIL 2).
- **T1:** derived `western_generic_01_type1` - helmet + tail, tuft, ears.
- **T2:** loadout body `african_generic_03_type1` + tail, tuft, ears.
- **Both:** attic `greek_helmet` (fore-aft crest), dory, hoplon, `spear_shield`, their own crew slots on plain bodies; `accept_clipping: ["tuft"]`.
- Validator 0 errors, 4 warnings (step 6). Checklist `build/test11/checklist.txt`: per-tier derived-body lines (removal included), a side-view procedure (V1-V4), a body comparison (B1), and map-scale reading (D6, which decides the lighter hair texture).

- **Result (2026-10-16): PASS.** Both tiers look fine; every part and both derived bodies work in game. error.log and "tail side-on": **not reported** (the user's report left them as placeholders).
  - **Feedback 1, ears:** they read as cat ears, not a horse's ("catboy Spartans"), and look paper-thin. Wanted: longer and narrower, a softer rounded tip, set wider apart on the sides of the crown, angled slightly out and back, real thickness, a cupped inner side.
  - **Feedback 2, quality:** our parts look flat next to the vanilla body's hand-painted textures.

**Quality measurements (2026-10-16, `scripts/plan11/measure_quality.py`; read-only):**
- **Definitions:**
  - texel density = sqrt(UV area in texels / surface area), texels per mesh unit;
  - detail = mean std dev of luminance in 8×8 blocks;
  - shading = luminance std dev after a 16 px blur (broad painted light, shadow and AO);
  - normal tilt = mean tangent-space tilt from flat;
  - gloss = spec alpha.
- **Base game:**

  | Mesh | Texture | Density | Detail | Shading | Value range | Normal tilt | Gloss |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `western_generic_01_type1` | 512 | 32.8 | 12.6 | 21.6 | 13-132 | 12.9° | 6 |
  | `african_generic_03_type1` | 256 | 16.5 | 12.1 | 26.0 | 10-116 | 11.7° | 4 |
  | `western_halbard` | 512 | 25.2 | 11.3 | 6.9 | | 5.1° | 6 |
  | `western_musket` | 512 | 21.0 | 18.9 | 3.8 | | 13.4° | 18 |
  | `cavalry_saber` | 256 | 17.5 | 21.5 | 11.3 | | 4.2° | 8 |
  | indian shield | 256 | 26.9 | 12.0 | 12.0 | | 19.6° | 11 |
  | south american shield | 256 | 9.8 | 18.5 | 16.7 | | 7.6° | 6 |

- **Ours (test 11):**
  - **Strip-layout parts (dory, helmet, crest), 64 px: density 0.** Every face of a zone maps to one swatch texel, so each zone is a flat colour with no detail and no painted shading.
  - Hoplon (disc, 256): density 22.9 but detail 0 (flat colours).
  - All of them: normal maps flat (2.3°, compression); gloss 16-26, shinier than vanilla's 4-18.
  - Additions on the body texture: tail 15.7, tuft 40.3, ears 62.2 texels/unit against the body's 32.8 (inconsistent).

**Plan: ear redesign + "match vanilla quality" pass (PLANNED, nothing built).**
- **Q0 ears** (feedback 1; `build_additions` `ears`, rigid `head`, in the derived body):
  - length 1.1 → ~1.5; widest 0.50 → ~0.36 (about 4:1 instead of 2.2:1);
  - **rounded tip**: the last rings close in a small dome instead of a point;
  - placed **on the sides of the crown**: spread 0.5 → ~0.85 × half-width, base a little lower;
  - angled **out ~25° and back ~15°** (they leaned forward 8°);
  - **real thickness** (~0.12 at the base, a solid lens section, not a thin crescent) with a **cupped inner side**: the front face concave, its UVs on the darkest part of the hair region;
  - they come out through the Greek helmet's sides.
  - Acceptance: renders next to the old ears; `check_deform` clean (rigid on `head`); in game, reads as horse, not cat.
- **Q1 UV unwrap and texel density.** Replace swatch-point UVs with real unwraps:
  - shafts: cylindrical, the length along v;
  - blades and fins: planar;
  - helmet: spherical / dome projection plus a skirt strip;
  - shield: keep the disc, plus a rim strip.
  - Texture size from the area, targeting the body's density (≈33 texels/unit; base-game weapons 17-27). The dory's long shaft gets a tall strip (e.g. 64×512).
  - Additions on the body texture get UV scales normalised to ~33. Where the hair region is too small for that, Q5's derived hair swatch is used.
  - Acceptance: measured density within 17-35 for every part.
- **Q2 painted base colour** (procedural, per zone, in vanilla's style):
  - material noise: wood grain, hammered bronze, horsehair strands, leather;
  - **baked AO** and a **curvature** pass (cavities darker, edges lighter: painted wear);
  - a soft top-light gradient, like vanilla's painted lighting.
  - Bakes in background Blender (Cycles, CPU, a few seconds a part; cached by job hash, like parts now).
  - Acceptance: detail 11-20, shading 7-20, value range comparable to the vanilla item of the same material.
- **Q3 normal maps** baked from higher-detail versions of each part (subdivided and displaced: grain, hammer marks, the rim bead, crest hair strands), tangent space in the .mesh's convention (`core.derived_body.tangents` matches vanilla's). Acceptance: tilt 5-15°.
- **Q4 geometry:**
  - smooth normals on curved parts (hard edges only where materials meet);
  - thickness for thin parts (ears, crest fin, cheek guards) and a small bevel on rims and edges, so they catch highlights;
  - triangle budgets kept (rigid parts ≤300-400; the helmet now 404).
- **Q5 gloss and hair:**
  - spec alpha into vanilla's 4-18 per material (bronze ~13 → ~10, wood and cloth ~4);
  - **T2 hair swatch** (deferred since step 5): a derived copy of the body's diffuse/normal/spec (base game only, `hr_` names) with a painted horsehair swatch in an unused grey island, sized for Q1's density. It also fixes the map-scale read: a lighter, warmer brown than the soldier's hair.
- **Q6 measurement as a check:** `measure_quality` becomes a validator report (`texture_quality`): warnings when a generated part's density, detail, shading, normal tilt or gloss falls outside the base-game range for its material.
- **Order:** Q0 → Q1 → Q4 → Q2 → Q3 → Q5 → Q6 → renders side by side with vanilla (close and 48 px) → in-game test 12.
- **Risks:**
  - Cycles baking in background Blender on CPU (time; cached);
  - DXT compression on small textures (measure after encoding);
  - painted style is subjective: the metrics bound it, the user signs it off from renders before the game.

**Q0 (2026-10-16): ears redesigned (awaiting the user's shape approval).** `build_additions` `ears`:
- **Shape:** 1.5 long, at most 0.36 wide (about 4:1); pinched base (0.26); widest at a third (0.36); narrowing rings close into a short blunt cap (a rounded tip).
- **Section:** lens-shaped, convex behind, the front cupped (centre 0.4 × the depth back from the edges), 0.12 thick at the base.
- **Placement:** on the sides of the crown (spread 0.85 × the head's half-width, 0.35 below its top), tilted 25° out and leaning 15° back (measured on type1: the tip 0.33 back and 0.6 out over 1.32 up), the opening turned 25° out.
- **Size:** 114 vertices, 220 triangles for both ears.
- **Deformation check:** clean on both bodies (`spear_shield`).
- **Renders:** `build/q0/ears_q0.png` (both bodies, bare and with the Greek helmet; front, side, rear-quarter); silhouettes `build/q0/ear_silhouettes.png` (schematic horse and cat outlines drawn from typical proportions, not traced; the old and new ear profiles).

**Q0 round 2 (2026-10-16, user feedback: in 3D round 1 read as rabbit ears or horns):**
- length 1.15; mid-ear width 0.42 (profile 0.30 / 0.39 / 0.42 / 0.38 / 0.30 / 0.20 / 0.12); rounded tip kept;
- outward tilt 13°; a gentle inward curve (`curve` 0.12: the tips bend towards the midline); lean back 15° kept;
- measured per ear, base to tip: up 1.09, out 0.11, back 0.24;
- deformation check clean on both bodies; renders `build/q0/ears_q0_round2.png` and `ear_silhouettes_round2.png`.
- **Requirement for Q2/Q5 (user):** the ears and the tuft must be **matte and hair-coloured, not glossy black, with a lighter inner ear**. The black gloss makes them read as horns.

**Q1 (2026-10-16): real UVs and texel density.**
- **Layout `atlas`** for dory, sarissa, xyston, xiphos, crest, horse_ears and greek_helmet:
  - `build_parts` records each face's zone in a face attribute (it survives triangulation), unwraps with Smart UV Project (66°, island margin 0.03), and picks a power-of-two size (64-512) for `TEXEL_DENSITY` = 32 texels per mesh unit;
  - it returns each triangle's zone and UVs; `textures.procedural.atlas_images` paints zone diffuse and spec (country masks, gloss) on that layout, edges bled 2 px against mip bleed;
  - cached alongside the mesh (`#atlas`); the hoplon keeps its disc layout (the blazon needs it).
- **Measured** (test-11 parts, `measure_quality.py`): dory 20.1 texels/unit at 512 px (the cap; base-game halberd 25.2), greek_helmet 34.3 at 256, hoplon 22.9 (unchanged), against 0 before. Detail is still 0 until Q2 paints them.
- **Additions on the body texture:** each uses only as much of the hair region as gives 32 texels/unit (the job measures the mapping's real density and scales once).
  - type1: tuft 32.0, ears 32.0; the tail 15.7 is capped (the region is too small);
  - african_generic_03: tail 3.6, tuft 9.2, ears 14.1 (its scalp region is 26×8 px).
  - Both are lifted by Q5's dedicated hair swatch.

**Q4 (2026-10-16): smooth normals.**
- `build_parts.smooth_normals`: every part shades smooth (`Mesh.shade_smooth`), with hard edges only where the surface turns sharper than 50° (`set_sharp_from_angle`) or where two colour zones meet (the `zone` face attribute → `sharp_edge`).
- io_pdx_mesh writes per-corner normals, so the hard edges survive export.
- The parts were flat-shaded before (each face lit on its own: the faceted helmet).
- **Thickness check:** Q0 ears 0.12 at the base, crest fin 0.28, helmet shell 0.08, hoplon 0.12; no single-sided sheets.
- **Rim bevels skipped:** smooth shading plus Q3's normal maps give the edge highlight without more triangles.
- Before/after: `build/q4/before_after.png`.

**Q2 (2026-10-16): painted base colour.**
- Each colour zone has a material class (`ZoneDef.material`: wood, bronze, iron, hair, leather, paint, plain).
- **Bake** (`build_parts.bake_maps`): after export, Cycles (CPU, 16 samples, 4 px margin) bakes four maps on the part's own unwrap (or the hoplon's disc) with temporary per-zone materials:
  - `ao` (ambient occlusion);
  - `curv` (pointiness: edges);
  - `up` (world Z of the normal: top light);
  - `pattern`: a 3D material pattern in object coordinates, seamless across UV seams: noise plus a 4× finer octave; wood adds a wave texture, bronze Voronoi hammering; iron and hair stretch along the length.
- The maps are cached with the mesh (`#maps`).
- **`textures/paint.paint`** composes per texel: zone colour × pattern × AO × top light, plus convex-edge wear and a deterministic per-texel brush grain, with per-material strengths (`STYLE`, tuned against the base game's measured style). Country zones stay neutral grey, shaded the same way.
- **Measured** (test-11 parts): detail dory 12.0, hoplon 10.3, helmet 9.5 (was 0; base game 11-22); shading dory 12.5, hoplon 11.1 (base game 4-26). The helmet's 51 counts its dark lining next to the bronze in one texture.
- **Noted:** the dory's long shaft islands pack into a narrow column of its 512 px atlas. The density (20.1) is in the base-game weapon range, so the wasted space is accepted for now.
- Textures: `build/q2/textures.png`.

**Q3 (2026-10-16): baked normal maps.**
- **Convention, measured:** the base game's normal maps are plain RGB (type1 mean 126 127 250) and **OpenGL-style, green up the image**. Over 122 rivet points on the type1 helmet, red rises to the right of each bump (+26.7) and green upwards (+30.7). That is Blender's default bake axes, and our bake images keep Blender's v-up as image-up, so no flip is needed.
- **Bake:** `bake_maps` adds a `normal` pass, tangent space on the part's UVs (MikkTSpace, as io_pdx_mesh exports `ta`). Each zone's shading normal is Bevel (radius 0.04, rounded edge highlights instead of bevel geometry) feeding Bump (the 3D material pattern, strength per material in `BUMP`, 0.35-1.4). `BuiltParts.images` uses the baked map.
- **Measured tilt:** dory 9.1°, hoplon 8.2°, helmet 6.0° (was 2.3° flat; base game 4-20°). Normal maps: `build/q3/normals.png`.

**Q5 (2026-10-16): gloss and the hair swatch.**
- **Part gloss** into the base game's 4-18: metal zones 13% → 8%, the hoplon's painted face 10% → 4%, the rest 3%. Measured spec alpha: dory 10, hoplon 10, helmet 13 (was 12-26).
- **Hair swatch** (`textures/hair.py`, `loadout._hair_swatch`): a derived body with additions gets derived copies of its base diffuse, normal and specular (base game only; same DDS format, size and mips as each base; `hr_<set>_body_<tier>_{diffuse,normal,specular}.dds` beside the derived .mesh; `derived_from` recorded).
  - **Where:** in the largest texture area no remaining base triangle uses (`derived_body.free_rect`, found from the mesh's own UVs, so a removed part's area is free too) at least 8 px wide and an eighth of the texture tall (type1 32×292 px; african_generic_03 32×144 of 256).
  - **What is painted:** warm brown horsehair (112 78 50) in strands along the length, a lighter inner-ear patch (168 128 104), strand relief in the normal map (green up), spec RGB 0 (no country mask) and a matte alpha of 6.
  - **Layout:** split along its length: tail 0-62%, tuft 64-78%, ears 80-92%, inner ears 93-100%. The ears' cupped-front vertices map to the inner patch.
  - **Material:** the derived .mesh's material names the derived textures (`retexture_material`). `check_derived(..., textures=)` allows exactly that change, and rule `derived_body` checks that the derived textures were generated and record their base. Undecodable base textures or too little room fall back to `HAIR_REGIONS`.
- **Measured:** type1 tail 32.0, tuft 32.0, ears 64.4 texels/unit (was 15.7 / 32 / 32); african_generic_03 tail 14.5, tuft 16.2, ears 31.0 (its own body texture: 16.5).
- **Renders:** `build/q5/renders/hair_swatch.png`: brown, strand-textured tail and tuft; brown ears with a lighter inner ear. They no longer read as horns.

**Q6 (2026-10-16): quality as a check.**
- `textures/quality.py` holds the measures (density, detail, shading, normal tilt, gloss; `scripts/plan11/measure_quality.py` uses the same definitions).
- **Rule `texture_quality`** warns when a generated part (its .mesh and the three textures its material names) falls outside `RANGES`: density 15-40, detail ≥ 8, shading ≥ 4 (no upper bound: a texture may hold dark lining next to bright metal), normal tilt 4-20°, gloss 2-20.
- **Test-11 parts now** (dory, hoplon, helmet, tiers 1-2): all in range. Detail 9.3-12.0, shading 11-51, normal 5.1-9.1°, gloss 10-13, density 20.1-34.3.

**Quality pass sign-off renders (2026-10-16; awaiting the user's sign-off before test 12):** `build/signoff/signoff_vs_vanilla.png`.
- **Rows:** the vanilla western tier-1 infantryman (type1 + halberd, `polearm_twohanded`) against ours on T1 (type1 - helmet + ears, tail, tuft; greek_helmet, dory, hoplon; `spear_shield`) and T2 (african_generic_03 + the same), each in walk (f14) and attack (f30).
- **Views:** side and rear-quarter close-ups, and map scale (48 px from above, front- and rear-quarter, also ×4).
- **Limits:** Workbench renders show diffuse and shape only. Normal maps, gloss and unit colours aren't shown (the hoplon face and the cloth stay grey here); those show in game.

**Order:** H1 check → (writer remove-triangles mode, or H2) → new shapes plus `check_deform` → `greek_helmet` → T1 textures (T2 if needed) → pre-checks → test 11.

**Horse test character: FROZEN as "pipeline proven" (user, 2026-10-16).** No more tuning of its looks; test 12 for it is skipped.
- **Proven in game or by checks:**
  - derived bodies (removal + skinned additions, every base block kept, verified in game);
  - the chain-weight tool and the automatic deformation check;
  - rigid parts and the Greek helmet;
  - real UVs, painted and baked textures, normal maps, matte gloss and the hair swatch;
  - the texture-quality check.
- **Finding:** procedural parts are good for **rigid** parts (weapons, shields, helmets, crests) and have hit their ceiling for **organic** shapes. The tail reads as a plug and the ear placement is off. Organic parts come in through the import path (R-I below), hand-made, CC0 or generated (TripoSG).

**Plan (PLANNED 2026-10-16, nothing built), in order:**

**R-D. Every character fully data-driven.** One character spec JSON, editable in the GUI, nothing character- or test-specific in code. Proven by a second original character from a new spec with no code changes.
- **`character.json` (schema 1)**, replacing today's spread (CLI flags + the unit-set form + loadout.json + recipe JSON):
  - `set`: tags, cultures, gfx, base_gfx, set_name, name, adjective, map colour, tech group, unit colours;
  - `textures`: the Phase 2 recipe, inline;
  - `loadout`: slots, bodies, `derived` per tier {add (kind + param overrides), remove}, accept_clipping, weapon_point;
  - `hair`: swatch colour, inner colour, gloss;
  - `test`: test tag, sprite techs, base mod, give_cores, stage dir; kept apart, so a real export never carries test settings.
  - Paths inside it (blazons, imported models) are relative to the spec file.
- **Tables moved from code into data:**
  - a **body library** (`library/bodies.json`, base-game facts only: names, removable parts as bone + UV box, hair regions), extendable by a user file;
  - an **addition library** (`library/additions.json`: shape builder, params, chain, exempt rings, behind joint);
  - the **material library** (paint `STYLE`, `BUMP`, gloss defaults);
  - **helmet styles** (`RIM_DROP`) as data the `greek_helmet` kind reads.
  - Code keeps only builders and checks. Validators read the same files.
- **GUI:** a Character page (new / open / save spec; every page edits a section of it); a **Derived body** editor per tier (removable parts of the chosen body, additions with their params, accept clipping); hair colours; the Test page reads `test`.
- **CLI:** `--spec character.json` for generate, test-mod and parts-preview. Today's flags stay as overrides.
- **Proof: an original Hellenistic hoplite**, built from a new spec only:
  - **body:** type1, helmet removed (its bare head is already bearded);
  - **head:** a Corinthian `greek_helmet` with a crest;
  - **cloth:** a linothorax recolour (Phase 2 recipe: off-white linen, painted panels);
  - **kit:** dory and hoplon.
  - Report every gap that needed code. **Known gap already:** a derived body can't take a texture recipe yet ("can't combine with a texture recipe's retextured body"). The fix is generic: the recipe retextures the derived body's derived textures, with the hair swatch on top when there are additions.
  - Acceptance: the hoplite builds and validates clean from its spec; renders; any code change is a generic fix, listed.

**R-I. Import part.** An external model (.glb, .obj, .fbx), hand-made, CC0 or generated, through the pipeline. Organic parts (tails, ears, hair) come in this way.
- **Rights first (CLAUDE.md rule 8):** each import records licence (original / CC0 / licensed + terms), author and source URL in the spec. The licence is written to INTEGRATION.md and the manifest. Imports are refused without it; never vanilla or DLC files.
- **Blender job `import_part`:**
  - import (built-in glTF, OBJ, FBX), join, apply transforms;
  - orientation from the spec (up and forward axes; the GUI previews it);
  - **auto-scale**: to a target size (e.g. a tail length) or fitted to a bone or body measure (head extents, back profile);
  - **placement**: at an anchor (bone + offset, or snapped to the body surface: tail root on the pelvis back, ears on the crown).
- **Two modes:**
  - **rigid:** a static part mesh attached to a bone (like procedural parts; loadout kind `imported`);
  - **skinned:** appended to a derived body. The chain-weight tool generalises from rings to a **length coordinate** (each vertex projected onto the part's root→tip axis), with the same chains, rules and deformation check.
- **Decimate to budget** (Blender Decimate, UVs kept): rigid ≤ 400 triangles, skinned additions ≤ 400 each, body total ≤ 4,500.
- **Textures:**
  - **when the model has its own:** they are baked into ours (diffuse DXT1; normal converted to RGB green-up, DirectX → flip; roughness → spec alpha gloss into 4-18; spec RGB 0 or zone masks);
  - **skinned parts:** their texture is re-baked into the derived body's free texture area (free_rect), so the body keeps one material;
  - **when the model has none:** Q2 paint from a material class in the spec.
- **Checks:** part_mesh, the derived_body rule (chain weights), the deformation check, the texture-quality check, budgets, and the licence recorded.
- **Tests:** a synthetic .obj made in the test (no third-party files), through the job (real Blender when present, else the fake runner).
- **Acceptance:** an original, hand-made test model goes through both modes clean, with renders.

**R-T. TripoSG (step 8) feeding R-I.**
- Local TripoSG (MIT) in its **own isolated environment** (never the app's .venv), run as a subprocess like Blender, on the user's GPU (RTX 3060, 12 GB). The user installs the model weights; the app only calls it. No Hunyuan3D; Tripo API stays opt-in later.
- **Input:** an original or licensed reference image (rule 8), one part per image (a tail, an ear, a crest).
- **Output:** .glb geometry → R-I with clean-up (keep the largest component, fill holes, remesh and decimate to budget) and orientation confirmed in the GUI.
- **Texture:** Q2 paint by default; projecting the reference image is experimental.
- **Acceptance:** one organic part (e.g. a tail) generated from an original reference image passes all R-I checks and reads better than the procedural one in renders.

**Order:** R-D (spec, data libraries, GUI, hoplite proof, gap report) → R-I → R-T. **Changed by the user (2026-10-16):** spec format and data libraries → the hoplite from a spec file via `--spec` → fix the gaps it finds (texture recipes on derived bodies included) → the GUI Character page on the settled format → R-I → R-T. Plus a converter from the old files and flags.

**R-D 1 (2026-10-16): data libraries and the spec format.**
- **Data libraries** (`eu4unitgen/library/{bodies,additions,materials}.json`, read by `library.data.library`):
  - removable parts and hair regions per base body;
  - the tail, tuft and ears (shape, params, chain, exempt rings, behind joint);
  - paint styles, relief, the hair swatch, helmet styles and slot.
  - Code keeps only builders and checks; the part job receives the values it needs in its entry.
  - A rebuild of the test-11 set is byte-identical (57 files).
- **`core/character.py` `CharacterSpec`** (schema 1, strict, unknown keys refused): sections `set`, `textures` (recipe, inline), `loadout` (inline), `hair` (swatch colours → `Loadout.hair` → the hair swatch), `export` (prefix, version, pack order), `test` (sprite techs, base mod/name, give cores; only the test mod reads it). Relative paths resolve against the spec's folder.
- **CLI:**
  - `--spec character.json` on generate, test-mod and parts-preview; the spec's export and test sections give defaults, and explicit flags win;
  - `to-spec` converts the old flags plus `--loadout`/`--texture-recipe` (and test options) into a spec, embedding both files and rebasing their relative paths (recipe images and masks, `mask:` regions, blazons) onto the spec's folder.

**Phases and acceptance (after R0):**
- R1: character recipe + reference analysis + mapping UI. Accept when a user-confirmed mapping produces a loadout-equivalent recipe.
- R2: derived bodies (bareheaded, merged skinned additions). Accept on an in-game test with no error.log lines and no visible artefacts in idle/walk/attack.
- R3: new rigid kinds (ears, kopis, guards, helmet-with-openings). Accept in game like tests 7-8.
- R4: skinned clothing (chiton, cuirass) with copied weights. Accept on the R0 metrics + in game.
- R5: step-8 AI geometry for unusual rigid parts. Accept when decimated output ≤ budget and passes `part_mesh`.
- R6 (only if R0 fails and the user accepts the cost): extra bones + re-authored animations.

**Open questions:**
1. ~~Derived body meshes~~: allowed (2026-10-16).
2. Reference images: none yet; the user supplies original or licensed ones later. Until then the written test character is used.
3. Is a bareheaded base-game body available (search `body_candidates` for a head without a hat)?
4. Does io_pdx_mesh anim playback match the game?
5. Can attached entities be skinned to the parent skeleton? (UNVERIFIED; would avoid derived bodies.)
6. Cavalry: the same character as a rider needs the `cavalry_rider_*` anims in R0's successor.

### Track F: Prompt front end (PLANNED 2026-10-17; mockups approved with changes)

The app opens on a prompt screen like the AI app builders (one box, example chips, one Generate button). Everything built so far stays, unchanged, behind an "Advanced editor" link; both edit the same character spec, so they stay in sync and share undo.

**Screens**
- **Home:**
  - "Describe your soldiers…" box. While you type, suggestion chips complete known words, and an "Understood" strip shows what was matched. Each chip has × to remove it, and clicking a chip offers the other choices of its kind.
  - "Not understood: …" lists the leftover words. Each has "add this word as…", which saves a custom synonym to the user's vocabulary file.
  - "Upload reference image" (CLAUDE.md rule 8: original or licensed). It runs the F5 palette extraction and adds its suggestions as chips.
  - "For:" nation picker by name: vanilla localisation plus the mod's own, including mod-only tags.
  - "Tiers:" "Same look on all tiers" or "Gets heavier with tech" (the template's per-tier steps).
  - Template chips: Hoplite, Phalangite, Companion cavalry, Peltast. One Generate button.
- **Result:**
  - Gallery of infantry, cavalry and artillery × tiers 1-4 as 3D previews, rendered one by one in the background and cached. Clicking a tile selects that tier.
  - The Understood strip again, still editable.
  - Unit names: in-game names per unit type (e.g. Pezhetairoi, Hetairoi), written to localisation.
  - Right panel, two tabs:
    - Parts: picture cards for helmet, weapon, shield and body look, each with "Change ▾" and "Apply to: all tiers / this tier only".
    - Colours: the three unit colours, material swatches (tint the finishes), shield emblem (a built-in original shape, your own PNG, or none).
  - Bottom: "Test in game" and "Export". Validation runs silently and only shows problems; Export is off while there are errors.
  - Export keeps rule 5: one short confirmation ("will write N files to …", list expandable), never overwrites files the tool didn't write.
  - Small "Advanced editor" link.
- **First launch:** EU4 install, owned DLC (U2) and Blender detected and shown as a status line.

**Interpretation (offline only).** `core/prompt.py` behind an `Interpreter` interface (text + optional reference palette → character spec, understood items, leftover words), so another interpreter could be added later. Only the keyword interpreter is built; no AI interpreter, no API keys, no local LLM (decided 2026-10-17).
- The vocabulary is data: `library/vocabulary.json`. Each concept (unit type, helmet style, weapon, shield, crest, finish per body zone, colour) has a canonical value and its synonyms, for example:
  - pike, sarissa, long spear → sarissa;
  - crimson, red, scarlet → red.
  - Multi-word phrases match before single words.
- User vocabulary: `vocabulary.json` in the user's config folder (`%APPDATA%\eu4unitgen` on Windows, `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/eu4unitgen` or `~/.config/eu4unitgen` on Linux), merged over the library. Never in the repo.
- Output goes through `CharacterSpec.from_json`, the same checks as a loaded file.

**Unit names.** The set section gains `unit_names: {infantry, cavalry, artillery}`. Tiers get " I"-" IV" after the name, as the generated default names already do ("Athenian Infantry I"), so the four tiers stay distinct in the unit list. The names go into the existing localisation file (`core/nation.py` builds the names, `loc.yml.j2` writes them). The validator rule `unit_names`: localisation text without a quote or line break (render.py already refuses those), and no two units sharing a name.

**Tier progression.** A template holds one look per tier: the "Gets heavier with tech" steps, for example tier 1 linen and a simple helmet, tier 4 bronze cuirass and a full helmet. "Same look on all tiers" copies the chosen tier's look to all four. "Apply to: this tier only" changes one tier's loadout slot and recipe slot; "all tiers" changes all four.

**Steps** (one commit each, with tests; a short report after each):
- F1: this plan.
- F2: Blender detection (`BLENDER_PATH`, Windows registry and Program Files, `blender` on PATH on Linux) and the first-launch status line. Done: `core.install.find_blender` reads the uninstall entries the Windows installer writes per version (DisplayVersion, InstallLocation; checked on the user's 4.2.23 and 5.2.2), then Program Files, Steam's Blender, and on Linux PATH, /opt, ~ and snap. The pinned series (4.2) wins; any other comes with a warning. Auto-setup fills the Blender box only when it is empty or names a missing file.
- F3: starter templates with per-tier steps (U8). Done: `library/templates.json` (hoplite, phalangite, companion cavalry, peltast) holds looks; `core/look.py` is the look model the front end edits (per unit type a weapon, shield and helmet or crest; per area a finish; steps; this-tier edits; colours; crest colour; emblem; names) and `compile_look` merges it into a spec: every tier on the type1 body, its own helmet removed under a Greek one, crew slots so gun crews drop the shield, finishes per tier slot. It replaces only the parts and `area:` finishes a look controls; painted masks and other ops and parts stay. Riders and crews get crests, not Greek helmets (derived bodies are infantry-only). The compiled specs pass `CharacterSpec.from_json`; in-game and render checks come in F9 and F12.
- F4: vocabulary, synonyms, keyword interpreter, suggestions, leftovers, user vocabulary. Done: `library/vocabulary.json` (templates, scope words, weapons, shields, helmets, crests, materials, garments, colours, "no", tier phrases, stopwords) and `core/prompt.py`: `Vocabulary` (library merged with the user's `vocabulary.json`; `add_synonym`, `suggest`: completions, and after a material or colour the garments or parts it can describe), `Interpreter` (interface) and `KeywordInterpreter` (clause by clause: scope words, adjectives waiting for their noun, negation, notes for things the parts can't do, leftovers). `Interpretation.items` are the chips (`remove`, `swap`) and `.look()` builds the look from them on a template (default hoplite). Armour means the chest (bronze); a cuirass or tunic is the torso (linen).
- F5: reference image palette per horizontal band (U7). Done: `core/reference.py` drops the background (the border colour or transparent pixels) and reads the middle 40% of the figure's width per band (head 0-20%, torso 20-55%, legs 55-100%; `library/vocabulary.json` "reference"). Each band's main colour becomes a finish by hue, saturation and value (linen, iron, skin, bronze, leather, else a tinted colour), plus a crest colour from a saturated head band and the main unit colour; they come back as chips. Limit: dark, shaded pictures read too dark (the hoplite render's linen reads as iron and its skin as leather), so these stay suggestions to accept or remove.
- F6: nation list (vanilla + mod localisation, mod-only tags). Done: `library.tags.country_names` (tags from common/country_tags, names from every `*_l_english.yml` under localisation/, `replace/` last; the mod folder's tags and names win) and `Session.country_list()` sorted by name. On the user's install: 974 vanilla tags, all named; with Victoria's Secret 11 mod-only tags (MAC "Macedonia" among them).
- F7: unit names in the spec, localisation and validator. Done: set section `unit_names` → `SetSpec.unit_names` → `Nation.unit_names` (checked: unit types only, no quotes or line breaks, at most 60 characters) → `default_units` ("Pezhetairoi I"-"IV"; types without a name keep "<Adjective> Infantry I"); `compile_look` writes the look's names; validator rule `unit_names` (no two units share a name; each name is in the localisation file as given).
- F8: Home screen; Advanced editor behind a link. **Stop: real screenshots.** Theme (2026-10-17, after the user saw invisible controls in Windows dark mode): `gui/theme.py`, Fusion plus a full palette from light or dark tokens (Settings → Theme: follow Windows, light, dark); every Home stylesheet colour comes from the tokens; app-wide popup, menu and tooltip colours; `tests/test_theme.py` renders Home under a simulated dark Windows palette in each theme and checks every text widget at 4.5:1 or better (WCAG). Image chips say "torso: dusty rose" with a swatch (`core.reference.colour_name`, nearest in Lab); chip arrows are part of the text; templates are buttons; a missing country outlines For: and turns Generate off with a hint. Done: `gui/home_page.py`; the window opens on it, the old pages are behind "Advanced editor" (with "← Simple view" back). The text is the one source of truth: chips (x, click to swap) rewrite it through `Interpretation.text_without` / `text_swapped` (items keep the character spans of their words), and leftovers offer "Add … as…" (saved to the user vocabulary) or "Take it out". The word being typed isn't flagged as a leftover. Image chips are kept apart from the text. Generate compiles the look for the picked country (reading the body's skin tone in the background) and, until F10, shows it in the Advanced editor. Tests point the config folder at a temp dir (conftest).
- F9: gallery renderer (spike first: cavalry rider on horse, artillery crew; time the 12 renders).
  - Done:
    - **What it renders:** `export/gallery.py` reads each unit's entity tree from a `World` (the generated set over the mod and the game, as the validator sees it), so the gallery shows the entities the game will get. `entity_tree` gives each entity's pdxmesh, its idle state's `.anim` and its attaches by bone (every attach node is a bone: `Saddle_Node`, `infantry_node`, `cannon_node`, hands, head). `stage` writes each mesh, with its diffuse tinted by the unit colours, into its own temporary folder.
    - **The job:** `blender/jobs/render_unit.py` poses every rig at frame 1 and puts each child where its parent's bone is (posed matrix times the bone-rest-to-bind offset, as test 11's posed renders did). All 12 units render in one Blender run (`Session.gallery`, which reuses a validation result).
    - **Attached scale:** an attached entity keeps its own pdxmesh scale, not times its parent's. Parts at 0.5 sat right in in-game tests 7-8, and a 0.5 rider on a 0.48 horse matches vanilla; multiplied, helmets shrank into the head.
    - **io_pdx_mesh rig lookup:** it reuses any armature that shares a bone name with what it imports, so the job scopes that lookup (a mesh import always builds its own rig; an animation goes to the rig named).
    - **Hidden helpers:** a mesh whose diffuse is missing or fully transparent is hidden (artillery_base is one triangle with `nospec.dds`: 4x4, alpha 0).
    - **Timing on the user's machine (hoplite template, ATH):** validation 27 s (it builds the parts); 12 tiles 53 s (infantry about 1.5 s, artillery 2.2 s, cavalry 9 s).
    - **The gallery shows two truths about generated sets:**

### Track C: Close to a reference (PLANNED 2026-10-18, nothing built)
Goal: from a reference image, a unit that is recognisably close to it, not only in its colours. Test reference (user's, rule 8 declared by the user): a cataphract: a helmet with a mail veil and a tall red plume, silver scale or lamellar armour, a red cloak with gold trim, an oval red shield with a gold cross, a kontarion, a curved sword, a fully armoured horse with barding and a chanfron.

**Why the first try gave a crested "Spartan" rider:**
- `core/reference.py` (F5) reads colour only, in head/torso/legs bands of a standing front view. On a rider on a horse the "legs" band is mostly horse, so colours land on the wrong areas. It never detects parts.
- Riders are the weakest path: they wear the untextured base body (F12 open 1). Derived bodies are infantry-only, so riders get crests, never a `greek_helmet`. `hoplon` is limited to infantry and crew. A cataphract is all rider and horse, so none of the armour, helmet or shield work reaches it today.
- The vocabulary has no cataphract words or template, so it fell back to a hoplite/companion look.

**Items, by value vs effort** (S/M/L). Only C11 needs TripoSG, and C11 is skipped (user, 2026-10-18).
- **C-1. Faster edits** (first, before C0; F12 open 2). An edit re-checks the set but re-renders only the tiles whose unit changed (unit types and tiers); the old picture stays, dimmed, until its new one arrives.
  - **Done (2026-10-18).** `export.gallery.fingerprint` hashes what a tile shows: each mesh of the unit's entity tree, the textures its shapes name, the idle animation, scales, attach bones and the preview unit colours. `Session.gallery_prints` runs after each check (0.2-0.3 s for 12 units); the Result screen re-renders only units whose print changed or that have no picture, one Blender run per unit type that has any. Tiles being redone keep their picture at 35% opacity; unchanged ones are undimmed when the check ends. A name edit renders nothing; a unit colour re-renders all 12.
  - **The set is still checked whole:** the validator's rules span the set (pack orders, the graph), so rule 4 stays intact. Parts are cached, so only changed parts go to Blender.
  - **Measured on the user's install** (F12's ATH phalangite army, real Blender): first generate 144 s (validate 50 s); a tier-3-only helmet edit 23.6 s (validate 19 s including the helmet build, 1 tile rendered), was ~69 s for 12 tiles.
- **C0. Riders get the full treatment** (blocks the rest; S-M). The retextured rider pdxmesh carries the `horse_*` ids (§7.2), so recipes and finishes reach the rider; helmets and shields on rider slots. In-game test.
  - **Finding (2026-10-18):** in the generated set the rider already wears the retextured infantry pdxmesh, with all 11 animation ids and its meshsettings (checked on F12's ATH army: `ATH_CAVALRY_1_rider` → `hr_ath_infantry_1_mesh`). The untextured rider was a **gallery bug**: `stage` took texture names from the `.mesh` and ignored the pdxmesh's meshsettings, so every retextured mesh (rider, horse, cannon) previewed with vanilla textures. F9's and F12's "the retextured body lacks `horse_*` ids" was wrong. Split into:
    - **C0a. Done:** the gallery honours meshsettings (`export.gallery.shape_textures`: matched by shape name, else index; names resolve in the mesh's folder as `validate.graph.meshsettings_texture_path`; the shader override too), staged under the name the mesh's material loads; fingerprints hash the same textures. Real install: the tier-1 rider renders with the infantry's crimson tunic, bare arms and bronze greaves (it keeps the vanilla type1 helmet; the crest sits on it).
    - **C0b. Done.** An optional `<tier>/rider` texture-recipe slot (`MESH_ROLES` gains `rider`), so a rider's look can differ from the infantry's; without it the rider shares the infantry's, as before. Its base is the tier's infantry body (the loadout's chosen body, else the culture's own; §7.2), so `hr_<set>_rider_<n>_mesh` has the same `.mesh` and every animation id, `horse_*` included (rule: state ids; `mesh_settings`). The Textures and Character pages offer the role. The Result screen's finishes still dress the infantry only; rider finishes come with the cataphract template (C7).
    - **C0c. Done (code).** Shields on riders: `hoplon` takes every loadout role; the look, the keyword interpreter ("cavalry with shields") and the Result screen's Shield card allow it for cavalry. **No base-game precedent:** no base-game rider entity attaches anything to `Left_hand_node` (checked in every `gfx/entities/*.asset` of 1.37.5); the rider's left hand holds the reins. Rule `loadout`: a rider with a shield gets an INFO ("UNVERIFIED in game"), not the infantry's `spear_shield` warning (riders play the `cavalry_rider_*` set). Gallery: the shield sits on the rein hand in front of the chest, slightly over the horse's neck. The test-mod checklist adds per tier a rider-shield line (`nRS`), a rider-textures line (`nTR`: own slot, or shared with the infantry) and the side-view section.
    - **In-game test 14 — STAGED (2026-10-18):** HDA / `c0_test`, spec `build/c0/c0.json`, stage `build/c0/stage`, checklist `build/c0/checklist.txt`; validator 0 errors, 1 warning (the tier-3 shield's flat fixed-red face: texture detail). Unit colours blue 40 60 160 / yellow 220 190 40 / off-white 230 225 210; test sprite techs 2 3 4.
      - T1: infantry red torso + dory + hoplon (control); rider **own slot**: silver torso (175 178 185, metal gloss), xyston, full-size hoplon (shared with the infantry), red crest.
      - T2: infantry red torso; rider **own slot**: green torso, xyston, small hoplon (radius 2.6).
      - T3: infantry yellow torso, **no rider slot** (the rider shares it); rider xiphos + hoplon with a fixed red face.
      - T4: plain western (control).
      - Decides: C0b (a rider texture slot in game; the rider still animates on the horse) and C0c (a shield on the rein hand: where it sits, clipping with the horse's neck or the rider's thigh, through idle, walk, attack, retreat).
- **C1. Scale, lamellar and mail finishes** (high; S-M). A tiled procedural diffuse plus normal-map relief, written into an area as a new recipe op; finishes in `materials.json`. Infantry and (after C0) riders.
- **C2. Oval shield and cross emblem** (high: the shield reads best at 48 px; S). An aspect param and a flatter dome on the hoplon builder; a cross in `core/emblems.py`. **UNVERIFIED:** a `Left_hand_node` shield with the `cavalry_rider_*` animations. Check vanilla entities for a rider with a left-hand attach, then test in game.
- **C3. Reference tagger, first half** (high; M). The user draws boxes on the image (helmet, body armour, shield, weapon, horse, cloak, plume), picks the closest part per box or "none exists", and colours are sampled per box (background drop and palette as in F5). Unmatched boxes become a "missing parts" list. This alone fixes the band problem above.
- **C4. Horse barding as texture** (high: the horse is half the unit; M). Horse areas from bone groups of the 30-bone horse skeleton (neck: `HorseNeck*`; body: `HorseSpine*`, `Root`; legs: `HorseFront*`, `HorseBack*`; head: `HorseHead`) in `bodies.json`, then the scale finish and a colour on the `horse` recipe slot. Caveat: the horse diffuse is 256², so scales will be coarse; the normal map is 512².
- **C5. Helmet with a mail aventail or face veil and a tall plume** (high; M). A new helmet style: a dome, a mail curtain below the rim with an eye slit (mail finish), the plume as a crest variant **in the same mesh** (one attached part per bone). On riders: a shell that slightly encloses type1's own helmet instead of a removal, which avoids untested derived rider bodies (Track R open question 6).
- **C6. Chanfron** (medium; S-M). A rigid part on `HorseHead`, fitted from the horse mesh (fitting generalised from the soldier's head to any bone). **UNVERIFIED:** a horse attach other than `Saddle_Node`; in-game test.
- **C7. Cataphract template and vocabulary** (medium: makes typed prompts work; S). Cataphract/kataphraktos, lamellar, scale, mail veil, kontarion, barding, chanfron, cloak, plume. The kontarion is the xyston under another name.
- **C8. Curved sword** (low-medium: at 48 px curved vs straight barely shows; S). A blade-curve param on the xiphos builder. With a lance in the right hand the sword goes in a scabbard on `hip` (**UNVERIFIED**; `head` worked) or is left out.
- **C9. Cape: painted only** (user, 2026-10-18). No cloth bones and no physics (Track R), so no flowing cloak without extra bones and re-made animations (R6, rejected). A red cloak with gold trim painted on the rider's shoulder and back areas (medium; S); at map scale it reads about as well as geometry. **Not planned:** a short rigid mantle (a stiff plate that clips into the horse and saddle when seated) and a cape skinned into a derived rider body.
- **C10. Tagger, second half** (low; M). A pattern hint (scale vs mail vs plain) from a box's pixels. Unreliable, so only a hint; the user picks from the crop.
- **C11. TripoSG crops: skipped for this look** (user, 2026-10-18; low value for this reference; L). Needs R-I and R-T, neither built. The crops are low-resolution, partly hidden and from one angle; the output must come down to 300-400 triangles and be repainted, so at 48 px it is no better than a procedural part. Crops are reference images too (rule 8). Use TripoSG for organic or ornate parts the builders can't make, not for this look.

- **C12. Auto-tag** (after C3; optional, **off by default**). A local vision model (Ollama with a vision-capable model that fits the RTX 3060's 12 GB; offline, no API key, run as a separate local process, never inside the app) looks at the reference and proposes C3's boxes and labels: helmet type, plume, shield shape and emblem, armour pattern, weapon, horse armour, plus the closest parts and colours, all mapped to `vocabulary.json`. The user confirms or corrects each proposal before anything is built; whatever has no matching part goes on the "missing parts" list. **Before building:** research which current local vision models fit 12 GB (and box/grounding ability, licence) and recommend one. Exception to Track F's "no local LLM" (2026-10-17), decided by the user for the tagger only.

**Order** (small steps, one commit each; stop for the user at each in-game test):
1. C-1 faster edits.
2. C0 → in-game test.
3. C2, C7, C1 → in-game test: a cataphract rider with a scale torso, an oval shield and a xyston.
4. C3, then C12 (model research and recommendation first).
5. C5 → C4 → C6 → in-game test (the `HorseHead` attach, the helmet shell on a rider).
6. C9 painted cape, C8.

**Acceptance:** the reference beside the gallery's cavalry tile, close up and at 48 px, with one line per tagger box: matched, approximated or missing. "Recognisably close" = the shield's shape and colour, the helmet outline with the plume, the armoured horse and the red-and-silver colours all read at 48 px.

### Test 15: a new armoured horse from a reference (Track C extension, user 2026-10-18)
Goal: a **genuinely new** horse mesh from the reference, working in game on the vanilla horse skeleton and animations. Reusing or recolouring the vanilla horse mesh doesn't count. This overrides C11's "TripoSG skipped for this look" (user, 2026-10-18).
- **Reference:** `Byzantine Cataphract Armor Reference Sheet.png` (1536×1024; a composite illustration: a 3/4-front rider on an armoured horse, a rear view, close-ups). **Rights (user):** a reference for a historical look; never bundled in exports or the repo. Kept in `assets_in/horse_ref/` (git-ignored, with every crop, AI output and render made from it).
- **Steps** (STOP for the user after 7 with the crop, the raw renders, the cleaned meshes and the worst deformation frames of both horses; step 10, the test mod, only after the user's OK):
  1. TripoSG install. 2. Crop + background removal. 3. Generate, render raw. 4. Blender clean-up (largest piece, fill holes, cut the rider above the saddle line, keep the barding, decimate to ≤ 3,000 triangles, new UVs). 5. A crude procedural control horse (not shipped) to tell pipeline problems from AI problems. 6. Fit to the vanilla horse skeleton (hooves, head, tail, `Saddle_Node` height; report saddle mismatch). 7. Weights by nearest-surface transfer from the vanilla horse + joint smoothing; deformation check on every horse animation the unit plays; worst frames rendered. 8. Chanfron merged into the mesh, 100% `HorseHead`; textures by our pipeline (silver scale, red/gold cloth). 9. Writer: "fresh mesh with vanilla skeleton" (skeleton, locators and material from the vanilla horse; new geometry and weights); re-import checks. 10. Test mod: HDA cavalry tier 1 with the new horse and a vanilla rider.
  - The control horse goes through 6-9 first, then the TripoSG horse.
- **1. TripoSG install — done (2026-10-18), all on D: (`D:\eu4unitgen_ai\`, ~13 GB), nothing in the repo or the app's .venv:**
  - Python 3.10.19 from uv 0.9.5 (standalone builds; `tools\uv`, `python\`): the user's download was the 3.10.22 *source* tarball, and 3.10 has no Windows installer after 3.10.11.
  - Environment `triposg_env` (uv venv): torch 2.14.1+cu126 (sees the RTX 3060, driver 617.42), diffusers 0.41, transformers 5.20, numpy 2.2.6, scikit-image, trimesh, pymeshlab, rembg.
  - TripoSG source `TripoSG\` (git, commit fc5c409, MIT); weights `weights\TripoSG` (VAST-AI/TripoSG, 7.95 GB).
  - **Deviations from the README, deliberate:** `diso` isn't installed (source-only on PyPI; needs the CUDA toolkit to build, which the machine doesn't have): the job stubs its import and runs `use_flash_decoder=False` (scikit-image marching cubes). numpy isn't pinned to 1.22.3 (incompatible with current torch, OpenCV, scikit-image); TripoSG imported and ran on numpy 2.2.6. RMBG-1.4 (non-commercial licence) isn't used: background removal is rembg's BiRefNet-general (MIT), and TripoSG uses the input's alpha as it is.
  - App side: `ai/triposg.py` `TripoSGRunner` (paths from `EU4UNITGEN_TRIPOSG_PYTHON`/`_REPO`/`_WEIGHTS`), job `ai/jobs/triposg_generate.py` (runs only in that environment, `python -I`). Blender job `render_model.py`: front/side/3-4 renders of any model file.
- **2. Crop — done:** the left horse with its rider, sheet px 30-662 × 200-965 (the rider's head is cut off: he is removed in step 4), BiRefNet cut-out → `assets_in/horse_ref/horse_ref_crop.png` (632×765 RGBA). Verified by eye: horse, barding, chanfron, reins and rider kept, background gone.
- **3. Generate — done:** seed 42, 50 steps, guidance 7, hierarchical octree depth 9: 177 s, peak VRAM 5.35 GB, **1,653,512 triangles, 125 pieces, not watertight**. Renders `assets_in/horse_ref/renders/sheet_raw.png`. Verified by eye: a recognisable horse with barding, headless rider, shield, reins. **Findings:** the barding is smooth drapery (its scales are texture detail, not geometry); **the horse stands mid-stride with the near foreleg lifted** (the reference's pose), unlike the vanilla bind pose: steps 6-7 must handle it (straighten the leg, or accept and measure the deformation).

- **Vanilla horse facts (1.37.5, `cavalry_horse.mesh`, tiers 1-2; scale 0.48):** 1,333 vertices, 1,874 triangles, one shape, PdxMeshColor, no locators; skin `bones = 3` (3 influences, 4 slots per vertex). PDX space: Y up, the head towards -Z, the left side at +X. Bind positions: `HorseHead` (0.20, 13.89, -9.21), `HorseNeckBase` (0, 12.03, -4.68), `Saddle_Node` (0.00, 12.57, -0.15), front heels (±1.19, 1.16, -4.60), back heels (~±1.0, 1.35, 4.4), tail end (0, 3.96, 6.71). Its back surface at the saddle is y 11.93: `Saddle_Node` sits 0.64 above it. Plays `cavalry_horse_{idle,moving,attack}.anim` (retreat = moving).
- **Deformation baseline (vanilla horse, check_mesh_deform; stretch = edge length / rest):** idle p05-p95 0.89-1.20, max 3.93, 13 flips; walk 0.91-1.14, max 3.17, 8 flips; attack 0.76-1.26, max 3.54, 32 flips. The vanilla horse deforms hard at the tail and hips: judge new horses against this, not against "no stretch".
- **9. Writer — done (core):** `core/fresh_mesh.py` `write_fresh` (the base mesh's header, names, skeleton, locators, skin influence count and material kept; p, n, ta from the UVs, u0, tri, skin, aabb replaced; winding follows the base) and `check_fresh` (re-import checks: skeleton byte-identical, locators, array lengths, skin ≤ the base's influences from the skeleton summing to 1, aabb, no boundingsphere). Both horses pass. **UNVERIFIED in game.**
- **Jobs:** `fresh_horse.py` (steps 4-7) and `check_mesh_deform.py` (whole-mesh check: stretch, flips against each triangle's dominant bone, worst frame and its bones, stretch-coloured worst-frame renders).
- **Pipeline findings (verified on the control):** collapse decimation leaves long slivers that span several bones and stretch 10×: the reduction is QuadriFlow (even quads) then a collapse trim. QuadriFlow silently does nothing on non-manifold input (it reports FINISHED): the job records `quadriflow_ran`, and a voxel remesh after the rider cut gives it a closed manifold solid. `vertex_group_smooth` won't run in background Blender: smoothing, the 3-bone limit and normalising are done in the job.
- **5. Control horse — done:** capsules on the vanilla bones, voxel 0.22, 2,710 triangles. Deformation ≈ vanilla: idle 0.86-1.21 max 4.56, 12 flips; walk 0.91-1.11 max 2.16, 2 flips; attack 0.80-1.17 max 3.51, 9 flips. **Steps 6, 7 and 9 proven on the control.**
- **4/6. TripoSG horse clean-up and fit — done, with limits:** box fit (nose-to-tail ×10.87), then a landmark fit (measured side profiles: the length lines up, but the generated horse stands lower): height ×1.065 to the croup, head and neck turned up 15.6° about the neck base, each leg swung about its top joint towards the vanilla heel. Rider cut above y 12.2 for z -3.8..5.0, voxel 0.3, QuadriFlow 2,800, collapse trim: **2,800 triangles, 2,615 vertices, one piece.** Saddle: our back at the saddle is 12.19 against vanilla 11.93, so the rider sits ~0.27 lower into the back than on vanilla (assumed acceptable; check in game). **Limits:** the skirts are shorter and fused (thin sheets don't survive a 2,800-triangle budget); the rider's lower leg is left as a lump on the near flank (it hangs below the cut); the lifted near foreleg stays bent at the knee (a swing about the shoulder can't straighten a knee); the head stays tucked.
- **7. TripoSG horse deformation — about twice vanilla's, not acceptable yet:** idle p05-p95 0.76-2.12 max 10.97, 163 flips; walk 0.80-1.51 max 6.44, 45 flips; attack 0.65-1.85 max 7.68, 108 flips. Worst frames (`assets_in/horse_ref/renders/sheet_triposg_deform.png`): the rear caparison, fused with the tail and hanging to the ground, takes tail weights and swings like a flap; the head and neck stretch in the attack (the generated head doesn't overlay the vanilla head, so nearest-surface weights mix head and neck badly). **Tried and dropped:** off-surface vertices weighted to body bones only (skirts ride the body) made it worse (max 19.1): a skirt over swinging legs tears wherever body-weighted skirt meets leg-weighted leg.
- **8. Chanfron and textures — not started:** held until the geometry and weights settle (both would be redone).

- **Round 2 (user, 2026-10-18): A-C, geometry fixes, step 8, then both horses in one test mod. Done up to step 8; the mod waits for the user's OK on the renders.**
  - **C, reshape onto the vanilla rest pose (`_horse_shape.reshape`):** height scaled to the croup (×1.065), then a 3D biharmonic spline from ~100 landmark pairs: each leg's centreline (tracked up from the hoof inside its own cross-section) onto the vanilla leg chain by distance from the ground (bent and striding legs come out straight at their own length); the head (same feature finder on both meshes: principal axis, poll region, neck centreline) turned ~20° and moved onto vanilla's, the neck by fraction; torso anchors fixed. Overlay `assets_in/horse_ref/renders/overlay_final.png`: legs, head, neck, back and croup on the vanilla horse.
  - **Clean-up order that works (each lesson verified by stage dumps):** a fine voxel solid (0.12) first, so the mesh is closed; then the edits; then a coarse voxel solid (0.3). A voxel remesh of a mesh with holes leaks and the body vanishes; filling large irregular holes (`holes_fill`) builds giant sheets; flattening the rider folds surfaces onto each other. What works on the closed solid: **boolean cuts** (EXACT, self-intersection on: the cutters are overlapping capsules). Rider: a box above the saddle line between z -3.8 and 5.0. Skirts: everything below the hem (y 5.2, above the knees 4.3 and hocks 4.4) except capsules of 0.65 round the vanilla leg chains and the tail (0.9 left a web between the hind legs). The rider's leg lump: pushed in to the other flank's width (mirror test, z -2.5..2, y 4.5..12). The caparison pushed 0.35 off the tail.
  - **Saddle:** measured by raycast on the centre line, vanilla's seat is **12.36** (vertex samples gave 11.93: they missed the saddle's top faces); `Saddle_Node` (12.566, never moved) sits 0.20 above it. Ours after a smooth vertical shift of the back: 12.348 (0.015 under; tolerance 0.05). A shift over 1.0 is refused (the first raycast-less version ran away into a tower).
  - **Not fixed:** the tail. TripoSG's tail hung inside the rear caparison and merged into the rump, so the horse has almost no tail; the tail chain weights 6 vertices. A small stub under the belly on the centre line stays (most likely the horse's sheath, modelled by TripoSG, not the rider).
  - **A, weights (`_horse_shape.chain_weights`, `clean_surface_rows`):** head/neck, each leg and the tail by inverse distance⁴ to their bone segments (each bone owns the segment to its child; heel to ground, head to muzzle, tail end extended), each region including its spine parent along the segment to its first joint; the torso keeps nearest-surface transfer, cleaned of tail and rein (`Rem`) weights (our rump sits where vanilla's tail is) and of a leg's lower bones farther than 1.0 from that leg (skirts don't swing with the legs); 12 smoothing passes. Smoothing was the largest single gain (4 -> 8 -> 12 passes).
  - **Deformation, final TripoSG horse (2,744 triangles incl. the chanfron) vs vanilla and the control** (p05-p95 stretch, max, flips, edges outside 0.85-1.2):
    - idle: 0.83-1.25, 4.26, 16, 10.6% | vanilla 0.89-1.20, 3.93, 13, 6.2% | control 0.86-1.21, 4.56, 12, 7.6%
    - walk: 0.85-1.20, 4.72, 15, 7.5% | vanilla 0.91-1.14, 3.17, 8, 4.1% | control 0.91-1.11, 2.16, 2, 3.7%
    - attack: 0.76-1.22, 4.98, 40, 9.6% | vanilla 0.76-1.26, 3.54, 32, 10.7% | control 0.80-1.17, 3.51, 9, 7.0%
    - Round 1 (v30a) for comparison: idle 0.76-2.12, 10.97, 163; walk 0.80-1.51, 6.44, 45; attack 0.65-1.85, 7.68, 108. Sheet `renders/sheet_before_after.png`. Remaining red: shoulders, hips and inner hind legs (where vanilla is red too, less so). Flips are ~1.2-1.9× vanilla's.
  - **Step 8 (`add_chanfron`, `_horse_paint`):** chanfron = the forehead and nose faces copied, lifted 0.06, 0.04 thick, merged (part = 1), 144 triangles, all 74 vertices 100% `HorseHead` (rigid with the head). Textures painted per texel from its 3D point (patterns run across UV islands): silver scales (rows on the plane each face looks along least) on the neck and front body; red caparison and saddle with a gold cross on each rear flank; gold trim at the hem and the scale/cloth seam; chanfron silver with a gold ridge; head and lower legs dark coat. Colours from the reference by hue (silver 207/198/188, red 82/27/28, gold 152/114/68, coat 26/8/6), used as silver 150/147/142, red 122/32/34, gold 152/114/68, coat 45/30/25 (**assumed**: test 12's lesson that bright metals read pale in game). Spec RGB 0 (no unit colours), gloss in alpha (metal 14-16, gold 12, cloth 4, coat 5); normal map flat. 512² each (**UNVERIFIED**: vanilla horse diffuse is 256²). Renders `renders/sheet_final_textured.png`.

Code: loadout `horses` (per tier: geometry JSON, texture PNGs, base horse, rights required, source), `core/fresh_mesh.apply_horses` (the .mesh on the base horse's skeleton, procedural DDS in the base textures' formats, a pdxmesh with the base horse's animations and scale, the cavalry entity pointed at it; names `hr_<set>_fresh_horse_<n>` from naming.py), rule `fresh_mesh` (re-import checks, animation ids, entity, textures generated, budget 3,000), checklist lines nH0-nH6. **Gallery pre-check** (`build/test15/gallery/sheet.png`; io_pdx_mesh imports our .mesh, plays idle, attaches the vanilla rider): both fresh horses load and carry the rider at `Saddle_Node`; on tier 1 the rider's legs vanish inside the barding (wider than vanilla's flanks).
- **Automatic vs tuned for this image** (answer to the user, 2026-10-18; the generalisation track comes after test 15 passes):
  - pose to bones (`reshape`): automatic method; its thresholds (leg tracking from y 6, slice radius 0.8, head region y > 8 and 3.5 deep) were set on this horse only. To be automatic: run on more references, confidence checks per landmark (track length, head axis spread) and a fallback, or keypoints detected in the image.
  - head untuck, foreleg straighten: part of `reshape` (same feature finder on both meshes; all four legs tracked): automatic, same caveat.
  - rider removal: tuned (the box z -3.8..5.0 read off this horse's side profile; its height is automatic, vanilla's seat). To be automatic: cut above a back line interpolated between the withers and the croup, between fractions of the body length around Saddle_Node.
  - leg lump: automatic method (mirror test), tuned box (z -2.5..2, y 4.5..12) and margin 0.3. To be automatic: the mirror test over the whole torso between the leg chains.
  - caparison trim/detach: the rules are general (cut below a hem except capsules round the leg and tail chains; torso weights cleaned of tail, rein and far lower-leg bones); the numbers were tuned (hem 5.2, leg capsule 0.65 after 0.9 left a web, tail gap 0.35). To be automatic: hem from the vanilla knee and hock heights, capsule radius from the measured leg width.
  - saddle fit: automatic (raycast against vanilla's seat, smooth shift, 0.05 tolerance, refused over 1.0).
  - chanfron: automatic on any reshaped horse (forehead/nose faces by a facing test against vanilla's head axis); lift, thickness and the facing threshold are fixed numbers.
  - texture colours: tuned (hue classes picked by hand, then darkened by hand) and the layout is this look's (scale front, cloth rear, crosses). To be automatic: colours per tagged box (C3/C12) or projected from the reference; layout from the tagger's labels.
  - also tuned: the crop box, voxel sizes (0.12, 0.3), remesh budget (2,600), 12 smoothing passes, TripoSG's seed. Not solved at all: the tail (missing; needs the tail rule).

Legs from slices of area-weighted surface samples (vanilla's low-poly legs have almost no vertices mid-bone; rays mis-paired). rider_leg: the share of the seated vanilla rider's lower legs visible from the side, the rider from `render_unit`'s new `dump` (posed meshes through `Saddle_Node`'s rest; the idle animation turns the horse's root ~45°). Seated boot sole ≈ (±3.2, 7.86, -1.9).

Hems from the reference (front 4.4, side 4.9, rear 1.3) raised to knee/hock + 0.4 (4.7-4.9) so the legs show. Weights: the body vertex nearest each anchor, minus leg/tail/rein bones. Stirrups from scratch under the boot, 100% HorseSpineTop. 2,912 triangles in all (body 2,200 target).

Deformation: idle 0.85-1.18 max 3.17, 25 flips, 7.2% | walk 0.90-1.13, 3.96, 8, 5.3% | attack 0.77-1.16, 3.09, 32, 8.2% (vanilla: 0.89-1.20/3.93/13/6.2%, 0.91-1.14/3.17/8/4.1%, 0.76-1.26/3.54/32/10.7%). Re-import checks clean.

Tilt mean 5.7°, body 9.3° (base game 4-20). **Known defect:** a lumpy streak on the back at the saddle (the low back was shifted 0.43 up to vanilla's seat after the source was taken). **UNVERIFIED in game:** the normal map's handedness for a fresh mesh (Blender's tangents on PDX-space geometry vs write_fresh's `ta`).

- **Round 3b (user, 2026-10-19): streak fixed, tail, close renders, test 16 staged; SDXL after the test.** Horse v20 (`work/v20*`, maps `v20b_*`).
  - **Rule: the bake source gets every reshape the low mesh gets.** fresh_horse writes `bake_source_out` (the fine surface after the edits, then the tail clearance and the saddle raise applied to it too). bake_horse checks low against high before baking: low body faces more than 0.3 (about one coarse voxel) from the source are flagged, grouped into areas and written as a mask (`*_mismatch.png`); optional flatten. v17: 51 faces on the back 0.36 above the source (the saddle raise); v20: 26 small faces at the armpits and between the hind legs, under the panels (the coarse voxel solid filling concave corners).
  - **The streak's real cause:** not the saddle raise but steps in the fine source. `flatten_lump` (the rider's leg bulge removed on one flank) snapped vertices to the other flank's width per 0.5 cell: terraces and a hard box edge. Rewritten as a blurred width grid read bilinearly, a soft clamp (tangent at the limit) and the move faded out at the box. The other warps lost their hard edges too (flank grid ends and leg exemption, leg inflation's reach, the neck's radius). Each reshape reports **steepness** (worst change of displacement per edge length); fresh_horse lists any over 2 in `steep_reshapes`. Lump 15.9 → 1.5, flank 10.8 → 1.5, legs 25.8 → 1.2.
  - **Rule: tail.** `_horse_measure.tail_state` checks six stations down vanilla's hanging tail: missing, fused (no air in front of it), short (a stub) or free. Unless it is free, the stub is cut off (before the bake source is taken) and our own tube is built along the tail chain from a root buried in the rump: 96 triangles, 6 sides, hair widening then tapering, weighted two bones at a time (each bone whole mid-segment, half and half at joints, like vanilla's), painted as hair. TripoSG's was "short": a stub the barding hid. The deformation check has per-group numbers now: tail travel idle 8.2 / walk 0.8 / attack 5.7 (vanilla 7.5 / 1.1 / 5.3), stretch 0.71-1.17, 0 flips.
  - **v20 vs vanilla:** depth 1.06, width 0.83, belly 0.92, legs 0.79 (gentler inflation; limit 0.75), neck 1.15, volume 0.90, rider_leg 0.97: all pass. 2,912 triangles. Deformation: idle 0.86-1.17, max 4.07, 36 flips, 7.0% | walk 0.89-1.11, 3.10, 3, 4.9% | attack 0.78-1.16, 5.09, 37, 8.1%. Idle has more flips than v17 (25) and vanilla (13); the worst edges are at NeckBase/SpineTop, not the tail. Bake: tilt mean 4.8°, body 8.0°.
  - **Lit renders** (gallery `lit`: EEVEE): io_pdx_mesh wires normal maps as G/A, but EU4's are plain RGB (vanilla horse: mean 126/127/252, alpha 255). The lit mode wires plain RGB with green inverted, because the import's axis swap mirrors the mesh and so the tangent frame. Only that way does the vanilla horse light correctly. Ours behaves the same under the same convention, so its handedness matches vanilla's (still **UNVERIFIED in game**). GAME_CLOSE elevation, front/side/3/4 (azimuths turned 45° for the idle pose's root turn): `build/test16/close/sheet_game_close.png`.

Still bad: the panels read as flat slabs (a box or tarp), the chest and neck mail looks like wool and is bulky, a broken white patch on the head, the front legs mostly hidden, the tail sticks up over the rump. **The control horse (tier 2) read more like a horse than the TripoSG one.**

  - **Shared fixes:**
    - **Tail:** anchored on our rump. Its hanging nodes move by the offset from vanilla's TailTop to our nearest rump point, and the weights stay on the tail bones.

    - **Paint:** mail at 0.25 with an anti-aliased rim in one continuous projection. A projection plane per face made a patchwork, and a hard rim at 0.2 aliased to speckle.

Clipped: depth 1.1, width ≤ 1.0 (a wider barrel hides the vanilla rider's legs), neck ≤ 1.0, legs 0.95-1.1. The reference's topline is applied ±0.5, faded at the saddle.
    - **Assembly:** voxel 0.15, relaxed. Its own smooth surface is the bake source (normal tilt 2.6°: nothing to add).

    | | depth | width | belly | legs | neck | rider leg | flips idle / walk / attack |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | B | 1.03 | 0.92 | 0.92 | 1.08 | 0.99 | 1.06 | 36 / 3 / 44 |
    | A | 1.06 | 0.90 | 0.92 | 0.85 | 0.97 | 0.97 | 35 / 18 / 58 |

    - **Bending:** worse than vanilla in idle and attack, at the spine under the panels.
  - **Provisional (B won the renders; confirm in test 17): the hybrid approach.**
    - **Base body:** procedural, on the target skeleton, its sections measured from the base-game template and fitted to the reference's proportions and topline. The generated model gives shape cues, not geometry.
    - **Armour and cloth:** driven by the reference (coverage, panel length and splits, drape) and built over the body.
    - **Rigid parts:** TripoSG or procedural (chanfron, plates), merged and weighted to one bone.
    - **Textures:** from the reference's colours, with a scale that reads at map size.

    - Panel flips.
