#### M18. The self-reading pipeline: a new horse with no hand setup (2026-10-21)
The user (after test 22's report; in-game tests 21 and 22 skipped for now, "the game side is proven"): make the pipeline read each reference by itself. Steps:
1. parts detection per image (SAM 2 plus a classifier or local vision model, colours fitted per image, no hand colour rules);
2. build only what the labels show (no armour or caparison on a horse without; a saddle cloth when there is one);
3. automatic landmarks: a licence-clean keypoint model if one exists (options and licences listed, installed only on the user's OK), otherwise from the silhouette and the fitted body;
4. a command that writes the subject config and the skeleton input; no fixed view names;
5. horse 2 rerun completely untouched, its sheet next to its reference, and a report of what still needed a human.

- **Step 1 done: `ref3d/parts.py` and the AI job `ref3d_parts`.**
  - **Model:** SigLIP 2 (google/siglip2-so400m-patch14-384, Apache-2.0; weights in `<AI root>/weights/`, never in the repo) names every SAM 2 segment against plain material prompts: metal, gold, cloth, leather, coat, hair, plume. The prompts name no colours.
  - **Colours per image:** a 12-colour palette (k-means in Lab). Each colour takes the material of the segments where it is a main colour. A colour that is a minority inside its segment keeps its own material when that is confident (gold embroidery on red cloth becomes trim). A segment covering most of the horse is split by the palette first.
  - **Layout from the silhouette** (`_ref3d_silhouette`): facing, the opened mask (straps gone), the belly (a caparison's hem), back, chest, buttock, the head end, legs and tail. This gives the template's classes: metal on the head is chanfron; coat (or lit-grey "metal") below the belly is leg; hair at the rear is tail, elsewhere mane (coat); a plume only on top of the head. It writes the zones (head, legs, tail, trunk, plume) the hand configs carried.
  - **Parts present**, each with share, box and fitted colours: armour, chanfron, caparison, saddle cloth (cloth not reaching the belly and shorter than 0.6 of the trunk), saddle, bridle, plume, bare coat, tail.
  - **Trials:**

    | Version | Change | Cataphract S | Cataphract L | Horse 2 |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | v008 | — | 61% | 48% | coat speckled with leather (v002–v003) |
    | v009 | each palette colour named alone | 33% | 49% | — |
    | v010 | colours named by segment votes | 65% | 55% | armour invented (v005) |
    | v011 | whole-horse segment split by palette | 65% | 55% | correct (v006) |
    | v013 | crops 0.15/0.15 | 68% | 55% | correct (v007) |
    | v015 | lit-grey legs fixed | 70% (legs 0.93, chanfron 0.98, tail 0.79) | 55% | correct (v009) |

    Cataphract figures are pixel agreement with the hand-approved labels (S v007, L v006). The fixes behind the other rows:
    - "a horse's head" left the coat prompts after v008: the armoured head went to coat;
    - v013's crop setting: 0.3/0.35 let the armour around a leg name it metal; 0.0 (v014) invented armour and a plume on horse 2;
    - v015: the legs below the belly were lit grey and read as metal.
  - **Parts found:**
    - **Cataphract S:** armour, chanfron, caparison, saddle, bridle, plume, tail; no bare coat.
    - **Cataphract L** (front three-quarter): armour, chanfron, caparison, plume. It misses the saddle and bridle, which are grey-green there.
    - **Horse 2:** saddle cloth (blue, with its trim), saddle, bridle, bare coat, tail; no armour, chanfron, caparison or plume.
  - L's 55% is mostly the hand labels' gold speckle and the saddle; S is the main evidence for the head, legs and tail.
  - About 25 s per view.
- **Step 2 done: the master builds only what the labels show** (`master.subject_parts`: a part is present when any view's parts file shows it; labels without parts files, such as the cataphract's hand labels, build everything as before):
  - **No armour:** no E-B run, no plates, no plates_bigger break.
  - **No caparison:** no E-C run, no side panels, no chest panel, no tassels, no caparison_short break, no hue loop.
  - **No chanfron:** the head is coat. **No plume:** none.
  - **Saddle cloth (new):** a sheet cast from its box in the main view onto the body (rays through a 10 × 16 grid), 15 mm off it, carried over the back along the body's top; the far side is the near side's mirror, with the view's image projected on it. The E-D colour breaks reach it.
  - **A self-read subject's body carries the main view's image projected** (the near side as seen, the far side its mirror; the labels projected likewise as its classes). The bridle, straps, saddle, markings and dark legs are texture. The first build (v002) used label shells: cut from the body's large triangles, they stood out as jagged strips.
  - **The parts job writes the view as a texture:** the background is filled with the nearest horse pixel, from the horse eroded by 0.5% and leaving out pixels within ΔE 15 of the backdrop.
    - v004: a 400 px fill left the far background grey on the far legs.
    - v010: the mask's anti-aliased edge spread grey.
  - The tail outline comes from S when there is one, else from the main view.
  - **Horse 2, master v006** (body: step 3's fit v002; labels: parts v012):
    - Built: saddle cloth, the projected bay coat, tack and dark legs.
    - Not built: armour, panels, chest panel, plume, chanfron.
    - 84 s, against about 40 min with E-B and E-C.
    - Gates: colour and hue pass; regions, landmarks and coverage fail on the tail and throat. The cause is step 3's fit: its camera for this true side view is turned 31° and rolled 6°, which step 5's refit addresses.
    - The lit render shows a white band under the belly; it is the sky lighting the underside, not the texture (the albedo is brown).
- **Step 3: automatic landmarks.**
  - **Keypoint models checked (2026-10-21); none is clearly licence-clean, so none was installed:**
    - **DeepLabCut SuperAnimal-Quadruped (horses included):** the TensorFlow model card says academic, non-commercial use only. One PyTorch card revision carries an Apache-2.0 tag that its own text contradicts ("all rights reserved"; commercial licences through EPFL-TTO).
    - **RTMPose / ViTPose trained on AP-10K:** the AP-10K data is for non-commercial use only (MMPose docs). The weights' own terms aren't stated and derive from that data.
    - **Horse-10 (DLC):** its licence isn't confirmed by an authoritative source; mirrors list it as non-commercial.
    - **X-Pose (formerly UniPose, IDEA Research):** licence terms not confirmed; trained on mixed datasets that include AP-10K.
    - **A licence-clean route, if wanted later (on the user's OK):** DINOv2 (Apache-2.0) feature correspondence, carrying landmarks over from an annotated reference the user owns.
  - **Done instead: landmarks from the silhouette** (`_ref3d_silhouette.landmarks`, written by the parts job as `<view>_landmarks.json` and drawn on the labels overlay):
    - **Head:** muzzle (front-most), the ears (the two highest peaks of the top outline, from pixels that can be an ear: not cloth, trim, plume or tail), the poll (under the rear ear, at the notch between them), the chin (lowest point of the head's front half), the throat (top of the lower outline behind the jaw).
    - **Trunk**, from the horse without its hanging cloth: chest_front, buttock, croup.
    - **Feet:** the columns that reach the ground, in runs; the lower foot of a pair is the near leg. The fetlocks sit 0.09 × (ground − back) above the hooves.
    - **Tail:** its top and tip.
    - **Not found:** the eye, nostril and bit ring (no outline shows them). E-A fits with the landmarks present, and the fitted body places those three.
  - **Scored against the hand landmarks** (error as a share of the body length):

    | View | Median | Worst |
    |---|---|---|
    | Horse 2 L | 1.1% | tail tip 5.1%, croup 4.9%, chest 4.3%: points the hand placed loosely |
    | Cataphract S | 1.4% | buttock 9.7% (the hand placed it under the tail, on the armour), chest 6.0%, an ear 5.9% (next to the plume) |
    | Cataphract L (front three-quarter on a rock) | — | hind feet 16–18% |

    The silhouette method is for side views.
  - **Fixes on the way:**
    - the tail closed and grown before the buttock is read (the buttock was the tail's edge, 11%);
    - the chest read below the breast collar;
    - the poll at the ears' notch (a dip search reached the mane);
    - ears scored as an unordered pair, as E-A matches them.
- **Step 4 done: `python -m eu4unitgen.ref3d.auto new <image> --name <subject> --rights "<declaration>" --ai-python <env python>`** writes the subject from the image alone:
  - **The view:** the whole image and its horse mask. A horse facing right is mirrored to show a left side (`mirror`; the template's near legs are the horse's left). Tested on horse 2's image flipped: it was detected, mirrored back, and gave the same parts, with landmarks within 19 px.
  - **Parts, labels, zones and landmarks:** from the parts job (steps 1 and 3), approved as read.
  - **The config:** zones (head, legs, tail), landmarks, body length (chest to buttock), the head's pivot (poll), `details: []` (the eye is in the projected texture; E-A now takes a view with an empty details list).
    - E-A's side-view camera window, when all four feet show apart.
    - The trunk landmarks steering the main view in the refine.
    - Workers: half the cores, at most 6.
  - **The skeleton input** from the install's cavalry_horse.mesh: bones, seat (the back's surface at Saddle_Node, by ray in plain Python), vanilla positions and triangles, round 5's B parameters (now a constant, `auto.B2_PARAMS`). It is identical to the hand-copied file (seat within 3e-6).
  - **AI folders follow from the AI python:** `<AI root>/rembg_models`, `weights/sam2.1-hiera-large`, `weights/siglip2-so400m-patch14-384`, `TripoSG`, `weights/TripoSG`. The environment variables still override.
  - **No fixed view names:** the config's `main_view` (`s1.main_view`; "L" for the configs before M18) replaces "L" in:
    - E-A: the independent fit's main body, the refine's head and leg views, the b2 pose;
    - shells, E-B, the master and its assembly, the game version and its repose;
    - E-C's panel job: its driver renames the main view to the job's internal "L".
  - **Horse 2 as `horse2_auto`:** 112 s with no manual input, against the hand config of finish line step 3 (25 landmarks and the zones by hand). It found: saddle cloth, saddle, bridle, bare coat, tail; 19 landmarks; a side view.
  - **`auto run <subject.json> --ai-python ... [--test testNN]`** chains shells, fit, refine, master and game untouched. It records each step in `auto_run.json`, so a stopped run continues where it stopped.
  - **What a human still gives:** the image, a name and the rights declaration (rule 8: a statement only the user can make).
- **Step 5 done: horse 2 rerun completely untouched** (`horse2_auto`: `auto new`, then `auto run`). Sheet: `assets_in/horse2_auto/final_sheet.png`.
  - **First attempt:** Claude Code stopped it (the PC was low on memory) after 5 of the 8 TripoSG seeds.
  - **Lighter, safer runs (the user's request):**
    - **Shells:** each seed is kept as it lands, and a stopped run reuses every whole GLB (glTF magic and the declared length); the 5 were reused.
    - **One heavy job at a time;** each TripoSG seed runs in its own process, so its memory is freed when it ends.
    - **A free-RAM check before each step** (`ref3d/memory.py`): shells 10 GB, fit 8.5 GB, the rest 8 GB. Below that, the run stops and says so.
    - **The fit's processes follow the free RAM:** (free − 6 GB reserve) / 2.5 GB, at most 6. That gave 4.
  - **A bug on the way:** the generated config's `ea.refine` block put a plain fit into refine mode (KeyError 'candidate'). `fit_args` now leaves it out. The failed folder is kept as `fit/v001_failed_refine_key`.
  - **RAM (31.9 GB machine; 14.3 GB free at the start, with EU4, Firefox, Discord, Steam and CurseForge open):**

    | Step | Time | Lowest free RAM |
    |---|---|---|
    | Shells | 503 s | 9.6 GB |
    | Fit | 7,018 s | 6.6 GB |
    | Refine | 694 s | 16.0 GB |
    | Master | 107 s | 14.6 GB |
    | Game | 97 s | 14.6 GB |

    - A fit worker measured about 0.2 GB of resident RAM. The 2.5 GB assumption is conservative, and the rule could allow more workers.
  - **Results:**
    - **Fit v001:** procedural is best; the restarts converge (15.5 / 15.6 / 16.6; step 3's did not: 5.3 / 18.5 / 25.1); region overlap 0.92; worst landmark 8.4% (the 2.5% gate fails, as in step 3).
    - **Refine v002.**
    - **Master v001:** a bay horse with bridle, saddle, the blue saddle cloth and its trim, dark legs, a hanging tail; no armour, caparison, chest panel, chanfron or plume. Colour and hue pass. The tail's region (IoU 0.36) and one landmark (near hind fetlock 6.8%; head 3.7%) fail.
    - **Game v001:** 2,030 triangles. It reads as horse 2. Its tail is thin and short from the side (fresh_horse's own tail, built along vanilla's bones).
  - **What still needed a human:** the image, the name and the rights declaration, nothing else. No annotation, no colour rules, no view naming, no copied files, no environment variables, no worker count.
  - **What is still weak:**
    - the tail's shape (master and game);
    - E-A's landmark gate on a single painted view (8.4%; no allowance set for this subject);
    - the silhouette landmarks are for side views;
    - the parts classifier agrees 55% with the cataphract's three-quarter view L.

### Phase 4: Test loop
- "Launch & check": run EU4 with the mod, parse error.log, map errors back to generated files in the GUI.

### Phase 5+: Extensibility
- "Asset family" plugin model: entity templates + slot schema + validator rules.
- Ships: same entity/pdxmesh chain, but `sprite_level` (1–5) lives on the ship unit and ships don't auto-upgrade with tech.
- Portraits and cities: separate subsystems (2D sprites; graphical-culture building sets). Keep `naming.py` and `graph.py` generic.

#### M19. A horse in the app, and a faster 3D backend (2026-10-21)
- **Step 1 done: the Horse screen** (`gui/horse_page.py`, Home → "Horse from image"; the work in `ref3d/job.py`).
  - **Inputs:** drop in an image, give a name and the rights statement (rule 8, required), and the AI environment's python (remembered). The EU4 install comes from Settings.
  - **Before starting, warnings to confirm** (`job.precheck`, Pillow only):
    - **Background not clean:** under 75% of the border is plain. Clean AI renders score 89%, the cataphract reference sheet 31%.
    - **Not a side view:** the subject is less than 1.1 times as wide as it is tall (the three-quarter crop is 0.80).
    - **Cropped:** the subject touches 2 or more edges.
    - **Low RAM:** under the shells' 10 GB. The warning names the 5 programs using the most RAM.
  - **The run outlives the window:** `job.start` runs `job go` detached. Its `status.json` holds:
    - each stage's state and time;
    - a heartbeat every 15 s (a run silent for 4 beats counts as stopped);
    - any error, the parts found and the sheet.
  - **Resume:** finished steps are kept by `auto run`. A half-read subject is read again. A step without enough RAM shows "close programs, Resume".
  - **Progress:** stages with "x of about y". The estimates are the median of earlier runs under the workspace (the fit per process), else horse 2's times.
  - **Results:** the final sheet (`job.final_sheet`: the reference, what was read, the master, the game version side and three-quarter).
  - **"Add to mod":** `job stage` stages the newest game version at tier 1 of a new `build/<name>_NN` test mod, with the vanilla horse at tier 2.
- **Step 2 dropped (the user, 2026-10-21):** "Speed is now the priority". The hand-built path (TripoSG shells + the 2-hour E-A fit) stays only as a fallback, with no more polish. Next: a bake-off of tools that make a textured mesh directly (TRELLIS.2, Hunyuan3D 2.1, a cloud option); the winner becomes the default backend in front of the proven steps (the vanilla skeleton, weights, game version, writer).

#### M20. The simpler pipeline: body, cloak, projected colour (2026-10-21)
- **The user's change of approach (after test 23):** drop the high-detail master path (modelled plates, cloth simulation, precision fitting): too slow, and test 23's tier 2 looked bad. The new pipeline:
  1. a normal horse body, built from the image as horse 2's was;
  2. armour and cloth as a cloak: a thin layer over the body that keeps the horse's outline, only where parts detection finds them;
  3. colour projected from the reference; unseen areas filled from matching visible ones, never stretched;
  4. the game model by the existing steps, with the head droop fixed.
  - The TRELLIS.2 bake-off is on hold.
- **Quick proof (test 24 tier 1; `assets_in/proof_t24`):** test 15's horse v10 with textures projected from `cataphract_side_clean.png` (`ai/jobs/ref3d_project_texture.py`).
  - **Fit:** the mesh's side silhouette is fitted to the mask (scale and offset per axis, IoU 0.74). A thin-plate spline then runs through the landmarks the mesh (from its skeleton weights) and the image share. With the fit alone, v10's raised head took the chest's colours.
  - **The user's verdict:** the colours are right (silver scale, gold chanfron, red cloth and crosses). The shape is wrong: a llama neck, a sack body, thin legs, a bent hind leg, a smeared orange/red underside.
- **Tier 2: the new pipeline (`cataphract_auto`; subject `"pipeline": "cloak"`, now the default from `ref3d.auto new`):**
  - **Body:**
    - a light fit: the procedural candidate only, 2 restarts, half the evaluations (`auto.FAST_FIT`), no TripoSG shells, no refine;
    - the master is the body alone;
    - `game_master` reposes it.
  - **Head droop:** the repose turned the head from its fitted pitch onto the default 40° (horse 2: 25° to 40°) and laid the neck on vanilla's rest bones (horse 2's neck was raised 28°). Now it keeps the fitted pitch and half the neck's raise (`GAME_DEFAULTS["repose"]`).
  - **Cloak:** `fresh_horse`'s drape, thin and continuous (offset 0.06, small folds, no slits, a readable hem; `GAME_DEFAULTS["cloak"]`), only when armour or a caparison is found.
  - **Colour:** projected onto the low mesh (`game.project_colour`):
    - body texels inside the cloak's outline and width are hidden, so they take the coat's colour from visible coat or leg texels;
    - other unseen texels take the distance-weighted mean of the 48 nearest seen texels of their own part (body, cloak, other), mirrored across the centre plane;
    - the hooves come from the body's geometry (the lowest point per quarter), not from weights (the cloak's hem won a foreleg's weights).
  - **Legs never thinner than vanilla's** (`fresh` `leg_allow` 1.0). The fit made the hidden legs 0.14–0.4 units wide (horse 2: 0.7–1.3), and the remesh cut the near foreleg off at its knee (game v001).
  - **Times:** read 187 s, fit 1,330 s (2 processes), master 112 s, game 48 s: **28 minutes from the image** (horse 2: 2 h 37 min). Game v002: 2,666 triangles, 66% of the texels seen directly.
  - **Weak spots:** boxy cloak corners, a neck slimmer than the reference's, the skirts' red and gold on the upper legs, a flat tail.
- **Test 24 staged** (`build/test24`; sheet `build/test24/sheet.png`):
  - tier 1: the proof;
  - tier 2: `cataphract_auto` game v002;
  - tier 3: vanilla.
  - Every unit is named "T24 ...", through the spec's `unit_names`. The validator found 0 errors and 0 warnings.

#### M21. TRELLIS.2 as the front end (2026-10-22)
- **Licences checked from the files themselves (rule 8, 2026-10-22):**
  - **TRELLIS.2 code** (`D:/eu4unitgen_ai/TRELLIS.2/LICENSE`): MIT, Microsoft. **TRELLIS.2-4B weights** (model card): MIT. **TRELLIS-image-large** (the sparse-structure decoder): MIT.
  - **DINOv3 ViT-L/16** (`facebook/dinov3-vitl16-pretrain-lvd1689m`, `LICENSE.md`: the DINOv3 License, last updated 2025-08-19), the image encoder TRELLIS.2 conditions on. Gated; the user accepted it and downloaded it with their own Hugging Face account on 2026-10-22. It is kept in `<AI root>/weights/`, never in the repo, the app or a mod.
    - §1a: a worldwide, royalty-free licence to use, copy, modify and make derivative works. No non-commercial clause and no field-of-use limit beyond §1b.
    - §1b.i: anyone who *redistributes* the DINO Materials must include the licence. We don't redistribute them: each user downloads them under their own acceptance. Generated meshes and textures are outputs, not DINO Materials.
    - §1b.v: no ITAR work, and none of the end uses that trade controls prohibit ("military or warfare purposes, nuclear ..., espionage, ... guns or illegal weapons"). That clause covers real-world military end uses under trade-control law. A historical game mod showing soldiers is entertainment, not a military end use.
    - §5a: the user owns the derivatives and modifications they make. Meta claims nothing over outputs.
    - No attribution requirement for outputs (§1b.ii only applies to research publications).
    - **Verdict:** the user's use (local generation of horse and unit models for a free EU4 mod, offline, weights not redistributed) is allowed.
  - **Not used:** briaai/RMBG-2.0 (CC BY-NC 4.0, non-commercial). The job replaces it with our BiRefNet-general (MIT) cut-out.
  - **nvdiffrast and nvdiffrec: not used (checked from their `LICENSE.txt`, 2026-10-23).**
    - nvdiffrast v0.4.0: the "Nvidia Source Code License (1-Way Commercial)". nvdiffrec (JeffreyXiang's `renderutils` branch): the "NVIDIA Source Code License for nvdiffrec". Both §3.3: the Work "only may be used or intended for use non-commercially", defined as "for research or evaluation purposes only" (nvdiffrast adds "and not for any direct or indirect monetary gain").
    - The user's mod is free and non-commercial, which meets the no-money test. Generating assets for a released mod is not plainly "research or evaluation", though, so we don't rely on that clause.
    - **Outputs vs the tool:** the licences restrict use of the software and works derived from its code. A mesh or texture rasterised with it contains none of that code and isn't a derivative work of it, so the licence doesn't attach to the outputs. The question is only whether the *use* is permitted, and removing the packages settles that.
    - **Where TRELLIS.2 uses them:** nvdiffrec only in the preview renderer (`trellis2/renderers/pbr_mesh_renderer.py`, environment lighting; imported lazily, never reached by our job). nvdiffrast in `o_voxel.postprocess.to_glb`'s texture bake (rasterise the mesh in UV space, interpolate positions), in the texturing pipeline and in the renderers (neither used).
    - **Replacement:** `ai/jobs/_uv_raster.py` (ours, GPL-3.0), a torch UV rasteriser with the same outputs, registered as `nvdiffrast.torch` before o_voxel is imported. Neither package is built or installed (`tools/wsl/trellis2_setup.sh`). Checked once against nvdiffrast on the GPU (evaluation, which the licence allows), with a 180k-triangle UV grid at 2048², chunked as the bake does: 0.003% of texels differ in coverage (edge ties), 99.35% have the same face (the rest are on shared edges), and interpolated positions agree within 3.8e-5. Time: 0.22 s against nvdiffrast's 0.04 s. nvdiffrast was then uninstalled. A later fix (one face per texel, so a texel shared by two triangles can't mix two faces' u, v and id) is covered by `tests/test_uv_raster.py` (CPU and CUDA, against a brute force).
    - **Others in the environment (licences):** CuMesh and FlexGEMM: MIT (Jianfeng Xiang). o-voxel: part of TRELLIS.2 (MIT). Eigen (header-only, built into o-voxel): MPL-2.0. xformers: BSD. torch: BSD. All allow this use. None of them is redistributed with the mod.
  - **Inputs:** `cataphract_side_clean.png` (§M17, rights recorded 2026-10-20) and `horse2_light_cavalry.png` (§M17, rights recorded 2026-10-21), both generated by the user with ChatGPT and owned by the user.
- **Environment: WSL2 (2026-10-23).** The user kept Smart App Control on, which blocks the Windows build's unsigned extensions, so the environment runs in WSL2 Ubuntu 26.04 instead (`tools/wsl/trellis2_setup.sh`):
  - **Toolchain:** CUDA 12.9 with a header patch for glibc 2.43 (`tools/wsl/cuda_glibc_patch.sh`), and gcc-13.
  - **Packages:** transformers pinned to 4.57.1.
  - **Reused from D: through /mnt/d:** the TRELLIS.2 checkout (75fbf01) and every weight folder.
  - **On the Linux disk:** the environment and the build tree. C: had 275.7 GB free.
  - **Memory (32 GB host):** `.wslconfig` gives WSL 20 GB and 16 GB swap, with `autoMemoryReclaim=dropCache`. 24 GB with no reclaim starved Windows: Claude Code's low-memory reaper killed a build.
  - In low-VRAM mode every model sits in system RAM, so a 1024_cascade run peaks near 19 GB in WSL with up to 1.8 GB of swap. Windows kept about 2 GB available. Runs are started with `setsid`, so a reaper killing the launching shell doesn't stop them.
- **First raw results (2026-10-23; sheet `assets_in/bakeoff_m19/trellis2/raw_sheet.png`; seed 42, 1024_cascade, decimation 300k, texture 2048):**

  | | load | generate | export | peak VRAM | faces |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | horse 2 | 93 s | 239 s | 38 s | 4.14 GB | 284,323 |
  | cataphract | 92 s | 220 s | 63 s | 5.59 GB | 280,787 |

  About 6 minutes per horse, against M20's 28 minutes. No out-of-memory fallback was needed.
  - **Horse 2:** the silhouette, pose and proportions match the reference, and so do the bay coat, the black lower legs, the blue pad with its gold border, the brown saddle with stirrup and the bridle with reins.
    - The mane and tail are spiky strand geometry. Their base colour is near-black with grey streaks.
    - The metallic map is high everywhere (mean 0.65; the hair renders silver under PBR).
  - **Cataphract:** the layout matches: gold chanfron; silver scale on the neck, chest and hindquarters; red caparison with gold borders; red tassels; black legs and tail.
    - **Lost detail:** the Jerusalem crosses (the large flank cross became blurred gold shapes, and the small crosses are gone).
    - **Other differences:** some gold tassels came out pale or white, the scales are coarser than the reference's, and the saddle is near-black, not brown.
  - **For processing:**
    - The facing is not consistent (the cataphract's head points along +X, horse 2's along −Y), so it must be normalised.
    - The metallic and roughness maps don't carry over to EU4's material.
    - The UV atlases are very fragmented (thousands of small charts, mostly hair), so downsizing the 2048 textures needs padding or a re-bake.
- **The user's verdict (2026-10-23):** both raw results approved; TRELLIS.2 is the default model source for new horses.
- **Processing, first try (game v001):** fresh_horse's model path (box fit, reshape onto vanilla's rest pose, voxel 0.3, QuadriFlow) with the base colour baked on (Blender job `bake_colour`). The user: worse than the raw model (a goose-like neck and head, a lumpy body, a blurry texture).
- **The direct path (the user's rules: keep the model's shape; minimal posing; 1024 texture baked from the raw model)** — fresh_horse source `"direct"`, all automatic:
  1. **Import:** TRELLIS.2's mesh welded at its UV seams (it comes split there), the largest piece.
  2. **Facing:** turned about the vertical to face −Z (the long horizontal axis, then the end that reaches higher holds the head). Horse 2: 0°; the cataphract: −90°.
  3. **Scale:** one uniform scale, so proportions are kept: the croup's height first, then the seat matched to vanilla's. The seat is measured by downward rays at Saddle_Node: the dip between the saddle's rims.
  4. **Posing:** each lower leg is slid sideways so its hoof stands under vanilla's heel, faded out from the knee to the belly (horse 2: 0.45–2.2; the cataphract: 0.9–2.8). The head and neck aren't moved: they're put on vanilla's poll only while the weights are taken (horse 2: 2.3 away, 19° off).
  5. **Hair, read by colour and place:**
     - **Tail colour:** the median of the rear-most hanging vertices at the height of vanilla's tail tip. The first rules failed: darkness took horse 2's shaded bay coat, and greyness would take silver scale.
     - **Tail:** vertices within 40 (RGB) of that colour, behind the hips and the buttock, connected; cut off the closed solid. Cut off the open model instead, its ragged root leaked through the voxels and the hindquarters vanished.
     - **New tail:** `hb.tail` on vanilla's tail bones, sized from the cut tail and painted from its colours (`bake_colour` `part_colours`). The body's tail-bone weights are cleared: the rump had swung with the tail in idle.
     - **Mane:** vertices of the same colour on the neck's top half, smoothed hard on the even voxels into one crest. Cutting it took the neck's top half; smoothing the dense model barely moved it.
  6. **Closed solid, then decimation:**
     - **Solid:** a flood fill. Surface voxels are closed by one voxel, the outside is flooded in from the box, and the rest is solid. A plain voxel remesh of the cataphract's open surface made a hollow shell (volume 74) that collapsed to lace; filled, it was 627.
     - **Smoothing:** a voxel remesh at 0.1, retried coarser if the collapse stalls.
     - **Decimation:** quadric collapse to 2,500, then degenerate edges dissolved. A collapse straight on TRELLIS.2's surface stalls at 36–61k: thousands of non-manifold edges and open sheets.
  7. **UVs:** a few large islands (each leg, the head, the trunk with the neck, the tail; left and right halves).
     - Borders are straightened by a neighbour vote; unwrap conformal; packed with a 0.002 gap, then stretched to fill the square.
     - Smart UV gave 45–59%. Larger islands' jagged borders packed at 52% until straightened.
  8. **Weights:** chain weights; the tail along its own bones.
  9. **Textures:**
     - **Colour:** `bake_colour` bakes the raw model's base colour selected-to-active at 1024 and pads the gutters.
     - **Normal and AO:** `bake_horse` from the raw model.
     - **Specular:** coat gloss everywhere for now. The metal gloss for armour is still to do.
  - **Comparison:** Blender job `render_compare` renders the raw model and the game version through one orthographic camera per view. The sheet adds a silhouette overlay and its IoU.

  | cataphract v006 | 2,432 | 67% | 0.73 | 0.85–1.19 / 4.1 / 17 | 0.90–1.11 / 5.7 / 13 | 0.77–1.18 / 10.4 / 33 |

#### M22. The standard horse pipeline: image → TRELLIS.2 → processing → game (2026-10-23)
The horse track's result. It replaces M18–M20's self-reading, fitted and cloak pipelines as the default for new horses; those stay as fallbacks.

**One command** (`ref3d/trellis.py`):
```
python -m eu4unitgen.ref3d.trellis run <image> --name <subject> --rights "<declaration>" --ai-python <Windows AI python>
                                       [--emblem <decal.png>] [--country-mask] [--test testNN]
```
It writes `assets_in/<name>/subject.json`, then `assets_in/<name>/trellis/`: `input/`, `raw.glb`, and `game/vNNN/` with the textures, `manifest.json`, `compare_sheet.png` and `deform/`. `process <glb> --out <dir>` and `stage --test testNN --names TNN <dir>@<subject.json> ...` run the later stages alone.

**Inputs:** a side view of the horse on a plain background; a name; the rights declaration (rule 8: the user's own statement). For a nation-specific unit, a decal mask (white on black) and the country-colour option.

**1. Cut-out** (Windows AI environment): BiRefNet (MIT, ai job `ref3d_mask`) masks the horse; the image goes to TRELLIS.2 as RGBA, so its own background remover (RMBG-2.0, non-commercial) is never loaded.

**2. TRELLIS.2** (WSL2, `tools/wsl/trellis2_setup.sh`; the environment, licences and memory are in M21): ai job `trellis2_generate`.
- 1024_cascade, seed 42, low-VRAM mode, our UV rasteriser instead of nvdiffrast.
- About 6 minutes and 4–6 GB of VRAM; up to ~19 GB of system RAM in WSL.
- The result is a ~280k-face textured `.glb`.

**3. Processing** (Blender, fresh_horse source `direct`; everything automatic):

| Step | What it does | Why (the case that taught it) |
|---|---|---|
| Weld, largest piece | coincident vertices merged | TRELLIS.2 splits its mesh at UV seams |
| Face forward | the long axis onto z, the head's end (higher) to −Z | TRELLIS.2 picks its own facing (the cataphract: 90° off) |
| Scale | one uniform scale: the croup, then the seat (rays at Saddle_Node, the dip between the saddle's rims) at vanilla's | the rider sits on `Saddle_Node` |
| Slim | a body whose side silhouette is over 1.15× vanilla's is scaled about the seat, the legs stretched to the ground | test 26: bulkier than vanilla's slim horse (1.44–1.65×) |
| Head found before the slim, its poll carried through | head features on the unslimmed model | after it, the cataphract's chest was taken for its head |
| Legs | each lower leg slid under vanilla's heel, faded from the knee to the belly; the head and neck not moved (put on vanilla's poll only for the weights) | test 25 v001's reshape bent the neck into a goose curve |
| Hair | the tail found by its own colour (the rear-most hanging vertices), behind the buttock, cut off the closed solid; our tube on vanilla's tail bones, sized and painted from it; the body's tail-bone weights cleared. The mane's voxels smoothed into a crest | the strands tore in idle; darkness and greyness rules took coat and silver |
| Closed solid | flood fill (closing 0.2, kept only where thick; an opening of 0.2 on the head and neck, the ears kept); voxel remesh 0.1, relaxed | the cataphract's surface is open (a shell, then lace); legs standing close welded; fragments (mane flaps, the bit) |
| Decimate | quadric collapse to 2,800 body triangles (2,928 with the tail), degenerate edges dissolved; the close retried coarser if it stalls | a collapse on TRELLIS.2's surface stalls at 36–61k |
| UVs | region islands (legs, head, trunk with neck, main panels, tail; left and right), borders straightened, conformal, the panels 2× and the trunk 1.4× texel density, packed, stretched to the square | Smart UV's many islands packed at 45–59% |
| Weights | chain weights; the tail along its bones | |

**4. Textures** (1024; `bake_colour`, `bake_horse`):
- base colour baked from the raw model, selected-to-active, gutters padded;
- the tail painted from its colours;
- head band and marks (with a chanfron), emblem decal and country-colour mask (opt-in);
- gloss by colour class (silver 14, gold 12, matte 5; TRELLIS.2's metallic map isn't used);
- the colour matched in Lab to the raw model's lit render (lightness half way), silver held at 0.85 of the raw's;
- the normal map and AO from the raw model.

**5. Checks:**
- the comparison sheet (raw and game version through one camera, overlay and IoU: 0.75–0.80 side);
- the deformation check against vanilla in idle, moving and attack;
- the gallery, staged with `stage`;
- the validator at staging.

**6. Game:** the loadout's `horses` (geometry, textures, rights) on `cavalry_horse`'s skeleton and animations, through `core/fresh_mesh` and the test-mod writer.

**Results:** the cataphract and horse 2 are in M21 (tests 25–28). The cataphract works in game (test 27).

**Known limits:**
- the reins and stirrup leathers are lost;
- the cataphract's skirt is solid to its hem;
- texture use is 60–72%;
- horse 2's moving and attack deformation is above vanilla's since the slim (its legs stretched);
- head marks and the band are texture, not geometry;
- the country colour's brightness in game is UNVERIFIED (test 28).

**End to end, untouched (2026-10-23):** `run` on `new_refs/horse2_light_cavalry.png` as subject `horse2_t2`.
- Total 13.6 min: the cut-out; TRELLIS.2 10.4 min (load 212 s, generate 360 s, export 48 s; 4.0 GB VRAM; 289k faces; our rasteriser); processing, bakes, the sheet and the deformation check 85 s.
- Facing turned −0.3°, slimmed 0.90 (1.43× → 1.23× vanilla's side silhouette), seat 12.30 against 12.23, 2,927 triangles, side IoU 0.81.
- Deformation idle 0.86–1.21 / 3.6 / 23, moving 0.86–1.16 / 5.4 / 35, attack 0.73–1.24 / 5.7 / 55.
- Its only human inputs: the image, the name, the rights declaration and the AI python.

**Next track: the rider** (the user named `assets_in/new_refs/cataphract_rider.png`; the folder holds `cataphract_rider_v1.png`).

## 11. References
- EU4 wiki, Model modding: https://eu4.paradoxwikis.com/Model_modding
- EU4 wiki, Unit modding: https://eu4.paradoxwikis.com/Unit_modding
- io_pdx_mesh: https://github.com/ross-g/io_pdx_mesh (binary `.mesh` layout used by `scanner.py`)
- JoroDox (format reference/viewer): https://github.com/DaanBroekhof/JoroDox
- pdx-asset-go (mesh/DDS reader prior art; tested on Vic3/EU5, not EU4): https://github.com/kaiser-chris/pdx-asset-go
- Steam guide "Eu4 Unit Modding Tutorial": https://steamcommunity.com/sharedfiles/filedetails/?id=1965958854
- Forum: "How to change unit models": https://forum.paradoxplaza.com/forum/threads/how-to-change-unit-models.1461268/
- Blender command-line/background scripting: https://docs.blender.org/api/current/info_tips_and_tricks.html
- DirectXTex / texconv: https://github.com/microsoft/DirectXTex/wiki/Getting-Started

#### M24. The standard rider pipeline: image → TRELLIS.2 → processing → game (2026-10-23)
The rider track's result (its development record is M23). The counterpart of M22 for riders, and the path for any soldier on the vanilla infantry skeleton.

**One command** (`ref3d/trellis.py`):
```
python -m eu4unitgen.ref3d.trellis run <image> --rider --name <subject> --rights "<declaration>" --ai-python <Windows AI python>
```
or `trellis.process_rider(<raw.glb>, <dir>)` on a TRELLIS.2 model. It writes `assets_in/<name>/trellis/game/vNNN/`: the body's geometry and textures, `parts/` (the lance, shield and plume geometry and textures; the sword if asked for), `manifest.json` (its `parts` are ready loadout part entries) and `deform/`.

**Inputs:** a front view of the soldier, arms a little out, weapons in hand, on a plain background; a name; the rights declaration (rule 8).

**1. Cut-out and TRELLIS.2:** as M22 (BiRefNet, then TRELLIS.2 in WSL2). About 5 minutes, ~280k faces.

**2. Processing** (Blender job `fresh_rider`, reusing `fresh_horse`'s direct-path helpers). The rider wears the base infantry body (`western_generic_01_type1`, 28 bones) and plays its `horse_*` ids (`cavalry_rider_*` animations) at the horse's `Saddle_Node` (§7.2).

| Step | What it does |
|---|---|
| Split | the lance: the column standing beside the body above its top, by its fitted axis and shaft radius (fingers kept at the grip, the blade and tassel taken). The shield: the plate on the other side, from its outermost points' plane, connected within its thickness |
| Scale and place | the helmet's top (not the crest's red) at 16.4, feet on the ground, centred |
| Slim | x and z towards vanilla's rider's waist and chest, at least 0.75; across the torso x scales, the arms shift in whole |
| Arms | each arm turned about its shoulder so the lance's grip points at `Right_hand_node` and the shield's at `Left_hand_node`; the weapons carried with their hands |
| Plume | cut off the body and rebuilt as a rigid `mesh_head` part (`plume_line` + `trellis.plume_part`): a gold finial on the crown and a crimson tube along the raw plume's centre line, rising then flowing back, clear of the helmet and cloak |
| Solid | flood fill (closing 0.2); a slot between the legs under the crotch; the cloth hanging under each arm outside the torso cut; the collar above the neck outside the helmet's radius cut |
| Decimate | quadric collapse to 2,600 (vanilla's rider body: 2,883), the slivers evened (3 passes) |
| Weights | nearest-surface transfer, then region rules: head (and anything rigid on it) above the neck; arms along their chains; the cloak behind the back on the spine by height; legs and skirt halves along the leg chains; 24 smoothing passes, 4 bones at most |
| Parts | each weapon closed, collapsed (lance 200, shield 400), unwrapped and baked, written in its node's frame as a `mesh_weapon` / `mesh_shield` part |
| Lance | along −Z from the grip (the rider's "down": levelled forward in game) |
| Shield | strapped to the left forearm: `rider_frames` measures `Left_hand_node`'s frame in the seated pose (frame 1 of `cavalry_rider_idle`); face outward, top up, half way to the elbow, lowered 1.5, at 0.85 |
| Sword (optional, off by default: `sword=True`) | `sword_part`: our own sword in its scabbard (pommel, grip, crossguard, scabbard with locket and chape) in the reference's colours, a `mesh_belt` part on the pelvis bone; on the side the shield doesn't cover; hanging back 65° along the horse's flank, clear of the caparison |
| Textures | `bake_colour` (the red cloth and the shield's face as the country colour's mask; gloss by colour class; the colour matched to the raw front render), `bake_horse` (normal and AO) |

**3. Checks:**
- the deformation check against vanilla's rider (idle, moving, attack; per group: head, arms, legs, torso);
- the gallery with the rider on its horse, and close-ups;
- the validator at staging.

**4. Game:**
- **The body:** the loadout's `riders` (geometry, textures, rights, base `western_generic_01_type1`): `core/fresh_mesh.apply_horses(kind="rider")` writes the .mesh on the base body's skeleton and points the cavalry rider entity's pdxmesh at it (rule `fresh_mesh`).
- **The parts:** the loadout slot `<tier>/rider` (part kinds with their own mesh).
- **Colours:** with a cloth mask, the horse's `unit_colours` give the set's tags without colours of their own the cloth red (BYZ: 255, 77, 70).

**Results:**

| | flips idle / moving / attack | worst stretch |
|---|---|---|
| ours | 32 / 33 / 41 (mostly torso and legs) | 2.4 / 2.1 / 2.8 |
| vanilla's rider | 12 / 0 / 12 | 7.2 / 4.1 / 8.4 |

The flips are 2.6–3.4× vanilla's in idle and attack (and 33 against vanilla's none in moving), and the worst stretch is lower than vanilla's. 2,600 body triangles; about 80 s of processing after TRELLIS.2. Test 31 staged (the finished cataphract).

**Known limits:**
- the arms are fitted to one pose (the hands' rest positions), elbows unbent;
- the cloak's hem is cut under the arms;
- the parts' normal maps are flat;
- the sword may touch the caparison;
- the flips are still 2.6–3.4× vanilla's in idle and attack;
- one-sided weapons only (lance right, shield left).

#### M23. The rider: image → TRELLIS.2 → processing → game (2026-10-23)
**Reference** (rule 8): the user named `assets_in/new_refs/cataphract_rider.png` ("the arms-out version, ChatGPT-generated from my original, I own the output"). That file isn't in the folder, so `cataphract_rider_v1.png` (a front view, arms slightly out, the lance in the right hand, a kite shield in the left) is used until it is. Recorded in `assets_in/cataphract_rider/subject.json`.

**Raw TRELLIS.2** (`assets_in/cataphract_rider/trellis/raw_sheet.png`): 1024_cascade, about 5 minutes, 4.5 GB VRAM, 278k faces.
- It matches the reference: the scale cuirass, gilded pauldrons and bracers, red cloak and skirt with gold trim and tassels, scale greaves, the helmet with its mail veil, the lance and the kite shield.
- The crest is spiky strands; the shield's crosses are blurred.

**Processing** (Blender job `fresh_rider`; `python -m eu4unitgen.ref3d.trellis run <image> --rider ...` or `trellis.process_rider`). The rider wears the infantry pdxmesh and plays its `horse_*` ids (`cavalry_rider_*` animations) at the horse's `Saddle_Node` (§7.2).

| Step | What it does | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Split | the lance: the column standing beside the body above its top; axis fitted, within 1.6 shaft radii (1.15 at the grip, 0.035 H round the blade and tassel). The shield: the outermost points on the other side give its plane; what is connected to them within the plate's thickness | the weapons come fused to the hands |
| Scale | the helmet's top (the highest point near the centre line that isn't the crest's red) at 16.4, feet on the ground, centred | the narrowest-slice neck took the crest for the head (15.7 tall) |
| Arms | each arm turned about its shoulder so the lance's grip points at `Right_hand_node` and the shield's at `Left_hand_node` (28° and 31°), the rest slid; each weapon carried with its hand | sliding the hands 4.6 and 5.9 units stretched the arms |
| Plume | smoothed on the closed solid; rigid on `head` | its lower half took the spine's weights and stretched |
| Solid | flood fill (closing 0.2), a slot between the legs under the crotch, the cloth hanging under each arm outside the torso cut | closed round both legs, the skirt was one block that tore when the seated legs spread; the cloak draped over the shield arm tore when the arm moved |
| Decimate | quadric collapse to 2,600 (vanilla's rider body: 2,883) | |
| Weights | nearest-surface transfer, then region rules: head above the neck; arms along their chains (within 1.2); the cloak behind the back on the spine by height; legs and skirt halves along the leg chains; 12 smoothing passes | nearest-surface alone: ~100 flips seated |
| Parts | the lance and shield each closed, collapsed (200 and 400 triangles), unwrapped, baked; written in their nodes' frames (the lance along −Z from the grip, the rider's "down", levelled forward in game; the shield's face along +X, top +Y) as `mesh_weapon` and `mesh_shield` parts | |
| Textures | `bake_colour` (the red cloth and the shield's face as the country colour's mask; gloss by colour class; the colour matched to the raw front render) and `bake_horse` | |

- **New code:** part kinds with their own mesh (`mesh_weapon`, `mesh_shield`: geometry and textures of their own, `build_parts` builder `mesh`); loadout `riders` (`fresh_mesh.apply_horses(kind="rider")`, rule `fresh_mesh` for both); `ref3d.trellis.process_rider`.
- **Byzantium's colour:** a game horse with a cloth mask carries `unit_colours`, and the set's tags without colours of their own get them (`export.pipeline.horse_unit_colours`). BYZ gets the cloth red (255, 77, 70) as its primary, instead of its map purple.

**Deformation** (flipped triangles; stretch p05–p95 / max):

| | idle | moving | attack |
|---|---|---|---|
| ours | 39 (head 5, arms 6, legs 15, torso 18); 0.51–1.17 / 2.9 | 28 (arms 3, legs 11, torso 15); 0.55–1.04 / 2.7 | 52 (head 11, arms 11, legs 11, torso 29); 0.54–1.15 / 4.1 |
| vanilla rider | 12; 0.68–0.94 / 7.2 | 0; 0.70–0.90 / 4.1 | 12; 0.65–1.01 / 8.4 |

The path went: nearest-surface weights 133 / 98 / 119 flips → leg slot → region weights → the cloak under the arms cut 59 / 39 / 71 → 12 smoothing passes 39 / 28 / 52. The worst stretch is lower than vanilla's; the flips are 3× vanilla's, mostly the torso, cloak and skirt.

**In-game test 29 STAGED** (`build/test29`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the gallery `gallery/sheet.png`, close and near rows):
- Tier 1: our rider on the cataphract. Tier 2: vanilla's rider on the cataphract. Tier 3: vanilla. Units are named "T29 ...".
- The validator found 0 errors and 2 warnings: the parts' flat normal maps, under the base-game normal-strength range.
- Seen in the gallery: seated at the saddle, the lance levelled forward, the shield on the left arm; the plume is a heavy red arc; the body is bulkier than vanilla's rider.
- **The user on test 29's gallery (2026-10-23), test 29 not run; fixed as general steps:**
  - **Plume:** cut off the closed solid (smoothed, it was a heavy red arc over the head). A `crest` part takes its place: a short arc (12° each side, 1.3 tall, 0.5 thick, fixed red 128, 22, 22), an upright tuft on the helmet.
  - **Shield strapped to the forearm** (on the rein hand it sat over the horse's neck, as C0c's did). Blender job `rider_frames` measures `Left_hand_node`'s frame in the seated pose, from frame 1 of `cavalry_rider_idle` on the base body: where the elbow, outward and up are in the part's own PDX coordinates. The left node's local +Z is outward, so the hoplon convention (+X) points a shield half backwards on a rider. The shield is turned face outward and top up, and centred half way to the elbow and 0.6 out.
  - **Slimmer:** x and z scaled towards vanilla's rider's waist and chest (half-width 1.69 against ours 2.82, the cloak's sides), to at least 0.75. Across the torso (|x| under 2) x scales; beyond, the arms shift in whole (scaled, the hands fell short of their nodes and the slide stretched the arms).
  - **Flips:** the low mesh evened (3 smoothing passes on the collapse's slivers) and the weights smoothed 24 times. Side slits in the skirt were tried and dropped (no gain).
    - The counts move by ~20 between equivalent meshes (each change re-triangulates the collapse); compare runs, not single numbers.
    - Runs (idle / moving / attack): v007 39 / 28 / 52 → with the plume cut and slim 0.7, v009 57 / 54 / 55 → slim 0.85 and 24 smoothing passes, v013 42 / 37 / 51 → plus evened, v015 39 / 34 / 38 → slim 0.75, v016 30 / 29 / 41.
- **Final rider v017:** 2,600 triangles, worst stretch 2.4 / 2.1 / 2.8 (vanilla's 7.2 / 4.1 / 8.4). Flips 30 (arms 8, legs 12, torso 15) / 29 (6, 12, 12) / 41 (10, 14, 23); vanilla's 12 / 0 / 12.
- **The reference:** the user saved the arms-out version as `cataphract_rider.png`. The folder holds `cataphract_rider.png.png`, pixel for pixel the `_v1` image used since test 29 (`_v1` no longer exists), so the raw model was not regenerated.
- **In-game test 30 STAGED** (`build/test30`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the gallery `gallery/sheet.png`):
  - Tier 1: our rider (v017) on the cataphract. Tier 2: vanilla's rider on it. Tier 3: vanilla. Units are named "T30 ...".
  - The validator found 0 errors and 3 warnings: the parts' flat normal maps, the shield's texel density, the tuft's shading.
  - Seen in the gallery: the shield on the left forearm, face outward, its cross showing; a small red tuft on the helmet; slimmer.
- **The user on test 30's gallery (2026-10-23), test 30 not run; fixed as general steps:**
  - The lance stays as it is (good for attacks).
  - **No plume:** the tuft read as a hooded blob. The cloak's collar is cut round the helmet (the solid's `carve_out`: above the neck, farther out than the helmet's steel radius, 1.13), so the head shows clean steel and its mail veil with the collar below.
  - **Sword:** a sword in its scabbard as a rigid belt part (`mesh_belt` on the pelvis bone, `sword_part`).
    - It is on the right hip, the side the shield doesn't cover (the reference hangs it on the shield's side).
    - Hanging down (25°), it ran inside the cataphract's caparison (about x 3.9 at the saddle). At 65° it lies back along the flank.
    - `rider_frames`' forward was backwards (the rider faces Blender −Y): fixed. The camera's elevation makes the far hip's sword look raised from the left; that is projection, not placement.
  - **Shield:** lowered 1.5 and at 0.85. Centred on the raised forearm, the kite's top stood over the helmet, and its rim showed as a ribbon over the head from behind.
- **In-game test 31 STAGED** (`build/test31`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the gallery `gallery/sheet.png`, the close-ups `gallery/closeups.png`: head, sword, shield):
  - Tier 1: the finished cataphract (TRELLIS.2 horse v012, rider v020). Tier 2: vanilla's rider on it. Tier 3: vanilla. Units are named "T31 ...".
  - The validator found 0 errors and 3 warnings (the parts' flat normal maps, the shield's texel density).
  - Flips 32 / 33 / 41.
- **The user's correction after test 31 (2026-10-24): the plume back.** Test 29's arc had the right shape but merged into the helmet and mail. Test 31 was not run. Fixed as general steps (`process_rider(plume="part", sword=False)`, now the default):
  - **Body:** the raw plume is cut off the body. Before this, `plume="part"` left the plume in the body, smoothed into the helmet.
  - **Centre line:** `fresh_rider.plume_line` takes the raw plume's centre line as shells by distance from the finial's top, with each shell's centroid and side-to-side spread. It stops before the helmet (its radius + 0.35) and the cloak (neck + 1.5).
  - **Finial:** it moves onto the low body's crown, and the line moves with it.
  - **Part:** `trellis.plume_part` builds our own geometry, a gold finial (socket, post, knob) and a crimson tube out of the knob. The tube is narrow at the base, then as wide as the raw plume (half width 0.3 to 0.55), tapering at the end. It is a rigid `mesh_head` part on the head bone.
  - A first try cut the raw plume mesh out with a gap round the helmet. It lost the rise and read as a flat lid.
- **In-game test 32 STAGED** (`build/test32`, rider v024; units named "T32 ..."):
  - The run sheet is `README.txt`; the head compare is `gallery/head_compare.png`.
  - The validator found 0 errors and 3 warnings.
  - Flips 32 / 33 / 41.
- **The user's test 32 notes (2026-10-24), test 32 not run; fixed as general rules (`core/gear.py`, pure Python):**
  - **Horsehair (plumes, crests, manes):** a flat blade along the hair's line (`horsehair_blade`), not a round tube.
    - The blade is tall across the line and thin side to side, and thins towards its top.
    - Spike columns between line points give a jagged top edge.
    - `strand_texture` paints strands along the length: a dark base with lighter streaks and a darker root.
    - The plume (`trellis.plume_part`) uses the reference's sampled reds: dark (86, 14, 22), light (150, 38, 52).
    - Applied so far: the rider's plume. The horse's mane and the `crest` part kind still use their own builders (TODO: move them onto the blade; the cataphract's mane is under its scale armour).
  - **Kite shield (shields):** `kite_shield` builds the reference's almond kite.
    - Shape: a rounded top over the widest point (18% above the centre), sides narrowing to a point, 0.52 as wide as tall.
    - Curved 0.1 W across and 0.04 H down. The gold rim is raised 0.05.
    - The emblem is painted at 4× and reduced, so it stays crisp: a cross with flared ends, its bar at the widest point, and a small cross in each quarter, clipped inside the rim.
    - The studded rim, the studs and the cross are raised in a height map turned into the normal map.
    - The field is on the country colour's mask.
  - **`trellis.kite_part`:** the kite replaces the raw shield, 0.88 of the raw shield's largest extent (5.6). It is strapped on `rider_frames`' `Left_hand_node` frame, 1.2 out and 0.3 up. With the raw disc's offsets (0.6 out, 1.5 down) its point sank into the caparison.
  - **The game camera:** it looks down at about 58°, so an upright kite looks about 1.5 times as tall as wide in the gallery and in game.
  - **`rider_frames` takes the .mesh's bind like `render_unit`:** the identity on `western_generic_01_type1`, checked.
- **Detail experiment (the user):** the cataphract at three budgets.
  - `process(cfg)` and `process_rider(cfg)` take `target_tris`. Weight smoothing scales with the count (horse 12/24/48, rider 24/48/96): at a fixed pass count the denser meshes' flips tripled (horse 11,327 tris: 167 / 180 / 302).
  - Built into `assets_in/cataphract_budget/{horse,rider}`:

| tier | horse tris | rider tris | horse flips | rider flips | processing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2,927 | 2,600 | 11 / 15 / 46 | 32 / 33 / 41 | 71 s + 78 s |
| 2 | 5,727 | 5,200 | 29 / 31 / 62 | 72 / 65 / 71 | 83 s + 91 s |
| 3 | 11,327 | 10,400 | 76 / 70 / 143 | 170 / 155 / 185 | 151 s + 107 s |

  - Flips grow about in step with the triangle count.
  - At map zoom the three look the same in the gallery; up close the higher budgets are a little smoother in outline.
  - Textures are equal across the tiers.
- **In-game test 33 STAGED** (`build/test33`; tiers 1–3 are the three budgets; units named "T33 ...").
  - The run sheet `README.txt` includes a frame-rate check with 30+ regiments on screen.
  - The validator found 0 errors and 13 warnings: the budget on tiers 2–3, the parts' texture-quality notes once per tier.
- **The user on test 33 (2026-10-24):** the shield is fine; only the plume still looks off. The user asked for hair cards if EU4 has a cutout shader for unit meshes.
  - **Checked against the base game (`gfx/FX/pdxmesh.shader`):**
    - Both blend states have `AlphaTest = no`.
    - The only alpha clip in pdxmesh is `PixelPdxMeshAlphaBlendShadow` (`clip(vColor.a - 0.5)`), a shadow pass.
    - `PdxMeshAlphaBlend`, `PdxMeshAlphaBlendSkinned` and `PdxMeshAlphaBlendNoZWriteSkinned` blend by the diffuse's alpha (SRC_ALPHA / INV_SRC_ALPHA). Only 16 African city meshes and `Mapitems/selection.mesh` use them; no unit does.
    - `tree.shader` cuts out (`clip(vDiffuseColor.a - 0.5)`), but it is the map trees' own shader (effects `tree`, `treeunlit`, `treeshadow`). Nothing shows a unit pdxmesh can use it (rule 6).
  - **Known limit: no cutout for unit meshes.** Plumes, crests and manes stay solid horsehair blades (`core.gear`), with no see-through gaps between strands. Alpha-blended cards (`PdxMeshAlphaBlend`) are untried for units; expect sorting artefacts where cards cross, and no country colour.
- **In-game test 34 STAGED** (`build/test34`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the head close-up next to the reference is `gallery/head_closeup.png`):
  - Tier 1: the cataphract at the standard budget (horse `cataphract_budget/horse/v001`, rider `cataphract_budget/rider/v001`: blade plume, kite shield). Tier 2: vanilla's rider on it. Tier 3: vanilla. Units are named "T34 ...".
  - The validator found 0 errors and 3 warnings.
  - The cataphract is done; next is packaging.
- **The user on test 34 (2026-10-24): two last touches.** The helmet gold: the reference's tall gold tube finial with the plume rising out of it, plus the brow band, nasal guard and ribs, in the chanfron's gold. The shield: part of the design, not a clean sticker (the caparison's deep red and aged gold, wear and shading, studs on the rim, a little darker).
  - **Helmet (`trellis.plume_part`, `helmet_gold`; `core.gear.lathe`, `surface_band`, `stud`, `raycast_far`):**
    - The finial is a turned solid (`HELMET["finial"]`, 12 sides): a flange on the dome, a waisted neck with a ring, a flared lip. It is about 0.85 helmet radii tall, taken from the reference's proportions. The blade plume starts inside the lip.
    - The bands are ray-cast from the dome's centre (the crown less a helmet radius) onto the low body's outer surface: a brow ring with 12 rivets, four ribs from the brow up to the finial, and a nasal guard tapering down the front. They are raised strips (0.05 helmet radii proud), using the plume texture's gold strip, which is shaded across its width.
    - The gold is the horse's: the median of its texture's gold texels, (147, 116, 80) (the chanfron's). Before this it was (205, 165, 70).
    - Cost: the head part grew from about 600 to 1,540 triangles.
  - **Shield (`core.gear.kite_shield`):**
    - The colours are the caparison's: its red (94, 26, 23), the horse bake's head-band red; its gold (147, 116, 80).
    - `KITE_WEAR`: low-frequency grime and grain; edge darkening towards the rim; a top-to-bottom light falloff; 60 light scratches; 50 chips worn out of the emblem; a dark painted edge round the emblem. Overall it is about 12% darker than the caparison.
    - Studs: all of them now (every other one before), larger, each with a shadow and a highlight.
    - The field's mask grey is computed against the set's colour (255, 77, 70), the horse's, not the rider bake's (255, 105, 92).
  - **Found: the helmet wasn't rigid.** `fresh_rider`'s weight smoothing (24 passes at the standard budget) carried the neck's weights into the dome, so it was about 70% on the head. `trellis.pin_head` now pins everything above y 14.45 back onto `head`, fully above 14.75. It is pure Python, runs on `low_geometry.json` in `process_rider` after the job, and is applied to v004/v005.
  - **Found: the rider animation's scale (UNVERIFIED in game).**
    - `cavalry_rider_*` scales every rider bone to 0.805 at frame 1, measured in Blender (infantry `polearm_twohanded_idle`: 1.0). The body shrinks with it.
    - `render_unit` places attached parts without the bone's scale, so a head part built in body units came out 1.24 times too big and too far from the head bone. The finial floated about 0.35 over the dome; test 34's cube did the same, unnoticed at the game camera's angle.
    - The "attached scale" fact above (parts keep their own pdxmesh scale) was found on infantry, whose animations have no scale. Whether the game applies an animated bone scale to an attachment is unknown.
    - `plume_part(scale=)` scales the whole part about the head bone. `process_rider` uses `RIDER_ANIM_SCALE` = 0.805, the gallery's model, until test 35 decides.
    - The lance and kite are positioned from `rider_frames`' posed positions (already scaled), but sized in body units. If the game applies the scale, they come out about 20% smaller and closer to the hand than in the gallery; the user approved them in game either way.
- **In-game test 35 STAGED** (`build/test35`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the close-ups are `gallery/head_shield_closeup.png`):
  - Tier 1: rider `cataphract_budget/rider/v004`, head part ×0.805 (right if the game ignores the bone scale; it matches the gallery).
  - Tier 2: v005, the same but unscaled (right if the game applies it).
  - Tier 3: vanilla.
  - Horse `cataphract_budget/horse/v001` on both custom tiers. Units are named "T35 ...".
  - The validator found 0 errors and 6 warnings, the parts' texture-quality notes once per tier.
  - The tier whose finial sits on the helmet and whose bands lie on it sets `RIDER_ANIM_SCALE` (0.805 or 1.0) and closes the UNVERIFIED note.
- **Test 35 approved in game (the user, 2026-10-24):** "the cataphract is done (horse + rider)". The user didn't name a tier, so tier 1 is kept: rider `cataphract_budget/rider/v004`, the head part at `RIDER_ANIM_SCALE` 0.805, which is what the pipeline builds. The horse is `cataphract_budget/horse/v001`. Together they are the regression set's reference result (Track P1.4). Whether the game scales attachments by the animated bone scale is still UNVERIFIED: only tier 1 was approved.

### Track P: the app, built from what we learned (PLANNED 2026-10-24)
The user's brief after test 35: turn every lesson into a built-in rule, give it one flow in the app, tidy the repo and package it. Work in phases and commit after each step. Stop only for the user's decisions.

- **P1. App logic**
  1. **Audit M15–M24:** every fix becomes a general rule in code. One-offs are made general: the cataphract's colours, helmet, kite and emblem were hard-coded.
  2. **Parts are never placeholders.** Each part is cropped from the reference with SAM 2 (a point prompt where the split part projects), generated alone by TRELLIS.2, then decimated, baked (colour and normal), aligned to the split part and attached to its bone (`ref3d/part_gen.py`).
     - Fallback: `core.gear`'s procedural shapes, textured from the crop's colours.
     - Per-part check: the part rendered next to its crop, with silhouette IoU and colour distance; mismatches are flagged.
  3. **Known fixes:** a registry (`core/known_fixes.py`: the rule, the case that taught it, where it lives, the test that checks it). The SPEC list is written from it, and a test checks that every entry's test exists.
  4. **Regression set** (`ref3d/regress.py`): horse 2, the cataphract horse and the cataphract rider are rebuilt from their raw `.glb`s after every pipeline change. Their quality numbers are compared with `regression/baseline.json` (test 35 for the cataphract); anything worse is flagged.
  5. **Quality report** per model, in plain language: silhouette against the reference, deformation against vanilla, parts kept/rebuilt/lost, texture use, and brightness and contrast at map zoom against vanilla.
- **P2. App**
  1. **One flow:** image → name + rights → type (horse / rider; the foot soldier is shown as roadmap) and unit slot → Generate → staged progress with estimates → sheet next to the reference → quality report → Add to mod.
  2. **Edits that rebuild only their step:**
     - plume and tassels on/off;
     - emblem (upload or choose);
     - cloth colour and the country-colour mask;
     - brightness;
     - shield side;
     - hem length.
  3. **Input guide**, with warnings before the run.
  4. **Pre-run checks** (free RAM, EU4 closed, WSL and the AI weights, disk space), each with its fix.
  5. **Runs:**
     - They resume after the app closes or a drive drops out.
     - GPU stages wait while EU4 runs.
  6. **Export:**
     - a normal mod;
     - an "achievement-safe" mod: graphics only, a graphical culture's units re-dressed via `version`. Which files touch the checksum is read from the install's `checksum_manifest.txt`.
  7. **Add to mod:**
     - It writes into a mod folder the user picks.
     - If Windows blocks it (Controlled Folder Access), the app says exactly what to copy.
     - The unit names include the subject's name.
  8. **Errors** in plain words: what happened and what to do, with the details hidden behind a button.
- **P3. Cleanliness**
  1. **Repo:** the abandoned paths (hand-built master, cloth sim, precision fitting, procedural bodies, the self-reading M18 pipeline) move to `archive/` (outside the package); what the live path still uses is moved out first. TripoSG becomes optional.
  2. **Data:** `build/` and `assets_in/` keep the references, the regression set and test 35. Old tests and scratch go to `D:\eu4unitgen_archive`; the space freed is reported.
  3. **Settings:** one file for paths (`core/config.py`; EU4, mod folder, AI environment, WSL, Blender), editable in the app.
  4. **Docs:** a user README (install, first run, a good reference) and `docs/DEVELOPER.md` (the pipeline, where each rule lives, the regression set).
  5. **Licences:** every test passes; all model and tool licences are listed in one place (SPEC §9 and the app's About page).
- **P4. Packaging (Windows)**
  - **The package:** an installer that needs no terminal. It is built from Python's own venv and PowerShell, with no new tools. A one-file `.exe` would need Inno Setup or PyInstaller, which is the user's call.
  - **The app's Setup screen** checks or sets up WSL, the TRELLIS.2 environment, the weights and Blender, and guides the user step by step where admin rights, a reboot or a Hugging Face licence acceptance are needed.
  - **Tested as a fresh user:** install, open, horse 2 from its image end to end, staged.
- **Roadmap (later, not now):**
  - import a 3D model file (with a licence check);
  - the horse-girl foot unit;
  - infantry;
  - other unit types.

#### Track P: results (2026-10-24)
- **P1.1-1.3: the audit and the known fixes.** Every fix from M15-M24 is a general rule in code, each with the case that taught it, its code and its test (`core/known_fixes.py`, 46 rules; `tests/test_known_fixes.py`). One-offs made general:
  - the cloth, head band, emblem panel, country mask and crest found by the subject's own dominant colour (`blender/jobs/_colour.py`), not a fixed red; a horse's coat kept apart from its cloth;
  - the shield, plume and helmet gold read from the reference (`ref3d/part_gen.py`); the cataphract's colours (`KITE_COLOURS`, `FINIAL_GOLD`, `PLUME_DARK/LIGHT`) are only defaults;
  - parts' colours moved onto the unit's palette (`core/palette.py`: the horse's gold, cloth, steel);
  - the reins rebuilt (`_reins.py`, on vanilla's `Rem` bone) instead of lost;
  - every part with a normal map; the sword textured; the flat-colour tuft dropped;
  - the rider's legs moved out by the horse's extra barrel width (`trellis.leg_spread`): seated, the cataphract rider's lower body inside the horse went 23% -> 10% (vanilla 9%), measured from the gallery's posed meshes (`quality.legs_clear`);
  - shield side, plume and tassels as options; one settings file for every path (`core/config.py`).
- **P1.2: parts from the reference.** Each part is located in the reference (the split part projected, mirrored or not; the cataphract: fit 0.744), cut out with SAM 2, its occluders trimmed (a gripping hand) and filled from the mirror side, stood upright. TRELLIS.2 makes it from the crop (one model load, cached by the crop's hash: 3.4 minutes for the shield). Blender `fresh_part` faces a flat part along its thinnest axis, painted side out, wide end up as in the crop. Then it is baked, its colours put on the palette, and its emblem projected crisp from the crop's view. The check is the part drawn next to its crop: the cataphract's TRELLIS.2 shield scored outline 0.837-0.845, colour distance 12.5-13.5, and passed. The fallback is the procedural shield traced from the crop (outline, emblem, colours: 0.886 / 18.8). Plumes stay horsehair blades, coloured from their crop; the helmet's finial, brow band, nasal and ribs are read from its crop.
- **P1.4: the regression set** (`ref3d/regress.py`, `regression/baseline.json`; test 35 and horse 2's untouched run as the baseline). Run 1 (2026-10-24): nothing worse; the new measures added to the baseline.
- **P1.5: the quality report** (`ref3d/quality.py`). Six measures, each with a verdict:
  - the outline against the reference, relative to TRELLIS.2's own model's overlap (a rider's on its central band, with its parts masked out);
  - deformation against vanilla;
  - parts kept, rebuilt or lost;
  - texture use;
  - brightness and contrast at map zoom;
  - seated clearance.
- **P2: the app.**
  - `gui/unit_page.py`: the one flow, image -> name and rights -> horse or rider and slot -> Generate -> staged progress with estimates -> the result sheet next to the reference -> the quality report -> edits -> Add to mod.
  - The run engine (`ref3d/flow.py`) runs detached and resumes; GPU stages wait while EU4 runs. Edits re-run only build (TRELLIS.2 never) or only finish: on horse 2, brightness re-ran finish in 0.1 s and the preview, keeping its 80 s build.
  - Pre-run checks (`core/checks.py`), the input guide (`core/guide.py`), plain errors with the details behind a button (`core/errors.py`).
  - **Add to mod** (`export/modexport.py`):
    - normal: its own mod named after the unit, 46 files, 16 of them in checksummed folders;
    - achievement-safe: only gfx/interface, the culture's cavalry entity and rider rewritten from the unit's own entities with `version` 20, 0 checksummed files. UNVERIFIED in game: a mod entity with a higher version replacing a base-game culture's.
    - The checksum folders and extensions are read from the install's `checksum_manifest.txt` (13 entries: common, events, missions, decisions, history, map, map/random).
  - Setup and About screens (`gui/setup_page.py`, `core/setup.py`, `core/licences.py`); the io_pdx_mesh download URL checked against its 0.91 release (`blender-io_pdx_mesh.zip`, an extension package).
  - Horse 2 ran end to end through the new flow: cut-out 41 s, build 80 s, preview 4 minutes (TRELLIS.2 reused).
- **P3: cleanliness.**
  - The abandoned approaches moved to `archive/`: the hand-built master (E-A/E-B/E-C/E-D, the master assembly), the self-reading and cloak pipelines (`auto`, `parts`, `s1`, `game`, `job`), TripoSG, 15 AI jobs, 12 Blender jobs and helpers, and the M19 horse screen. `fresh_horse.py`'s procedural, anatomy and fitted-model sources are gone too (2,029 -> 1,318 lines). The live pieces were extracted first (`ref3d/runstate.py`; `trellis.next_version`, `ai_paths`, `stage`; `unitstage.TEST`).
  - Data: old tests, bake-offs and scratch moved to `D:\eu4unitgen_archive\2026-10-24` (14 GB); 13.5 GB freed on C:. Kept: the references, the regression set, test 35 (its build and source versions) and horse 2's app run.
  - The WSL setup scripts moved into the package (`setup_scripts/`).
  - The user README and `docs/DEVELOPER.md` were rewritten.
