#### Track P4: the Windows package and the fresh-user test (2026-10-24/25)
- **The package** (`python packaging/windows/build_package.py` -> `dist/eu4unitgen-0.1.0-windows.zip`, 0.5 MB):
  - **Contents:** the app's wheel, Install.cmd/install.ps1, Uninstall.cmd/uninstall.ps1 and README.txt. No game files, no weights, no third-party binaries. The wheel carries the WSL setup scripts (`setup_scripts/`).
  - **Install.cmd** needs no terminal; its questions are message boxes. It finds Python 3.11+ (or offers Python 3.12 through winget), makes a venv in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\eu4unitgen\app`, installs the wheel (PySide6, Jinja2 and Pillow come from PyPI), and adds Desktop and Start-menu shortcuts.
  - **Uninstall** keeps the settings, the runs and the AI setup.
  - **Licence:** the project's licence isn't decided (the user, 2026-10-25). No LICENSE file is committed or packaged until it is; `pyproject.toml` and `core/licences.py` still say GPL-3.0, as since Phase 0.
- **The app's first run** opens Setup while the EU4 install, the AI environment or Blender is missing. Setup finds and saves the EU4 install (Steam's library list), Blender 4.2 and the mod folder by itself. The steps for the AI side each run in a window of their own (`core/setup.py`).
- **The fresh-user test, on the author's machine:**
  - **Install:** from the zip, into a new prefix, with an empty settings folder and without the developer's environment variables. 17 s with pip's cache; a new machine downloads about 260 MB.
  - **Setup:** found EU4, Blender and the mod folder. The AI environment and folder were pointed at the ones already on this PC (D:\eu4unitgen_ai), as Setup's Browse would. The AI install steps were not re-run: they need about an hour, about 20 GB of downloads and the Linux password. Their commands were checked (the io_pdx_mesh URL against its release), not executed.
  - **The run:** horse 2 from its image, through the installed app's own flow (detached). Cut-out 31 s; TRELLIS.2 load 117 s, generate 153 s, export 35 s; build 68 s; preview 3.5 min. Quality "Usable": outline 64% against the raw model's 74%; flips 19/36/60 against vanilla's 13/8/32; tail and reins rebuilt; texture 52%; map zoom 13% brighter than vanilla.
  - **Found and fixed by this test:**
    - Staging failed for a fresh user: the command-line tool only read `EU4_PATH`. The EU4 install and Blender are now read from the settings file everywhere.
    - TRELLIS.2's own timings overwrote the stage's time.
    - The checks box didn't resize.
    - Setup's fields started blank.
    - Add to mod didn't fall back to staging on the writer's own error.
  - **Add to mod (normal):** Controlled Folder Access blocked the mod folder, so it was staged (30 files, 16 in checksummed folders) with the copy steps shown. Unit names: "Horse2 Fresh Cavalry I".
- **Found by the gallery, not the regression set:** the generalised cloth rule turned the cataphract's country colour orange (255 155 112 against test 35's 255 77 70).
  - Causes: a plain ±25° window took the copper trim; worse, the head band took the head's own dominant hue (the chanfron's copper), and that colour cleans the caparison panels.
  - Fixed: the cloth window never enters the orange-to-gold band unless the cloth itself is orange; the head band uses the cloth's colour.
  - The regression set now measures the country colour (Lab, within 8) and the mask's share (within 0.04) against test 35's.
- **Hem length:** first try left shards at the legs (any grey texel counted as cloth: the silver scale and the legs too). Now: the country mask, and only whole cloth triangles. The effect is subtle on a caparison that is one shape with the legs.
- **Shield side:** right forearm works, the lance mirrored into the left hand. The cavalry animations raise the right arm, so a right-side shield sits high behind the shoulder (said in its tooltip).
- **Regression run 2026-10-25 (all fixes):** nothing worse than test 35 or horse 2. The cataphract's country colour is 255 79 72 (test 35: 255 77 70); the rider's is 255 105 92 as before.
- **The shield (the user's decision, 2026-10-25): test 35's kite stays.** Sheet: `build/shield_cmp/shield_compare.png` (close-up, game camera, map zoom).
  - The TRELLIS.2 shield passed its crop check (outline 0.84, colour 12.5) but is not better. Its projected emblem breaks up close, its rim is plain, and it reads the same at the game camera and map zoom.
  - The check doesn't measure emblem crispness yet: a next step.
- **Package rebuilt** with every fix: `dist/eu4unitgen-0.1.0-windows.zip`.

#### Track P4b: the packaging upgrade (2026-10-25)
- **Why no single setup.exe (yet):** Smart App Control is on and enforcing on the author's PC (`VerifiedAndReputablePolicyState=1`); it refuses any unsigned `.exe`, which is what PyInstaller and Inno Setup produce. The user's decision: polish the PowerShell installer now; keep the `.exe` build scripts ready for a signed build later.
- **Signing options (for a later signed `.exe`):**
  - **Azure Trusted Signing** (Microsoft's signing service, about $10 a month): the cheapest path. It needs an identity check, and individuals must have a few years of verifiable history.
  - **A classic OV code-signing certificate** (about $200-400 a year, hardware token or cloud HSM since 2023): works anywhere, and builds reputation with SmartScreen over time.
  - **EV certificates** no longer skip SmartScreen's reputation check (Microsoft changed this in 2024), so they aren't worth the extra cost here.
  - With either: sign `eu4unitgen.exe` and `eu4unitgen-setup.exe` (signtool, SHA-256, timestamped).
- **The `.exe` scripts (not run; PyInstaller and Inno Setup aren't installed):** `packaging/windows/exe/eu4unitgen.spec` (a one-folder build, no console, the icon, the job scripts collected as files) and `eu4unitgen.iss` (per-user install, Start menu and optional Desktop, licence page, an uninstaller that asks about your work). TODO before use: `core/setup.app_python()` makes the AI venv from `sys.executable`, which in a frozen build is the `.exe`; ship the NuGet Python beside it.
- **The installer today** (`Install.cmd` -> `install.ps1`, a WinForms wizard; nothing typed):
  - pages: welcome and the GPL-3.0 text, location (default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\eu4unitgen`, no admin rights) with Start-menu and Desktop shortcut boxes, progress with live output, finish (start the app).
  - **No Python needed:** python.org's NuGet build of Python 3.12.10 is in the package (14.5 MB). It is unpacked into `<app>\python`; its `python.exe` must be validly signed by the PSF (checked when the package is built and again at install), which Smart App Control accepts. pip then installs the app's wheel and PySide6, Pillow and Jinja2 (about 260 MB download, 693 MB on disk).
  - shortcuts start `pythonw.exe -m eu4unitgen.gui` with the app's icon. An entry in Windows' Apps list (this user's: HKCU) carries the name, version, icon, size and the uninstaller.
  - **The uninstaller** (Settings > Apps > Uninstall, or Uninstall.cmd) removes the app, its shortcuts and its Apps entry. It asks, each unticked by default, before deleting your work (with its size), the AI folder (with its size), the TRELLIS.2 environment in WSL, and the settings and logs.
- **The icon** (`packaging/icon/make_icon.py`): a gold helmet with a red horsehair crest on a crimson tile, 16-256 px. Used for the window, the taskbar (its own AppUserModelID), the shortcuts and the Apps list.
- **The file layout:**

  | What | Where (installed app) | A source checkout |
  |---|---|---|
  | the app | `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\eu4unitgen` (`python\`, the icon, the uninstaller) | the repo |
  | your work | `Documents\eu4unitgen\` with `references\` (each image copied in), `projects\`, `result sheets\`, `exported mods\` | `assets_in/`, `build/` |
  | settings and logs | `%APPDATA%\eu4unitgen\settings.json`, `logs\` | the same |
  | AI environment and weights | the folder chosen in the setup wizard (default `eu4unitgen_ai` on the drive with most free space) | `D:\eu4unitgen_ai` |
- **The first-launch setup wizard** (`gui/setup_wizard.py`; the steps and their commands in `core/setup.py`). It opens by itself while EU4, Blender or the AI environment is missing, and from Setup's "Open the setup wizard". Eight steps, each with a ✔/○ state, a button, a progress bar and live output:
  1. Blender 4.2 LTS: found, or the official 4.2.23 MSI downloaded in the app and installed through Windows' permission prompt.
  2. io_pdx_mesh 0.91: downloaded and installed with Blender's own `extension install-file`.
  3. The AI folder: a folder chooser; free space shown per drive.
  4. WSL: `wsl --install --no-distribution` through the permission prompt; then a restart.
  5. Ubuntu 26.04: `--no-launch`, then started as its root user, so there is **no Linux user or password to make** (the old flow asked for one). `.wslconfig` (20 GB) is written if missing.
  6. TRELLIS.2: the code at commit 75fbf01, the build tools and CUDA 12.9 (as WSL's root), then `trellis2_setup.sh` (~40 min).
  7. The Windows AI environment: a venv from the app's own Python, PyTorch cu126 and the cut-out/SAM 2 libraries.
  8. The weights: BiRefNet (the exact URL rembg's own code uses), then TRELLIS.2, its decoder, DINOv3 and SAM 2.1. DINOv3 is gated: buttons open its licence page and Hugging Face's token page; the token is pasted, used for that download only and never stored.
- **Two TRELLIS.2 candidates per subject** (seeds 42 and 43 in one model load), scored by outline from 12 sides; the better is built and the other can be picked with the "3D model" edit. Horse 2, 2026-10-25: 42 → 0.826, 43 → 0.766; 42 built; the whole run took about 15 minutes. **Limit:** the score is outline only, rendered untextured. Seed 42 alone gave the same outline (0.824) and a near-identical texture (median ΔE to the reference 12.6 vs 11.6), but its mane was a shade darker and closer to the reference's near-black. A colour term in the score is a next step.
- **Tests:** `test_setup_plans_need_no_terminal` (every step has commands or a download, no `-NoExit`, no `sudo`, Ubuntu set up as root, admin steps marked, BiRefNet's URL, the pinned TRELLIS.2 commit).
- **Fresh-user test:** the package installed quietly into a new folder (`install.ps1 -Quiet -NoSystem`, which skips the shortcuts and the Apps entry on the author's PC): Python unpacked and its signature checked, pip, the app, the libraries and the start check all passed, in 30 s with pip's cache. **Not yet:** the wizard window, the shortcuts, the Apps entry and the uninstaller on a clean Windows. They wait for Windows Sandbox, which is enabled but needs a restart. The Sandbox has no GPU and no WSL, so the AI steps can't be tested there.

#### Track P5: one self-contained folder (the user, 2026-10-25)
- **The layout** (`core/layout.py`): everything under ONE folder chosen at install, default `D:\EU4 Unit Generator` (no D: drive: the fixed drive with most free space):

  | Folder | What |
  |---|---|
  | `app` | the program, its own signed Python (python.org NuGet 3.12.10), `layout.json` (the marker), the uninstaller, the icon, LICENSE |
  | `tools` | portable Blender 4.2.23 with a `portable` folder: its settings and io_pdx_mesh stay inside (verified: `resource_path('USER')` is `tools\blender\portable`) |
  | `ai` | the Windows AI environment (`ai_env`), the TRELLIS.2 code, `weights\`, `rembg_models\`, `hf_home\` (Hugging Face's cache) |
  | `wsl` | the Linux disk, registered as the WSL distribution `EU4UnitGen` |
  | `work` | `references\`, `projects\`, `result sheets\`, `exported mods\` |
  | `settings` | `settings.json`, `gui.ini` (the window's state: no longer the registry), `logs\`, `tmp\` |
- **How the root is found:** `EU4UNITGEN_ROOT`; else the installed app's own folder (a parent holding `app\layout.json`); else a checkout's `.eu4unitgen-root` file (git-ignored: the developer's checkout uses the installed tools, AI parts and settings; its work stays in `assets_in`/`build`). No root: the older paths (`%APPDATA%`, Documents).
- **Nothing else on the PC** but the shortcuts and the Apps entry. At start the app points TEMP/TMP, pip's cache and `HF_HOME` into the root (`layout.use_root_temp`; day-old leftovers in `settings\tmp` are cleared). Two exceptions are Windows' own: the WSL feature, and `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig` (WSL reads it only there; written only if missing). The marker records whether the app registered the distribution and wrote `.wslconfig`, so the uninstaller removes only those.
- **WSL commands quote every path** (`trellis.wsl_job_cmd`; the root has spaces). The wizard installs Ubuntu with `wsl --install -d Ubuntu-26.04 --location <root>\wsl --name EU4UnitGen` (WSL 3.0.3 supports both flags).
- **The download** (`build_package.py`): one zip unzipping to `EU4 Unit Generator\` with only `Install EU4 Unit Generator.cmd`, `README.txt`, `LICENSE.txt` and `files\`. Downloaded (internet-marked) scripts still run in PowerShell's full language mode under Smart App Control (tested with a hand-set Zone.Identifier), so the Windows Forms installer works there.
- **eu4unitgen-setup.exe:** Inno Setup 6.7.3 (portable, in the checkout's `.tools\`) builds a classic wizard (licence, folder, shortcuts) around the same `install.ps1`: one install logic, one uninstaller. It isn't signed, so Smart App Control refuses it; it's for PCs with Smart App Control off. (The PyInstaller spec in `packaging/windows/exe/` is no longer needed: the embedded Python replaces a frozen build.)
- **What signing would cost and change:**
  - **Azure Trusted Signing**, about $10 a month (Basic). It signs with a Microsoft-issued certificate, so Smart App Control and SmartScreen accept the exe once it has reputation. It needs an identity check: organisations with 3+ years of history, or individuals in supported countries (US/Canada as of 2025; check the current terms before buying).
  - **A classic OV code-signing certificate**, about $200-400 a year, on a hardware token or cloud HSM (required since 2023). It works the same way, and SmartScreen reputation builds with downloads.
  - **What changes:** with a signed setup.exe (and signed `install.ps1`/`uninstall.ps1`), Smart App Control users could use the exe; the zip stays as a fallback. Nothing in the layout or the app changes. Add the `SignTool=` line in `setup.iss` and sign at build time.
- **The uninstaller** (`<root>\app\uninstall.ps1`, from Windows' Apps list): refuses while the app runs. It asks once (work kept unless ticked), then removes the shortcuts, the Apps entry, `wsl --unregister EU4UnitGen` (only if the marker says the app registered it), `.wslconfig` (same rule), and everything in the root except `work\` if kept.
- **The author's PC migrated** (2026-10-25), by moving, not downloading again:
  - the app installed into `D:\EU4 Unit Generator`; the shortcuts and Apps entry now point there;
  - Blender 4.2 copied from Program Files into `tools\blender` (6 s); io_pdx_mesh copied and enabled in its portable preferences;
  - the AI parts moved within D: (renames): TRELLIS.2, the four weights, BiRefNet, CPython 3.10, the environment (`triposg_env` renamed `ai_env`, its `pyvenv.cfg` repointed); torch 2.14.1 and CUDA work from there;
  - the distribution "Ubuntu" exported as a VHD into `wsl\` (37.4 GB, 59 s) and registered in place as `EU4UnitGen`: the same user (`/etc/wsl.conf`), the TRELLIS.2 environment and the GPU work;
  - the settings written into `settings\`; the marker records `EU4UnitGen` and `.wslconfig` (written for this project in October) as the app's;
  - **the regression set through the new root: nothing got worse** (run 20261025-102411).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_layout.py` (every path inside the root, Blender and the distribution from the root, temp and caches inside it, the plans stay inside it, WSL commands quote spaces, the Blender zip's checksum).

### Track U: the UI and flow update (the user, 2026-10-28)
The brief: make the app as hand-holding as possible; the test is "could someone who has never seen the app make a unit without asking?"
- **The window** (`gui/app.py`): a top bar on every screen (Home · New unit · My units · Setup · Help), ticks for EU4, Blender and the AI (quick checks every 30 s; click: Setup), the version.
- **Home** (`gui/home.py`): "What should your soldiers look like?" leading into the picture pipeline: Soldier, Cavalry, Artillery (shown, off: planned below); drop a picture (wide: a horse, else a soldier); the text box kept, off: "Describe it (coming later: text to image)"; "Show me an example", the tour, the latest units.
- **New unit** (`gui/new_unit.py`): one action per screen, "Step N of 4", each with what happens and why; Next only when the step is done, the reason beside it.
  - Soldier or horse: the picture (the guide for its kind, its good and bad drawings, the picture's own checks) → name and rights (the in-game name shown; the example "Original character I made with ChatGPT; I own the output" one click away) → the tier (four cards with the tech and year) and the country colour → make it (the checks with their fixes, then the stages with their explanations, a bar and the time left).
  - Cavalry: pick the horse, then the rider, from My units, or make either right there (its own steps, then back, picked) → name and tier → make it. The cavalry run waits for its horse and soldier by itself.
- **The kinds** (`ref3d/flow.py`): `soldier` (one person on the infantry skeleton: on foot the infantry, so also the gun crew; can ride), `horse`, `cavalry` (a finished horse and soldier: build, finish, preview only; the soldier rebuilt for that horse), `rider` (older runs).
  - The soldier on foot: loadout `soldiers` → the infantry entity (`naming.fresh_soldier_*`), its pdxmesh keeping the infantry's own animation ids over the base body's; an own shield means the spear_shield stance, an own weapon alone polearm_twohanded (`loadout.RoleLoadout.resolved_stance`); its deformation checked under that stance's animations; rule `fresh_mesh` checks it. UNVERIFIED in game.
  - Add to mod for infantry (achievement-safe re-dresses `<gfx>_INFANTRY_<tier>`).
- **Runs:** one unit on the graphics card at a time (`flow.gpu_busy`: the next waits its turn); a turntable in the preview (12 frames through the game's close camera, `unitstage.turntable`).
- **My units** (`gui/my_units.py`): a gallery (picture, name, kind, state) and each unit's page: the turntable, the result sheet, the quality report in plain words, the progress while it runs, Resume, Edit the look, Use as rider / Use for cavalry, Add to mod, Delete (inside the projects folder only; asks first).
- **Help** (`gui/help_page.py`): the example, the tour, the picture guide, questions and answers, colours, Check for updates, About, the logs.
  - **The example** (`core/example.py`): the cataphract's three runs (horse, soldier, cavalry) walked through in ten steps with their real files and numbers, read from this computer's runs (their renders show the game's own bodies, so they are never bundled). **Its pictures are bundled** (`core/example_pictures/`: `cataphract_rider.png`, `cataphract_side_clean.png`, `horse2_light_cavalry.png`, with `RIGHTS.txt`; generated by the user with ChatGPT, owned by the user, bundling allowed 2026-10-28; the original reference sheet is not). Without the runs the Example shows the pictures and how to make it; New unit's picture step has "Use the example picture" (the rights filled in).
  - **The tour** (`gui/tour.py`): the real screens of a hidden copy of the window driven through a unit's steps (`grab()`), captioned, played in the app and saved as an animated GIF; made on the computer the first time (nothing bundled).
  - **The picture guide** (`core/guide.py`, `core/guide_pictures.py`): per kind (a soldier's front A-pose, a horse's side view, a gun's three-quarter view) what works, why, and good and bad pictures drawn by the app (original drawings, nothing bundled). The checks read gradient backgrounds (the border blended per row and column); a figure narrower than 0.38 of its height (arms down, a side view) is warned about.
  - **Check for updates** (`core/updates.py`): GitHub Releases' latest against this version; the repository is the settings' `update_repo` (blank: not set up yet, said so).
    - **Off for now (the user, 2026-10-28):** there is no GitHub repository yet. `updates.REPO` stays blank, so the button only says update checks aren't set up. To turn it on when the app is shared: create the repository, publish a release whose tag is the version (`v0.2.0`) with the zip and setup.exe attached, and set `REPO = "owner/name"` (or `update_repo` in the settings file).
- **The candidate pick** (`trellis.pick_score`): outline, less 0.01 per unit of colour distance (median Lab ΔE of the textured raw model from the outline's best side), less 0.05 per unit the worst stretch goes past vanilla's (a shape-only trial build on the game skeleton, about 40 s per candidate). The cataphract horse: seed 42 0.866 / 17.4 / 6.26 → 0.576, seed 43 0.859 / 16.9 / 6.89 → 0.542.
- **Archived:** the keyword/vanilla-parts builder (the describe Home, the Result screen, the Advanced editor) and the Track P unit screen, in `archive/src/gui_nation_wizard`.

- **In-game test 36 STAGED (2026-10-28)** (`build/test36`; the run sheet is `README.txt`, the app's render `expected_close.png`): HDA; tier 1 infantry the cataphract soldier on foot (run `cataphract`, build v002: its own body, the lance and kite in the on-foot convention, the plume; spear_shield animations), tier 2 vanilla infantry (at MIL 2), "T36" in every unit name; tier 1 artillery's crew is the same soldier. Staged from the soldier's own preview spec; validator 0 errors, 2 warnings (the shield's and plume's texture-quality notes). It closes the UNVERIFIED notes on the soldier on foot and its hand parts.

### Track A: Artillery from a picture (BUILDING, 2026-10-28; the user's go with Track L: the bombard `artillery_bombard.png`, CC BY 4.0)
**Built:** run kind `gun` (cut-out, TRELLIS.2 scored on outline and colour: a gun doesn't stretch, the build, finish, preview); `blender/jobs/fresh_gun.py` (the frame from above, the muzzle the higher end, the wheels the widest points; one scale so the wheels' radius is the wheels bone's height, the axle on it, the barrel to -x; rigid: wheels or Cannon per vertex; 1,500 triangles); `trellis.process_gun` (the base gun read at run time: bones, cannon_muzzle in world space (6.02, 2.07, 0), extent); `naming.fresh_gun_*`; loadout `guns`; `fresh_mesh._own_cannon` (the tier's cannon entity written in full from the one the artillery attached, states and muzzle event kept, the artillery's cannon_node pointed at it); rule `fresh_mesh` for guns (budget 1,500, the artillery attaches it); the gun's report (outline, the muzzle's distance from the flash, the wheels' share, size).
**How vanilla builds artillery** (read from the 1.37 install; nothing copied):
- **The unit entity** `<gfx>_ARTILLERY_<n>`: pdxmesh `artillery_base_mesh` (`artillery_base.mesh`: one hidden plane, 3 bones `root`, `infantry_node` at z -4, `cannon_node` at z +4); states `artillery_base_attack/idle/moving` (static); `attach = { name = "gunman" infantry_node = "<gfx>_INFANTRY_<n>" cannon_node = "<gfx>_ARTILLERY_<n>_cannon" }`. Tiers 2-4 clone tier 1 (`_generic`) with their own attach.
- **The gun** `<gfx>_ARTILLERY_<n>_cannon`: pdxmesh `artillery_mesh` (`interface/assets/weapons.gfx`: `artillery.mesh`, scale 1.1, ids `artillery_attack/idle/moving`); the attack state fires particle `artillery_cannon` at locator `cannon_muzzle`. **One gun for every culture and tier** (every `_cannon` clones `westerngfx_ARTILLERY_1_cannon`).
- **`artillery.mesh`:** 682 triangles, 566 vertices, PdxMeshStandard (no country colour), textures `siege_cannon_*`; 3 bones `Root` → `Cannon` → `wheels`, every vertex on one bone (rigid); locator `cannon_muzzle` on `Cannon`; extent x 5.86-11.79, y 0-2.41, z ±1.16. The animations move `Root`, `Cannon` (the recoil) and `wheels` (rolling while moving).
- **The crew** is the tier's infantry entity at `infantry_node`; it plays `artillery_attack` from its own pdxmesh. `western_generic_01_type1` (our soldier's base body) has it (`polearm_twohanded_attack_animation`), so **a soldier made in the app already crews a gun**: the loadout's soldier is the infantry entity. A crew dressed apart is the existing crew slot (`<TAG>_ARTILLERY_<n>_crew`).

**The plan (each step committed, tested and in the regression set):**
1. **Input:** a three-quarter picture of the gun alone (guide and check exist: `guide.GUIDE["artillery"]`); name, rights; the crew: one of your soldiers or the game's.
2. **Cut-out and TRELLIS.2** as now; the two candidates scored on outline, colour and the trial build's stretch.
3. **A new Blender job `fresh_gun`:** the long axis to vanilla's x, the muzzle end at `cannon_muzzle`'s side; one uniform scale to vanilla's length; the wheels found (round, low, either side: the largest near-circular pieces across the axle) and their axle put on the `wheels` bone's pivot and axis, so they roll; everything else on `Cannon`, rigid (one bone per vertex, like vanilla); the barrel's tip on vanilla's `cannon_muzzle` (so the locator, which is copied from the base byte for byte, fires at our muzzle); a closed solid, quadric collapse to about 1,200 triangles (budget: vanilla 682, a warning above 1,500); region UVs; colour and normal bakes as for horses.
4. **Files and names** (`naming.fresh_gun_*`): `.mesh` on `artillery.mesh`'s skeleton (`fresh_mesh.write_fresh`), its textures, a pdxmesh with `artillery_mesh`'s animation ids and scale; the tier's `_cannon` entity written in full (never `clone =`) with vanilla's states and its muzzle event, wearing it. **Rule `fresh_mesh`** extended to guns: the skeleton and locator byte for byte, the ids, the budget, the cannon entity wearing it.
5. **Preview:** the artillery at its tier against vanilla's next, turntable, quality report: the outline, the wheels' clearance while they turn (no wheel through the carriage), the recoil (nothing stretches: rigid), map-zoom light.
6. **Add to mod:** normal (the tag's artillery) or achievement-safe (re-dresses `<gfx>_ARTILLERY_<n>_cannon`, every culture's gun at that tier being the same).
7. **The app:** the Artillery card on: 1 the gun's picture, 2 name and rights, 3 the crew (pick a soldier or the game's) and the tier, 4 make it.

**The bombard (2026-10-28):** TRELLIS.2 from `artillery_bombard.png`; the fit's outline overlaps the raw model's by 89-91%; the wheels' radius to the base game's (scale 7.17), the width squeezed to the base game's (0.53: its track was 1.85 times it), a voxel remesh at 0.03 before the collapse (plain collapse stalled at 18,012 and shredded it; 0.045 tore the trail's planks), 2,400 triangles (1,500 tore the trail; budget 2,500), 15% on wheels; the colour bake matches only the brightness to the raw render (the full transfer greyed the wood and bronze). Its muzzle is 0.96 in front of the base game's cannon_muzzle point (the bombard's barrel is longer; the locator stays as it is). **In-game test 37 STAGED** (`build/test37`, `README.txt`): HDA tier 1 artillery, the bombard on its own cannon entity (the base game's states and muzzle event), crewed by the cataphract on foot; tier 2 the game's own; "T37" names; validator 0 errors, 2 warnings (the cataphract's shield and plume texture notes).

**Unknowns to test in game (one test mod, tier 1 on HDA):** that the muzzle flash fires at our locator; that the wheels roll on our pivot; that a gun with a different size and triangle count on vanilla's skeleton loads (it does for horses); that our soldier crews correctly. **Time:** about 15 minutes a run (most of it TRELLIS.2), build about 2 minutes.

### Track L: the part library (PLAN, 2026-10-28; the user's design change: parts made once, units assembled)
**The idea:** parts are made once from a picture and reused; units are put together from parts. My units becomes the **Library**.
- **Parts** (the library's three kinds), each a finished run of the flow as now: **horses** (side view), **soldiers** (front A-pose), **guns** (three-quarter; Track A). Only making a part needs the AI setup (BiRefNet, SAM 2, TRELLIS.2).
- **Units** are assemblies, not runs from a picture:
  - Infantry = a soldier (on foot: its stance's animations, its hand parts in the on-foot convention).
  - Cavalry = a horse + a soldier as rider (its seated parts; its legs spread for that horse's width: geometry only).
  - Artillery = a gun + a soldier as crew (no rebuild: the crew is the infantry entity on the same skeleton, playing `artillery_attack`; Track A).
  - One soldier serves as infantry, rider and crew.
- **Tiers:** a unit picks its parts per tier (1-4); a tier left empty is the game's own look for that tier (vanilla at the next tier, as the preview does now). E.g. tier 1 the bay horse, tier 3 the cataphract horse.
- **Assembly needs no AI:** the parts' built files (geometry, textures, parts) go into the test-mod spec (`loadout` `horses`/`soldiers`/`riders`/`guns` per tier); Blender only for the renders (gallery, turntable) and the deformation check. Minutes, not the 15-30 of a part. The old `cavalry` kind (the soldier rebuilt for its horse with SAM 2 and TRELLIS.2 parts) stays for its existing runs; new cavalry is an assembly.
- **Tags:** each horse has a style (`light`, `barded`, `steppe`, `arab`; more can be typed) and regions (free text, e.g. "Byzantine, Greek"). Soldiers and guns have regions only. Tags are the user's: set when the part is made, editable on its page; starter parts come tagged.
- **Suggestions:** picking a country (tag) or graphical culture suggests parts; any part can still be picked. The country's graphical culture and technology group are read from the install (`library.tags.tag_facts`; read only). The mapping is the app's own (`core/library.py:SUGGEST`; not EU4 syntax): westerngfx barded then light; easterngfx light then barded; muslimgfx arab then light; indiangfx arab; asiangfx steppe; a nomad technology group (`nomad_group`) steppe first; the rest light. A part whose regions name the country's culture or culture group comes first.
- **The starter library** ships with the app: the bay horse (`horse2_light_cavalry.png`; light, Western), the cataphract horse (barded, Byzantine), the cataphract soldier (Byzantine), and the gun once made (`artillery_bombard.png`). All from the user's own ChatGPT pictures (owned by the user; bundling allowed, 2026-10-28). **What a starter part holds:** our geometry (positions, UVs, normals, bone names and weights: our numbers, no vanilla bytes), our textures (PNG), its picture, its rights and tags, its quality report. **Never:** a `.mesh`, `.dds` or anything read from the install; the skeleton, locators and material are taken from the user's install when a unit is added to a mod (as now). Read-only in the app; "Make a copy" to edit one. A user without the AI setup builds units from these, changes colours and emblems (the finish edits: no AI), and adds them to a mod.
- **Plain words when the AI isn't set up:** New part says "Making a new part from a picture needs the AI setup (Setup, about 60 GB). You can build units from the parts already in your library without it." and links Setup; Build a unit always works.
- **Screens:**
  - **Library** (the top bar's third item; was My units): filters All · Horses · Soldiers · Guns · Units, a tag search; cards for parts (picture, name, kind, tags, "starter") and units (each tier's parts); "＋ New part" and "＋ Build a unit". A part's page: the turntable, the result sheet, the report, edit the look, its tags, "Use in a unit", Make a copy (starter), Delete (not starter). A unit's page: per tier its parts and turntable, Add to mod, Edit (back to the builder), Delete.
  - **Build a unit** (the top bar's New unit): 1 the type (infantry, cavalry, artillery); 2 the country or graphical culture (optional: it drives the suggestions and Add to mod's default); 3 the tiers: four columns, each with pickers for that type's parts (cavalry: horse and rider; artillery: gun and crew; infantry: soldier), "the game's own" for none, thumbnails, "suggested" marks; 4 name, then Build it.
  - **New part:** the guided picture flow (as New unit is now) for a soldier, horse or gun, with its tags on the About step.
- **Data:** parts stay runs (`<projects>/<name>/`, kind `horse`/`soldier`/`gun`, `request.json` gains `tags`); starter parts are read from the app's `library/starter/<name>/part.json` (relative paths). A unit is `<projects>/<name>/request.json` with kind `unit`: `{"type", "tiers": {"1": {"horse": ref, "soldier": ref, "gun": ref}}, "country", "gfx"}`, refs `"run:<name>"` or `"starter:<name>"`; its stages: `assemble` (the spec, staging, validation) and `preview` (gallery per tier, turntable, quality).
- **Order:** L1 SPEC (this). L2 the Library and Build a unit screens on `core/library.py` (shown to the user first). L3 assembly (kind `unit`, all three types, multi-tier specs). L4 the starter library (`library.pack`, the bay horse built from its run, a packaging check that a starter part holds no game file). L5 Track A, the gun from `artillery_bombard.png`, into the library. L6 in-game test 37: HDA, the gun crewed by the cataphract soldier, "T37" names.
- **The design pass (the user, 2026-10-28; shown before building further):** two designs in `gui/theme.py` (campaign: charcoal-brown and gold; admiralty: navy and bronze), each a dark grained ground, parchment cards, serif headings (the first installed of the design's list), one palette with separate status colours for the ground and the cards (every pair at least 4.5:1, tests/test_theme.py); one icon set drawn for the app (`gui/icons.py`); buttons: one primary per screen, the rest outlined, Delete red; cards with chips and a status dot; thumbnails lifted onto a lit backdrop (`core/thumbs.py`), a horse part shown alone. Top bar: Home · Build a unit · Make a part · Library · Setup · Help. Home: two choices, the example and the tour, the latest. Build a unit on one screen (compact tiers, a preview per tier, the name and Build it in a bar always in view; the name follows the type and country). Part pages: one plain line with its dot, the numbers and the full rights record behind Details; Add to mod on units only. Waiting for the user's choice of design.
- **Generic bases (the user, 2026-10-28):** the English soldier (`english_soldier.png`: kettle hat, mail, a white quilted jack with the red cross as the country-colour area, poleaxe; tags English, Western, generic), the English horse (`english_horse.png`: bay, white saddle cloth with a red cross) and the bombard: CC BY 4.0, in the starter library. **Found:** the country mask took the bay coat's reddish shades (`_colour.cloth_window` around the cloth's hue; the coat's hue is found but only kept out of the cloth-hue search): the bay horse's game version is grey. To fix before the English horse is built.
- **The user's choices (2026-10-28):** Admiralty is the default design (Campaign in Help → Design). The Library is a tile grid per type (Horses, Soldiers, Guns, Units): a big picture, the capitalised name, one line of chips ("+2"), a Starter badge, a status dot and Resume or View. Names shown capitalised everywhere (folders keep theirs). A part page: the buttons, then the game version and the picture side by side at one size, then Details. Builder: the game's own gun is a valid gun (your crew on it); a caption names what its picture shows. Home: How it works (Make parts → Build a unit → Add to mod).
- **L3 assembly (built):** `ref3d/assemble.py`, run kind `unit` (stages `assemble`, `preview`); cavalry seats the soldier with its seated hand parts (`trellis.to_foot` records `seated_geometry`; older builds matched by their triangles) and spreads its legs for the horse; infantry and artillery's crew are the tier's infantry on foot; a gun part is the tier's own cannon entity. Achievement-safe Add to mod takes every tier of a unit. A unit waits for its part runs (`flow.waiting_for`). Test: `l3_test_cavalry` (tier 1 the cataphract horse, tier 3 the bay horse, the cataphract riding both).
- **L4 the starter library (built; the user, 2026-10-28: raw TRELLIS.2 models + tags + rights, fitted on each PC on first use):** `library.pack` (raw.glb, the cut-out, the part crops cut from the picture (`crops_cache`: no SAM 2 needed), TRELLIS.2's part models (`parts_cache`), the picture, a raw render; nothing on the game's skeleton, no weights, never a game format), `library.fit` (a run of its own, the AI steps marked done, Blender only), `library.STARTER` packed from the author's finished runs when the installer is built (`pack_starters`; the folder is not in git). About 30-45 MB a part. Rights: CC BY 4.0, made by the app's author with ChatGPT.
- **Fixes found on the generic bases (2026-10-28; known fixes `shield_is_a_plate`, `blade_by_place`, `coat_not_cloth`; regression entries `english_soldier`, `horse2_mask` with rules that hold whatever the baseline says):** the English soldier had no shield, but his outstretched forearm was cut off as one (so he crouched in the spear-and-shield stance), his poleaxe lost its head, and his raised arm dragged the jack into a sheet (`swing_arm` now moves what joins the hand along the surface); the bay horse's coat was taken for its cloth. **AI steps wait for RAM** (`flow.NEED_RAM`: TRELLIS.2 14 GB, the build 8, the cut-out 4) and the progress names the largest programs to close (the English soldier's TRELLIS.2 run was killed at 3.2 GB free).

### Track V: soldiers that move and read like the game's (the user, after T38, 2026-10-29)
**Found in T38:** the English soldier looked flattened and squished in game, the bill arm bent badly, the cataphract's shield wasn't clearly on his arm. **Cause** (measured; overlays of the base body, TRELLIS.2's raw model and ours, front and side): not volume (his depth/width 0.52, the raw model's 0.47, vanilla's 0.49: one front picture is not the limit here) but the fit: one scale and a centring left the skeleton's joints outside the body (the cataphract's spine at 0.70-0.77 of his depth from the front, vanilla's 0.47-0.50, his neck bone at his back's surface; the English soldier's knees and ankles 0.9-1.7 off his legs); and, standing, the rider's cloak rule gave the backs of the legs and seat (14% of the English soldier) the spine's weights. Joints turning outside a limb swing it on a lever: the fold and squash.
- **V1 the joint fit** (`fresh_rider.joint_fit`, both fits): the torso shifted in depth by height so each spine bone sits where vanilla's body has it (from the front surface, the depth clamped to 0.8-1.2 of vanilla's: a cloak or plume behind isn't the body); each leg sheared under the thigh onto vanilla's stance at the knee and ankle (the wide stance); weapons moved whole with their grips. Measured density-independently (strip extremes; low meshes sampled over their triangles). Standing: the cloak rule only above the hips; weights smoothed 12 times as before (unsmoothed squashed less overall but folded triangles at hard weight borders: 60-102 flips, stretch 13-16; with the joint fit and 12 passes the English soldier: 18 / 25 / 25 flips, stretch 3.3-6.6, 20.5% of his surface squashed in attack against T38.s 25.7% and vanilla.s 10.5%). Rule: the quality report's **Joints inside the body** and the regression's `joints_spine` (at most 0.4) and `joints_legs` (0.6). After: English 0.17 / 0.13, cataphract 0.16 / 0.24.
- **V2 the animation sets** (`core.stances`, read from the install at run time): rifleman 62 bodies (a gun in Left_hand_node), polearm_twohanded 19 (western_halbard, asian_spear_01), polearm_onehanded 9, spear_shield 8 (a spear and a shield), musketeer_02 and eastern_generic_02_special (a musket); no sword set. A shield -> spear_shield, a long weapon alone -> polearm_twohanded (as `loadout`). **Weapons as the game holds them:** on foot a weapon is slid through the hand to the base game's grip for its stance (the weapon its base body's units hold: western_halbard 0.44 of its length from the butt, muslim_spear 0.47) and its shaft centred on the hand (the English bill was gripped at 0.63, its head 6.6 over the hand; the halberd's 13.9).
- **V3 the animation review** (`ref3d.review`): idle, walk and attack (the rest pose and five poses, front and side) next to a base-game unit playing the same set (`stances.example_unit`: westerngfx_INFANTRY_1 for polearm_twohanded, DLH_INFANTRY_1 for spear_shield), flags against it: bad bends (surface squashed under 0.8 of its rest area, 8 points over the game's), clipping (a hand part inside the body, 5 points over), floating (a hand part's surface farther from the hand node, 0.6 over). One Blender run per state (`render_unit` shots, `Session.gallery` shots and entities). In the soldier's preview (`<preview>/review/review_sheet.png`), the quality report (**In its animations**: good with no flags, ok up to 6) and the regression (`review_flags`, `review_bends`, `review_clipping`, `review_floating`). T38's English soldier: 13 flags (the bill through his body walking, 27% squashed attacking against the game's 12%).
- **V4 readability** (the user: the base game's units read at a distance because every part is distinct):
  - **The painted pass** (`blender/jobs/_paint.py`, after every body's bake: soldiers, horses, guns, generated parts): materials clustered in Lab and cleaned, flattened within each, contrast per material (1.3), creases from the normal map's curvature, dark borders only between distinct materials (CIE76 25 apart: the lit and shaded halves of the white jack are two clusters, no border), light from above (the object-space normal), a light sharpen. **Material regions:** from the model's own baked colours in texture space, not SAM 2 on the picture: SAM 2 sees only the front of the picture and its regions would need projecting onto the model (the back and sides guessed); the bake already carries the picture's colours over the whole model.
  - **The triangle budget** (`fresh_rider.importance`): the Decimate group (inverted) on the limbs (0.7) and material borders (0.4): the English soldier's limbs keep 74% of his triangles (67% before; borders at 1.0 stalled the collapse).
  - **Proportions** (`stylise`): head and hands up to at least vanilla's (at most 1.25 and 1.3; the shoulders' rule is off: their width at shoulder height follows how the arms hang, and it widened the cataphract's cloak 15% into a heavy top). The plume cut keeps what is nearer the helmet's steel (the cataphract's dome went with it: his head 1.7 over the neck against vanilla's 2.75, his height 15.6).
  - **Outline gaps** measured (`gaps`: legs at the knees, forearms at the wrists, against vanilla's 0.91 and 1.07-1.1); an empty hand swung to vanilla's hand bone like a held one (the English soldier's free arm hung 20 degrees low: 0.19 from his side -> 2.46). **Open:** the cloth between a weapon arm and the torso (0.03-0.08 at the wrist: the web under the bill arm) is measured, not yet cut.
- **Baselines:** the regression baselines for the soldiers are not updated until the user approves them in game (the user, 2026-10-29).
- **Found on the way:** mesh parts were wound by `recalc_face_normals` in `build_parts` (split at their UV seams, each patch guessed on its own: 54 of the cataphract lance's 200 triangles inside out, the shaft shredded by back-face culling; T38's had 1): our own geometry now keeps its winding, and every collapse ends with faces outward. The lineup renders an in-game test from its staged mod (the run folders are rebuilt in place: T38's spec now points at today's files).
- **Results (final builds; `build/review_t39`: the review sheets and the lineups, idle, walk and attack, T38 as shipped next to now and the game's native and western infantry):** joints good on both (English 0.16 / 0.12, cataphract 0.15 / 0.26); the animation review 8 flags (English: T38's 13) and 6 (cataphract); weapons held at the game's grip (in walk both now carry them like the western halberdier; T38's English bill trailed head down). **Folds** under their own stance (the same check on T38's builds): English 13 / 25 / 36 (T38 18 / 23 / 33), cataphract 17 / 50 / 58 (T38 13 / 26 / 55): **the cataphract's walk folds doubled (open)**; the game's body 8 / 4 / 14 and 8 / 5 / 23. Squash in attack (share of the surface under 0.8 of its area): English 20.5% (T38 25.7%; the game's 10.5%).
- **Regression run (20261029-113341):** horses unchanged by the painted pass (no worse measure); the rider better (flips 32 / 33 -> 15 / 18, feet 0.46 -> 0.81) but its shield now passes as TRELLIS.2's own (outline 0.889 -> 0.847) and its top colour moved (dE 5.4 -> 10.5: the painted pass); the soldiers now measured under their own stance (before: under the rider's, the build's own check), against baselines measured seated: not comparable until the baselines are refreshed after the user's in-game approval. Colour by region moved by the painted pass (English bottom 1.1 -> 5.7, cataphract top 2.9 -> 8.7; the limit 15).
- **Open:** the cataphract's walk folds; the cloth between a weapon arm and the torso (the English bill arm's web: 0.03 at the wrist); squash about twice the game's in walk and attack; the soldiers' overall value at map zoom (the English soldier 40% brighter than the game's: his white jack, not his texture's lightness: median 102 against the game body's 93).
- **Superseded for soldiers by Track W** (the user, after T39's review sheets). T39 not staged.

### Track W: a base body, the outfit over it (PLAN, the user, 2026-10-29; shown before building)
**Why:** the T39 review sheets. TRELLIS.2 gives one fused shape: body, cloak, armour and arms glued together. Whatever joint fit and weights we give it, the skeleton bends a lump: it folds where the joints turn, there's no outline of a person, and the cloth under an arm webs it to the torso. Tuning the fit won't fix this (Track V: joints inside the body, and still about twice the game's squash, the cataphract's walk folds doubled). The horse's lesson applies: a normal body, cloak it, shrink it.

**Found first and fixed (W0, done 2026-10-29):** the lance and the bill bent in walk and attack. They were already rigid (every vertex on the hand node; their shape is identical in every frame), so the bend was TRELLIS.2's own: the cataphract's lance waves about one and a half times along its length, 2.3% of it off the line; the bill 0.35%. `blender/jobs/_rigid.py`: the shaft straightened when it is split off (`fresh_rider`; 2.3% -> 0.09%, the bill 0.35% -> 0.07%), its head riding on it; the review flags any part that changes shape between poses ("bent: ... a part must be rigid"); the regression's `weapon_bow` (at most 0.4% of the length) and `part_rigid` (at most 0.2%), expected on the English soldier, the cataphract on foot and the rider.

**The layers** (every soldier: the same base body, only the outfit changes):
1. **The base body (B):** one plain human, ours, made once (from an A-pose picture of a plain person in tight clothing, through TRELLIS.2), rigged and tuned until it moves like the game's body. It ships in the starter library as our geometry (like the parts: no game bytes; the skeleton read from the install).
2. **The outfit (O):** the soldier's own shape and colours from his TRELLIS.2 model, moved onto the base body: the base body's vertices pushed out to his surface (the base body's topology, weights and UVs kept; his colours baked onto its UVs).
3. **Loose cloth (C):** cloaks, skirts, tassets: separate sheets in the same mesh with simple weights, cut where they would fold.
4. **Rigid parts (R):** weapons, shields, plumes, brimmed helmets: every vertex on one node (hand, head; the belt's on the hip). W0's checks hold them.

**B. The base body**
- **The picture (the user's, from ChatGPT; prompt below):** front view, full body, A-pose as the game's rest pose: arms 35 degrees below horizontal, a little forward; feet under the hips. A plain person in tight grey clothing, a plain close cap, simple shoes; the hands open, fingers together; a plain light background, even light.
- **Shape from TRELLIS.2, topology our own:** not TRELLIS.2's triangles collapsed (they have no edge loops where joints turn: the fold). A template built on the game's bones (read from the install at run time): each limb a tube of rings along its bones, the torso a ring stack along the spine, the neck and head, the hands as mittens and the feet as boots. Extra rings round each joint (elbow, knee, shoulder, hip, wrist, ankle: three each, as the game's body has), so bends happen on loops. Each ring's radius at each angle is measured from TRELLIS.2's body: rays cast out from the bone where the picture's limb is, then carried into the game's rest pose. The arms and legs are their own tubes, so there's nothing to web between arm and torso or between the legs. Shoulders and hips are bridged ring to ring. Budget about 2,000 triangles (the game's body 2,883), leaving room for the cloth layers inside our 2,950.
- **Weights by rule, not transfer:** along each chain by the ring's place (a joint's middle ring half and half, the rings either side 75/25, like the game's), the torso rings blended along the spine, the shoulder bridge three ways (top_spine, shoulder, arm). Smoothed only along the rings.
- **UVs once:** an island per limb, the torso front and back, the head; the outfit's colours bake onto these, so every soldier's texture has the same layout.
- **Tuned until it moves like the game's body (the gate):** under polearm_twohanded, spear_shield, rifleman and the rider's animations, against `western_generic_01_type1` in the same frames: folds (`check_mesh_deform` flips) at most the game's plus 25% (at least 3); squash (the review's bends) at most the game's plus 3 points per state; joints inside the body (`joints_spine` and `joints_legs` at most 0.2); the arm-torso and leg gaps at least the game's. If it misses, I tune the rings and weights, not the soldier. The review sheet of the base body alone (ours against the game's, as the soldiers' now) shows it.

**O. The outfit**
- The soldier's TRELLIS.2 model is split (weapons, shield, plume: R) and fitted as now (scale, joint fit, the stance). Then each base-body vertex is moved along its normal, out or in, to the soldier's surface, at most `push` (about 0.6; the armour and padding). A hit only counts if its nearest bone is in the vertex's own chain: an arm vertex never lands on the torso's cloth. That keeps limbs clear, so no web.
- Whatever of his surface lies farther out than `push` is not outfit. Above the neck it's a brimmed helmet (R, rigid on head: the English kettle hat's brim). Hanging below the hips or behind the back it's loose cloth (C). Anything else is dropped and recorded.
- "Shrink it" (as the horse): the pushed shape is pulled back towards the base body by `shrink` (about 0.15 of its push), so it stays slim and joints keep their volume. The outfit's thickness is kept below `push` at the joints' rings (about 0.3), so elbows and knees don't bulge.
- **Colours:** baked from his TRELLIS.2 model onto the base body's UVs (the bake as now, the painted pass after). Measured against his raw model by region (colour dE) and outline (the compare sheet).

**C. Loose cloth**
- **Skirt or tassets:** a ring of panels from the hips down to his hem (measured from his model), split front, back and at each side, so a leg can swing between panels. Each panel's top row is on `hip`; down the panel it blends to the nearer thigh, by at most half, so it follows the leg without stretching. Shaped by projecting it onto his model's skirt.
- **Cloak:** a sheet from the shoulders, its shape projected onto his cloak, on `top_spine` and `mid_spine` only (no leg weights: the T38 lesson). Its hem is kept above the knees' swing and split up the middle when it hangs below the hips.
- Each sheet is one-sided with its back face drawn (as the game's banners), a piece of the body mesh: one mesh, one texture. No new EU4 syntax.
- The review measures each layer's squash and clipping (a leg through the skirt) separately.

**R. Rigid parts:** as W0. A brimmed helmet becomes a head part (`mesh_head`, as the cataphract's plume), every vertex on `head`.

**Seated:** the same outfit body rides (the base body is tuned under the rider's animations too); the seated fit then needs only the legs spread for the horse (`spread_legs`) and the skirt or cloak lifted over the saddle. One build per soldier instead of two (`finished` and `finished_seated`).

**Where it goes:**
- `blender/jobs/base_body.py`: builds the base body from TRELLIS.2's model and the game's bones (B). A new starter part kind `body` (`library`).
- `blender/jobs/fresh_soldier.py`: replaces the standing and seated fits in `fresh_rider` for soldiers. Reuses `split`, `place`, `joint_fit` and `_rigid`, plus the outfit, cloth and helmet steps. `trellis.process_soldier` calls it.
- `ref3d.review`: the layers' measures. `ref3d.quality`: **Moves like the game's body** (the base body's gate, read from its starter record). `ref3d.regress`: the base body as an entry with the gate as rules whatever the baseline says; the English soldier and the cataphract rebuilt.
- Validator: rule `fresh_mesh` checks a soldier's body has the base body's vertex count for its outfit part (the layers counted apart), every weight row sums to one, and every rigid part sits on one node.

**Order (each step committed; the regression after each):**
- W0 the rigid parts: done.
- W1 the base body from the user's picture, until it passes the gate; its review sheet.
- W2 the English soldier's outfit and helmet (no loose cloth: his jack ends at the hips), then his review sheet and a lineup next to T38 and the game's infantry. **Shown to the user before touching the cataphract.**
- W3 the cataphract: the outfit, the cloak and skirt layers, the plume and shield as now. Review sheet.
- W4 the seated fit from the same build; the rider and the cavalry units rebuilt; the regression's soldier baselines refreshed only after the user approves them in game.
- W5 T39: both soldiers, the lineups and the review sheets.

**Risks:**
- The bridges at the shoulders and hips are where the game's body folds too. If our bridge can't reach the gate, the fallback is the arm tube overlapping into the torso (closed shells, a common low-poly trick; at map zoom it doesn't show).
- A bulky outfit (a padded jack, plate) pushed to `push` may still squash at the elbows. That's why `shrink` and the joint-ring cap exist; both are measured, not guessed.
- A picture that isn't in the rest pose (arms down, legs apart) is measured from where its limbs actually are. It's carried into the rest pose by rotating each ring about its joint, so a different A-pose still works. A hand in a fist or holding something doesn't.

- **The emblem-crispness check (the user, 2026-10-25).** A shield passes only if its emblem, traced from its own front view, overlaps the crop's emblem by at least 0.5 (`part_gen.emblem_match`, the best over small shifts and scales).
  - The cataphract's TRELLIS.2 shield scored 0.41 and is rejected. The crop-traced kite scores 0.53 and is what the pipeline now builds; it looks like test 35's kite (sheet `build/shield_compare_traced.png`).
  - Its colour distance (24.7) is over 18, so it shows as flagged in the report.
  - The approved cataphract keeps test 35's own kite. The baseline's shield measures were moved to the traced kite.
